Normal crossings singularities for symplectic topology: structures
Mohammad F. Tehrani, Mark McLean, Aleksey Zinger

Abstract
Our previous papers introduce topological notions of normal crossings symplectic divisor and variety, show that they are equivalent, in a suitable sense, to the corresponding geometric notions, and establish a topological smoothability criterion for normal crossings symplectic varieties. The present paper constructs a blowup, a complex line bundle, and a logarithmic tangent bundle naturally associated with a normal crossings symplectic divisor and determines the Chern class of the last bundle. These structures have applications in constructions and analysis of various moduli spaces. As a corollary of the Chern class formula for the logarithmic tangent bundle, we refine Aluffi’s formula for the Chern class of the tangent bundle of the blowup at a complete intersection to account for the torsion and extend it to the blowup at the deepest stratum of an arbitrary normal crossings divisor.



1
Introduction

Divisors, i.e. subvarieties of codimension 1 over the ground field,
and related structures, are among the central objects of study in algebraic geometry.
They appear in the study of curves (as dual objects),
singularities (particularly in the Minimal Model Program),
and semistable degenerations of smooth varieties (as the singular locus).
The complex line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
corresponding to a Cartier divisor
V\!\subset\!X
and
the log tangent bundle
TX(-\log V)
(or dually the sheaf of log 1-forms
\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log V)
)
corresponding to a normal crossings (or NC) divisor
are among such useful and well-studied structures.
They play important roles in the relative/log Gromov-Witten theories of Li
[
22
,
23
]
,
Gross-Siebert
[
21
]
, and Abramovich-Chen
[
1
,
7
]
.
Divisors also play an important role in symplectic topology,
including as representatives of the Poincare duals of symplectic forms
[
10
]
,
in symplectic sum constructions
[
20
,
24
]
,
in relative Gromov-Witten theory and symplectic sum formulas

[
35
,
19
,
30
,
31
,
11
]
,
in affine symplectic geometry
[
27
,
28
]
,
in homological mirror symmetry
[
3
,
33
]
,
and in relative Fukaya category
[
8
,
9
]
.

A
smooth
symplectic divisor is simply a symplectic submanifold of real codimension 2.
Applications involving a smooth symplectic divisor
V\!\subset X\!
typically rely on
the Symplectic Neighborhood Theorem
[
25
, Theorem 3.4.10]
.
It provides an identification
\Psi
(which we call a
symplectic regularization
)
of a neighborhood of
V
in
X
with a neighborhood
of
V
in its normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
.
Such an identification can then be used to construct an auxiliary
V
-compatible
“geometric” data/structure, such as a
V
-compatible almost complex structure
J

or a complex line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
over
X
with first Chern class
[V]_{X}
.
One then shows that an invariant defined using
J
or the deformation equivalence class
of an object constructed using
\Psi
depends only on
(X,V,\omega)
.
This approach is relatively straightforward to carry out in the case of smooth divisors.

Singular symplectic divisors/varieties and structures associated with them are
generally hard to define and work with because there is no direct analogue of
the Symplectic Neighborhood or Darboux Theorem in this setting.
Following an alternative approach,

[
14
,
15
]
introduce topological notions of NC symplectic divisor
and variety and geometric notions of regularization for NC symplectic divisors and varieties.
The latter is basically a “nice” neighborhood identification of the divisor/singular locus,
analogous to that provided by the Symplectic Neighborhood Theorem in the smooth case.
Every NC symplectic divisor/variety is deformation equivalent to one admitting a regularization.
For this reason, in our approach, we work with the entire deformation equivalence classes of
NC symplectic divisors/varieties, as opposed to a fixed NC symplectic divisor/variety;
see the end of Section
4.2
.

As stated in
[
13
]
, a regularization of an NC symplectic variety
V\!\!\subset\!X

can be used to construct an associated complex line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)

and a log tangent bundle
TX(-\log V)
.
The present paper carries out these constructions in detail.
While the constructions of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
and
TX(-\log V)

involve the auxiliary data of regularizations and other choices,
their deformation equivalence classes
depend on the deformation equivalence class of the symplectic structure only.

We denote the smooth locus of an NC symplectic divisor
V\!\subset\!X
by
V^{*}
.
For
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, let
V^{(r)}\!\subset\!V
be
r
-fold locus of
V
,
i.e. the locus that locally is the intersection of at least
r
branches of
V
;
see (
4.1
).
For example,
V^{(0)}\!=\!X
,
V^{(1)}\!=\!V
,
V^{(2)}
is the singular locus of
V
,
and
V^{*}\!=\!V^{(1)}\!-\!V^{(2)}
.
The subspace
V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r+1)}
is a smooth submanifold of
X
of codimension
2r
.
We denote its inclusion into
X
by
\iota_{V^{(r)}-V^{(r+1)}}
.
If
X
is compact and of (real) dimension
2n
,
\iota_{V^{(r)}-V^{(r+1)}}

is a pseudocycle in
X
of dimension
2(n\!-\!r)
; see
[
37
]
.
Thus,
V^{*}
and
V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r+1)}
determine homology classes

[V]_{X}\in H_{2n-2}(X;\mathbb{Z})\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\big[V^{(r)}\big]_{X}\in H_{2(n-r)}(X;\mathbb{Z}),

respectively.

Proposition 1.1
.

Let
(X,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold and
V\!\subset\!X
be an NC symplectic divisor.

(1)

An
\omega
-regularization
\mathcal{R}
for
V\!\subset\!X

determines a complex line bundle
(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}})

over
X
with a smooth section
s_{\mathcal{R}}
so that
s_{\mathcal{R}}^{-1}(0)\!=\!V
and

\textnormal{D}s_{\mathcal{R}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}(V^{*})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V)\big|_{V^{*}}

is an orientation-preserving isomorphism along the smooth locus
V^{*}
of
V
.

(2)

The deformation equivalence class
(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{i})

of
(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}})
depends only on the deformation equivalence class of
(X,V,\omega)
.

(3)

If
V^{\prime}\!\subset\!X
is another NC symplectic divisor so that

V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}\!\subset\!X
is also an NC symplectic divisor
and
V\!\cap\!V^{\prime}
contains no open subspace of
V
, then

\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}\big)\approx\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{i}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}\big).

(1.1)

The complex line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
appears in the smoothability criterion for
SC symplectic varieties in
[
16
]
and for general NC symplectic varieties
in
[
17
]
.
For a simple (normal) crossings (or SC) symplectic divisor
V\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S}\!V_{i}

as in Definition
3.1
,
(
1.1
) gives

\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\cong\bigotimes_{i\in S}\mathcal{O}_{X}(V_{i})\longrightarrow X.

If
X
is compact and of (real) dimension
2n
, the stated properties of
s_{\mathcal{R}}
imply that

c_{1}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\!\big)=\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V]_{X}\big)\in H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z}).

(1.2)

If
X
is not compact, this identity holds with
[V]_{X}
denoting the element
of the Borel-Moore homology of
X
determined by
V^{*}
; see
[
4
]
.

Theorem 1.2
.

Let
(X,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold and
V\!\subset\!X
be an NC symplectic divisor.

(1)

An
\omega
-regularization
\mathcal{R}
for
V\!\subset\!X
determines a vector bundle
T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)

over
X
with a smooth vector bundle homomorphism

\iota_{\mathcal{R}}\!:T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\longrightarrow TX

(1.3)

so that for every
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}

\displaystyle T\big(V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r+1)}\big)\subset T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\big|_{V^{(r)}-V^{(r+1)}},\quad\iota_{\mathcal{R}}|_{T(V^{(r)}-V^{(r+1)})}=\textnormal{d}\iota_{V^{(r)}-V^{(r+1)}},

\displaystyle\hbox{and}\qquad\iota_{\mathcal{R}}\big(T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\big|_{V^{(r)}-V^{(r+1)}}\big)=T(V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r+1)}).

(2)

An
\mathcal{R}
-compatible almost complex structure
J
on
X
determines a complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R},J}

on the vector bundle
T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)
so that the bundle homomorphism (
1.3
) is
\mathbb{C}
-linear.

(3)

The deformation equivalence class
(TX(-\log V),\mathfrak{i})
of
(T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R},J})

depends only on the deformation equivalence class of
(X,V,\omega)
.

(4)

If
V^{\prime}\!\subset\!X
is a smooth submanifold so that

V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}\!\subset\!X
is also an NC symplectic divisor and

V\!\cap\!V^{\prime}
contains no open subspace of
V
, then

\big(TX(-\log(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})\!),\mathfrak{i}\big)\!\oplus\!\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}\big)\approx\big(TX(-\log V),\mathfrak{i}\big)\!\oplus\!(X\!\times\!\mathbb{C}).

(1.4)

(5)

We have

c\big(TX(-\!\log V),\mathfrak{i}\big)=\frac{c(TX,\omega)}{1\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(1)}]_{X})\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(2)}]_{X})\!+\!\ldots}\in H^{*}(X;\mathbb{Q}).

(1.5)

The above equality holds in
H^{*}(X;\mathbb{Z})
if
V\!\subset\!X
is an SC divisor.

As shown in
[
12
]
,
the complex vector bundle
(TX(-\log V),\mathfrak{i})
plays the same role
in the deformation-obstruction theory of pseudoholomorphic curves relative to
an NC symplectic divisor as the complex vector bundle
(TX,J)

in the standard deformation-obstruction theory of pseudoholomorphic curves;
see
[
26
, Chapter 3]
, for example.
This provides a symplectic topology perspective on the constructions of log stable maps
in
[
30
,
21
,
1
]
.
In
[
18
]
, the vector bundle
TX(-\log V)
is used to define Seiberg-Witten invariants
of a closed oriented 4-manifold
X
relative to a smooth oriented Riemann surface
V
.
This perspective on the standard constructions of relative Seiberg-Witten invariants
reveals additional structures.

For an SC symplectic divisor
V\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S}\!V_{i}
as in Definition
3.1
,
(
1.4
) gives

\big(TX(-\log V),\mathfrak{i}\big)\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in S}\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V_{i}),\mathfrak{i}\big)\approx(TX,J)\!\oplus\!(X\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{S},\mathfrak{i}).

(1.6)

This immediately implies that

\begin{split}c\big(TX(-\!\log V)\!\big)&=\frac{c(TX,\omega)}{\prod_{i\in S}\big(1\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V_{i}]_{X})\big)}\\
&=\frac{c(TX,\omega)}{1\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(1)}]_{X})\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(2)}]_{X})\!+\!\ldots}\in H^{*}(X;\mathbb{Z}).\end{split}

(1.7)

The direct sum vector bundle on the left-hand side of (
1.6
) does not even
exist as a vector bundle in the general NC case;
see the example in the second half of Section
7
.
In Section
6.4
, we instead establish the de Rham cohomology analogue of (
1.5
)
by expressing the Chern classes on the two sides of (
1.5
)
in terms of the curvatures of connections in the vector bundles
TX
and
TX(-\!\log V)
.
We construct a
2k
-form
\tau_{k}
on
X
, supported in a neighborhood of
V^{(k)}
and
representing
\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(k)}]_{X})
in
H^{2k}_{\textnormal{deR}}(X)
, and
\mathbb{C}
-linear connections
\nabla
in
TX

and
\nabla^{\prime}
in
TX(-\log V)
so that the curvature of
\nabla
is the correct combination of
the curvature of
\nabla^{\prime}
and
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{r}
to yield (
1.5
);
see (
6.38
), Lemma
6.10
, and Proposition
6.11
.
Our proofs of (
1.4
) and (
1.5
) are carried out in the almost complex category.

When
V\!\subset\!X
is either an NC complex divisor in a complex manifold
or an NC almost complex divisor in an almost complex manifold compatible with a regularization for
V
,
in the sense defined in Section
4.2
,
we can pass to the blowup
\widetilde{X}
of
X
along the deepest stratum
V^{(r)}
of
V

and to the proper transform
\overline{V}
of
V
.
We can then compare the log tangent bundles for
(X,V)
and
(\widetilde{X},\overline{V})

and their Chern classes via (
1.4
), (
1.5
), and
Lemma
1.3
below.
This immediately yields Corollary
1.4
below,
except for the refinement in the vanishing torsion case.
This corollary refines
[
2
, Lemma 1.3]
in the SC case
and extends it to the general NC case.

Lemma 1.3
.

Let
(X,J)
be an almost complex manifold,
V\!\subset\!X
be an NC almost complex divisor
with a regularization
\mathcal{R}
, and
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
be such that
V^{(r+1)}\!=\!\emptyset
.
If
\pi\!:(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{J})\longrightarrow\!(X,J)
is the blowup of
(X,J)
along
V^{(r)}

determined by
\mathcal{R}
,
\mathbb{E}
is the exceptional divisor,
and
\overline{V}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}
is the proper transform of
X
, then

\widetilde{V}\!\equiv\!\overline{V}\!\cup\!\mathbb{E}
is an NC almost complex divisor in
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{J})
.
In this case,
there are a regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
of
\widetilde{V}
in
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{J})

and an isomorphism

\textnormal{d}^{\log}_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\pi\!:T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\widetilde{X}(-\log\widetilde{V})\xrightarrow{{~\cong~}}\pi^{*}\big(T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\!\big)

(1.8)

so that the diagram

\textstyle{T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\widetilde{X}(-\log\widetilde{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textnormal{d}_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}^{\log}\pi}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}}
\textstyle{T\widetilde{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textnormal{d}\pi}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}(T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\!)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{*}\iota_{\mathcal{R}}}
\textstyle{\pi^{*}TX}

commutes.
The first and third claims above also hold in the category of
complex manifolds with NC divisors.

Corollary 1.4
.

With the assumptions as in Lemma
1.3
,

\begin{split}&\frac{c(T\widetilde{X})}{(1\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}([\overline{V}^{(1)}]_{\widetilde{X}})\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}([\overline{V}^{(2)}]_{\widetilde{X}})\!+\!\ldots)(1\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}([\mathbb{E}]_{\widetilde{X}}))}\\
&\hskip 108.405pt=\pi^{*}\bigg(\frac{c(TX)}{1\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(1)}]_{X})\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(2)}]_{X})\!+\!\ldots}\bigg)\in H^{*}(\widetilde{X};\mathbb{Q})\,.\end{split}

(1.9)

The above equality holds in
H^{*}(\widetilde{X};\mathbb{Z})
if
V\!\subset\!X
is an SC divisor
or the torsion in
H_{*}(\mathbb{E};\mathbb{Z})
lies in the kernel of the homomorphism
\widetilde{\iota}_{*}

induced by the inclusion
\mathbb{E}\!\longrightarrow\!\widetilde{X}
.

The statement of Lemma
1.3
in the complex category is well-known;
its proof is recalled at the end of Section
2.1
.
We establish this lemma in the almost complex category with regularizations in
Sections
5.2
-
5.4
.
The regularizations are used to construct the bundles
TX(-\log V)
and

T\widetilde{X}(\!-\log(\overline{V}\!\cup\!\mathbb{E})\!)
and the blowup
\widetilde{X}
.

We can also pass to
a
blowup
\widetilde{X}
of
X
along the deepest stratum
V^{(r)}
of
V

and the proper transform
\overline{V}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}
of
V

if
V\!\subset\!X
is an NC symplectic divisor
with
V^{(r)}
admitting a tubular symplectic neighborhood
that contains the disk subbundle of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
of a fixed radius; see Section
5.5
.
This is automatically the case if
V^{(r)}
is the compact.
If so, each deformation equivalence class
[\omega]
of symplectic forms on
(X,V)

determines a deformation equivalence class
[\widetilde{\omega}]

of symplectic forms on the blowup
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{V})
of
(X,V)
along
V^{(r)}
,
a homotopy class of blowdown maps

\pi\!:(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{V})\longrightarrow(X,V),

and a homotopy class of isomorphisms

\textnormal{d}^{\log}\pi\!:T\widetilde{X}(-\log\widetilde{V})\xrightarrow{{~\cong~}}\pi^{*}\big(TX(-\log V)\!\big)

(1.10)

between the log tangent bundles associated with
[\omega]
and
[\widetilde{\omega}]
.
If
V^{(r)}
is not compact,
the existence of a tubular symplectic neighborhood that contains the disk subbundle of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}

of a fixed radius is unclear, even after deforming the symplectic form.
We suspect that the answers to the following closely related questions are negative in general;
the affirmative answer to Question
1.6
would imply
the affirmative answer to Question
1.5
.

Question 1.5
.

Let
(V,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold,
\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!V
be a direct sum of Hermitian line
bundles
(L_{i},\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})
determining fiberwise symplectic forms
\Omega_{i}
on
L_{i}
,
and
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}
be a neighborhood of
V
.
Is there a deformation of
\omega
through symplectic forms and of
(\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})
through
Hermitian structures so that the induced 2-form
\widetilde{\omega}
as in (
3.3
)
on the total space of
\mathcal{N}
is symplectic on the unit ball subbundle of
\mathcal{N}
,
with respect to the deformed metric, and this subbundle is contained in
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
?

Question 1.6
.

Let
(V,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold,
J
be an
\omega
-compatible almost complex structure,
and
C\!:V\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a smooth function.
Are there a symplectic form
\omega^{\prime}
on
V
deformation equivalent to
\omega

and an
\omega^{\prime}
–compatible almost complex structure
J^{\prime}
so that

\omega^{\prime}(v,J^{\prime}v)\geq C(x)\omega(v,Jv)\qquad\forall~x\!\in\!V,~v\!\in\!T_{x}V.

We review the complex geometric constructions of the complex line bundle
(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{i})
and
the complex vector bundle
(TX(-\log V),\mathfrak{i})
associated with an NC divisor
V

in a complex manifold
V
and some of their properties in Section
2.1
.
As a warmup to the general case, we construct these bundles for a smooth symplectic divisor
in Section
2.2
.
For the reader’s convenience,
Sections
3.1
and
3.2
recall the notions of SC symplectic divisor and
regularization, respectively, introduced in
[
14
]
.
Section
3.3
contains the constructions of the vector bundles
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)

and
TX(-\log V)
for an SC symplectic divisor
V\!\subset\!X
and establishes
Proposition
1.1
and the first three statements of Theorem
1.2

in this setting.
Section
3.4
establishes Theorem
1.2
(4)

for an SC symplectic divisor
V
.
The constructions and proofs in the SC case illustrate the arguments
in the general NC case, which are more notionally involved.
Sections
4.1
-
4.3
and
6.1
-
6.3

are the analogues of Sections
3.1
-
3.3
in
the local and global perspectives, respectively, on the NC symplectic divisors introduced
in
[
15
]
.
Section
4.3
also shows why the proof of Theorem
1.2
(4)

for SC symplectic divisors in Section
3.4
immediately extends to
the general NC case.
Sections
5
and
6.4
establish
Lemma
1.3
and Theorem
1.2
(5)
, respectively.
In Section
7
, we establish the remaining statement of
Corollary
1.4
and show
that (
1.5
) and (
1.9
) do not need
to hold with
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients for arbitrary NC divisors.

2
Standard settings

2.1
NC complex divisors

Let
X
be a complex manifold of (complex) dimension
n

with structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{X}
(the sheaf of local holomorphic functions).
An
NC divisor
in
X
is a subvariety
V\!\subset\!X
locally defined by an equation of the form

z_{1}\!\ldots\!z_{k}=0

(2.1)

in a holomorphic coordinate chart
(z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
on
X
.
The sheaf of local meromorphic functions with simple poles along the smooth locus of
V

is freely generated in such a coordinate chart by the meromorphic function
1/z_{1}\!\ldots\!z_{k}

as a module over
\mathcal{O}_{X}
.
Since this sheaf is locally free of rank 1,
it is the sheaf of local holomorphic sections of a holomorphic line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
.
The constant function 1 on
X
determines a holomorphic section
s
of this sheaf
satisfying the properties of
s_{\mathcal{R}}
in Proposition
1.1
(1)
.
It is immediate that (
1.1
) holds as well.
The dual of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
is the holomorphic line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{X}(-V)
;
the sheaf of its local holomorphic sections is freely generated in a coordinate chart
as above by the holomorphic function
z_{1}\!\ldots\!z_{k}
.

In a local chart as in (
2.1
),
the sheaf
\mathcal{T}X\!\equiv\!\mathcal{O}(TX)
of local holomorphic sections of the tangent bundle
TX
is generated by
the coordinate vector fields
\partial_{z_{1}},\ldots,\partial_{z_{n}}
.
The
logarithmic tangent sheaf

\mathcal{T}X(-\log V)
is the subsheaf of
\mathcal{T}X
generated by
the vector fields

\partial^{\log}_{z_{1}}\equiv z_{1}\partial_{z_{1}},\quad\ldots,\quad\partial^{\log}_{z_{k}}\equiv z_{k}\partial_{z_{k}},\quad\partial_{z_{k+1}},\quad\ldots,\quad\partial_{z_{n}}.

The dual of this subsheaf is the sheaf of logarithmic 1-forms
\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log V)
is the sheaf generated by

\frac{\textnormal{d}z_{1}}{z_{1}},\quad\ldots,\quad\frac{\textnormal{d}z_{k}}{z_{k}},\quad\textnormal{d}z_{k+1},\quad\ldots,\quad\textnormal{d}z_{n}.

Since
\mathcal{T}X(-\log V)
is locally free,
it is the sheaf of local holomorphic sections of a holomorphic vector bundle
TX(-\log V)
.
The inclusion of
\mathcal{T}X(-\log V)
into
\mathcal{T}X
gives rise to a holomorphic homomorphism

\iota\!:TX(-\log V)\longrightarrow TX

that realizes every section of
TX(-\log V)
as a section of
TX
with values in
TV
along
V
.

The normalization
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!V\!\subset\!X
of
V
is an immersion.
The inclusion of the sheaf
\Omega^{1}_{X}
of 1-forms on
X
into
\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log V)

and the Poincare residue map induce an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\Omega_{X}^{1}\longrightarrow\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log V)\longrightarrow\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}\longrightarrow 0

of sheaves on
X
,
where
\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}
is the direct image (or push-forward) sheaf of
the structure sheaf
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}
of
\widetilde{V}
.
Therefore,

c\big(\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log V)\!\big)=c\big(\Omega_{X}^{1}\big)\,c\big(\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}\big).

(2.2)

If
V\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S}V_{i}
is an SC divisor, then

\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}\!=\!\bigoplus_{i\in S}\mathcal{O}_{V_{i}}
as sheaves on
X
.
Furthermore, there is an exact sequence

0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(-V_{i})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{V_{i}}\longrightarrow 0

of sheaves on
X
for each
i\!\in\!S
.
Thus,

c\big(\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}})\prod_{i\in S}\!\!\big(1\!-\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V_{i}]_{X}\big)\!\big)=1\in H^{2}(X;\mathbb{Z})

in this case. We thus obtain (
1.7
) in the complex setting.

For an arbitrary NC divisor
V\!\subset\!X
,
the derived direct image sheaf
\iota_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}

coincides with the direct image sheaf
\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}

because the higher derived functors for an immersion vanish.
Along with the Grothendieck-Riemann-Roch theorem, this gives

\textnormal{ch}\big(\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}\big)\,\textnormal{td}(X)=\textnormal{ch}\big(\iota_{!}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}\big)\,\textnormal{td}(X)=\iota_{*}\big(\textnormal{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}})\,\textnormal{td}(\widetilde{V})\!\big)=\iota_{*}\big(\textnormal{td}(\widetilde{V})\!\big),

where
ch
is the Chern character and
td
is the Todd class;
see
[
32
, Theorem 1.3]
.
Thus,

\textnormal{ch}\big(\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}}\big)=\frac{\iota_{*}(\textnormal{td}(\widetilde{V}))}{\textnormal{td}(X)}\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q}).

(2.3)

This formula holds only with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients because the Chern character is
a map from the K-theory of
X
to the rational Chow group of
X
.
The proof of (
2.3
) in
[
32
, Corollary 5.22]
uses blowups
to reduce the problem to SC divisors;
we are not aware of a direct way for obtaining (
2.3
).
For the purposes of computing
c(TX(-\log V)\!)
via (
2.2
),
it is still necessary to translate
\textnormal{ch}(\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}})
into
c(\iota_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{V}})
.
Nevertheless, it is feasible to directly study the change in
c(TX(-\log V)\!)

under blowups, as is done in the proofs of Lemma
1.3

in Section
5.4
, and
to relate
c(TX(-\log V)\!)
to
c(TX)
in the spirit of (
2.2
),
as is done in the proof of (
1.5
) in Section
6.4
.

Let
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,
\pi\!:\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow\!X
,
and
\mathbb{E},\overline{V}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}
be as in Lemma
1.3

and
\widetilde{U}\!\equiv\!\pi^{-1}(U)
be the preimage of a coordinate chart
U
as
in the sentence (
2.1
).
For each
i\!=\!1,\ldots,k
,

\widetilde{U}_{i}\equiv\big\{\!\big(z_{i},(u_{j})_{j\in[k]-i},(z_{j})_{j\in[n]-[k]}\big)\!:\big(u_{1}z_{1},\ldots,u_{i-1}z_{i},z_{i},u_{i+1}z_{i},\ldots,u_{k}z_{i},(z_{j})_{j\in[n]-[k]}\big)\!\in\!U\big\},

where
[k]\!\equiv\!\{1,\ldots,k\}
, is a coordinate chart on
\widetilde{U}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}
;
these charts cover
\widetilde{U}
.
Since

\displaystyle\overline{V}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{i}=\big(u_{1}\ldots u_{i-1}u_{i+1}\ldots u_{k}\!=\!0\big),\quad\mathbb{E}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{i}=(z_{i}\!=\!0),

\displaystyle\hbox{and}\qquad\frac{\textnormal{d}z_{j}}{z_{j}}=\frac{\textnormal{d}u_{j}}{u_{j}}+\frac{\textnormal{d}z_{i}}{z_{i}}\qquad\forall~j\in[k]\!-\!i,

we obtain that

\pi^{*}\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log V)=\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{X}}\big(\log(\overline{V}\!\cup\!\mathbb{E})\!\big).

This establishes Lemma
1.3
in the complex setting.

2.2
Smooth symplectic divisors

It is fairly straightforward to adapt the constructions of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
and
TX(-\log V)

in Section
2.1
via the Symplectic Neighborhood Theorem.
Before doing so below, we carefully formulate the relevant notions.

Let
V
be a smooth manifold.
For a vector bundle
\pi\!:\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!V
, we denote by
\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}

the radial vector field
on the total space of
\mathcal{N}
; it is given by

\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}(v)=(v,v)\in\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}=T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{ver}}\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow T\mathcal{N}\,.

Let
\Omega
be a fiberwise 2-form on
\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!V
.
A connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{N}
induces a projection
T\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}
and thus
determines an extension
\Omega_{\nabla}
of
\Omega
to a 2-form on
the total space of
\mathcal{N}
.
If
\omega
is a closed 2-form on
V
, the 2-form

\widetilde{\omega}\equiv\pi^{*}\omega+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}}\Omega_{\nabla}\equiv\pi^{*}\omega+\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\big(\Omega_{\nabla}(\zeta_{\mathcal{N}},\cdot)\big)

(2.4)

on the total space of
\mathcal{N}
is also closed and
restricts to
\Omega
on
\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}\!=\!T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{ver}}
.
If
\omega
is a symplectic form on
V
and
\Omega
is a fiberwise symplectic form on
\mathcal{N}
,
then
\widetilde{\omega}
is a symplectic form on a neighborhood of
V
in
\mathcal{N}
.

We call
\pi\!:(L,\rho,\nabla)\!\longrightarrow\!V
a
Hermitian line bundle
if

L\!\longrightarrow\!V
is a smooth complex line bundle,

\rho
is a Hermitian metric on
L
,
and
\nabla
is a
\rho
-compatible connection on
L
.
We use the same notation
\rho
to denote the square of the norm function on
L

and the Hermitian form on
L
which is
\mathbb{C}
-antilinear in the second input.
Thus,

\rho(v)\equiv\rho(v,v),\quad\rho(\mathfrak{i}v,w)=\mathfrak{i}\rho(v,w)=-\rho(v,\mathfrak{i}w)\qquad\forall~(v,w)\!\in\!L\!\times_{V}\!L.

Let
\rho^{\mathbb{R}}
denote the real part of the form
\rho
.

A Riemannian metric on an oriented real vector bundle
L\!\longrightarrow\!V
of rank 2
determines a complex structure on the fibers of
V
.
A
Hermitian structure
on an oriented real vector bundle
L\!\longrightarrow\!V
of rank 2
is a pair
(\rho,\nabla)
such that
(L,\rho,\nabla)
is a Hermitian line bundle
with the complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\rho}
determined by the Riemannian metric
\rho^{\mathbb{R}}
.
If
\Omega
is a fiberwise symplectic form on an oriented vector bundle
L\!\longrightarrow\!V
of rank 2,
an
\Omega
-compatible Hermitian structure
on
L
is a Hermitian structure
(\rho,\nabla)
on
L

such that
\Omega(\cdot,\mathfrak{i}_{\rho}\cdot)=\rho^{\mathbb{R}}(\cdot,\cdot)
.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
X
be a manifold and
V\!\subset\!X
be a submanifold
with normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{X}V\!\longrightarrow\!V
.
A (smooth)
regularization for
V
in
X
is a diffeomorphism
\Psi\!:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow\!X

from a neighborhood of
V
in
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
onto a neighborhood of
V
in
X
such
that
\Psi(x)\!=\!x
and the isomorphism

\mathcal{N}_{X}V|_{x}=T_{x}^{\textnormal{ver}}\mathcal{N}_{X}V\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow T_{x}\mathcal{N}_{X}V\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{d}_{x}\Psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{x}X\longrightarrow\frac{T_{x}X}{T_{x}V}\equiv\mathcal{N}_{X}V|_{x}

is the identity for every
x\!\in\!V
.

Let
V
be a closed symplectic submanifold of a symplectic submanifold in
(X,\omega)
.
The normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
of
V
in
X
then inherits a fiberwise symplectic form
\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V}

from
\omega
via the isomorphism

\pi_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V}\!:TV^{\omega}\!\equiv\!\big\{v\!\in\!T_{x}X\!:\,x\!\in\!V,\,\omega(v,w)\!=\!0~\forall\,w\!\in\!T_{x}V\big\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\approx}}{{\longrightarrow}}\frac{TX|_{V}}{TV}\!\equiv\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V\,.

The symplectic form
\omega|_{V}
on
V
,
the fiberwise 2-form
\Omega\!\equiv\!\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V}
on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
,
and a connection
\nabla
on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
thus determine a 2-form
\widetilde{\omega}_{\nabla}

on the total space of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
via (
2.4
).
By the Symplectic Neighborhood Theorem, there exists
a regularization
\Psi\!:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow\!X
for
V
in
X
so that
\Psi^{*}\omega\!=\!\widetilde{\omega}_{\nabla}|_{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}
.

Suppose in addition that
V
is of codimension 2, i.e.
V
is a smooth symplectic divisor
in
(X,\omega)
.
If
(\rho,\nabla)
is an
\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V}
-compatible Hermitian structure on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
,
the triple
\mathcal{R}\!=\!(\!(\rho,\nabla),\Psi)
is an
\omega
-regularization for
V
in
X

in the sense of Definition
3.5

and determines a fiberwise complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\rho}
on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
.
Let

\begin{split}&\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V)=\big(\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{\!*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{X}V\!\sqcup\!(X\!-\!V)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\big)\big/\!\!\sim\,\longrightarrow\Psi(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})\!\cup\!(X\!-\!V)\!=\!X,\\
&\hskip 21.68121pt\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{\!*}\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{X}V\ni\big(\Psi(v),v,cv\big)\sim\big(\Psi(v),c\big)\in(X\!-\!V)\!\times\!\mathbb{C},\end{split}

(2.5)

where
\pi\!:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow\!V
is the bundle projection map.
This defines a smooth complex line bundle over
X
.
The smooth section
s_{\mathcal{R}}
of this bundle given by

s_{\mathcal{R}}(x)=\begin{cases}[x,v,v],&\hbox{if}~x\!=\!\Psi(v),~v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}^{\prime};\\
[x,1],&\hbox{if}~x\!\in\!X\!-\!V;\end{cases}

satisfies the properties stated in Proposition
1.1
(1)
.

For each
v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V
, the connection
\nabla
determines an injective homomorphism

\textnormal{h}_{\nabla;v}\!:T_{\pi(v)}V\longrightarrow T_{v}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V)

(2.6)

with the image complementary to the image of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
.
Let

\begin{split}&T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)=\Big(\!\!\big(\!(\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{\!*}\pi^{*}TV)\!\oplus\!\Psi(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\big)\!\sqcup\!T(X\!-\!V)\!\!\Big)\!\Big/\!\!\!\sim\,\longrightarrow\Psi(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})\!\cup\!(X\!-\!V)\!=\!X,\\
&~(\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{\!*}\pi^{*}TV)\!\oplus\!\Psi(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\ni\big(\Psi(v),v,w\big)\!\oplus\!\big(\Psi(v),c\big)\sim\textnormal{d}_{v}\Psi\big(\textnormal{h}_{\nabla;v}(w)\!+\!cv)\in T(X\!-\!V).\end{split}

This defines a smooth vector bundle over
X
.
The smooth bundle homomorphism (
1.3
) defined by

\iota_{\mathcal{R}}(\dot{x})=\begin{cases}\textnormal{d}_{v}\Psi\big(\textnormal{h}_{\nabla;v}(w)\!+\!cv),&\hbox{if}~\dot{x}\!=\!\big[(\Psi(v),v,w)\!\oplus\!(\Psi(v),c)\big],~v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}^{\prime};\\
\dot{x},&\hbox{if}~\dot{x}\!\in\!T(X\!-\!V);\end{cases}

satisfies the properties stated in Theorem
1.2
(1)
.

An almost complex structure
J
on
V
and the fiberwise complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\rho}

on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
determine an almost complex structure
J_{\mathcal{R}}
on the total space of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V

via the connection
\nabla
.
We call an almost complex structure
J
on
X

\mathcal{R}
-compatible
if

J
preserves
TV\!\subset\!TX|_{V}
and
\Psi
intertwines
J
and

J_{\mathcal{R}}\!\equiv\!(J|_{TV})_{\mathcal{R}}
, i.e.

J(TV)\subset TV\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad J\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\Psi=\textnormal{d}\Psi\!\circ\!J_{\mathcal{R}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}^{\prime}}\,.

Such an almost complex structure
J
induces a fiberwise complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R},J}

on
T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)
satisfying Theorem
1.2
(2)
.
It can be constructed by pasting together
J_{\mathcal{R}}\!\circ\!\{\textnormal{d}\Psi\}^{-1}

and an almost complex structure on
X\!-\!V
.

We denote by
\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(X,V)
the space of symplectic forms on
X
that restrict to
symplectic forms on
V
,
by
\textnormal{Aux}(X,V)
the space of pairs
(\omega,\mathcal{R})
consisting of
\omega\!\in\!\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(X,V)

and an
\omega
-compatible regularization
\mathcal{R}
for
V
in
X
,
and by
\textnormal{AK}(X,V)
the space of triples
(\omega,\mathcal{R},J)
consisting of
(\omega,\mathcal{R})\!\in\!\textnormal{Aux}(X,V)

and an almost complex structure
J
on
X
compatible with
\omega
and
\mathcal{R}
.
Since the Symplectic Neighborhood Theorem can be applied with families of symplectic forms
parametrized by compact manifolds, the projection

\textnormal{Aux}(X,V)\longrightarrow\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(X,V),\qquad(\omega,\mathcal{R})\longrightarrow\omega,

(2.7)

is a weak homotopy equivalence.
It is straightforward, by adapting the proof of
[
25
, Prp 4.1]
, for example,
to show that the projection

\textnormal{AK}(X,V)\longrightarrow\textnormal{Aux}(X,V),\qquad(\omega,\mathcal{R},J)\longrightarrow(\omega,\mathcal{R}),

(2.8)

is also a weak homotopy equivalence.

The above constructions of the complex line bundle
(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{i})
and
the vector bundle
TX(-\log V)
can be applied with compact families in
\textnormal{Aux}(X,V)
.
The construction of the complex vector bundle
(TX(-\log V),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R},J})

can be applied with compact families in
\textnormal{AK}(X,V)
.
Along with the previous paragraph, this confirms the statements of
Proposition
1.1
(2)
and Theorem
1.2
(3)

for smooth symplectic divisors
V
.

The constructions of the complex line bundle
(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V),\mathfrak{i})
and
the vector bundle
TX(-\log V)
do not involve the symplectic form
\omega
directly.
The first construction can be carried out for any closed codimension 2 submanifold
V

of a smooth manifold
X
endowed with a complex structure on the normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{X}V

and a smooth regularization
\Psi
.
The constructions of the vector bundle
TX(-\log V)
and of the complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R},J}

on it require in addition a connection on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V
in the first case and
also an
\mathcal{R}
-compatible almost complex structure
J
on
X
in the second case.

Corollary
2.3
below is used later in this paper.
We deduce it from the following observation.

Lemma 2.2
.

Suppose
V
is a smooth manifold,
\pi\!:\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!V
is a vector bundle, and

\nabla
is a connection in
\mathcal{N}
.
Let
T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{hor}}\!\subset\!T\mathcal{N}
be the horizontal tangent subbundle determined by
\nabla
.
If
\widetilde{\nabla}\!\equiv\!\pi^{*}\nabla
is the connection in
\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathcal{N}
determined by
\nabla
, then

\widetilde{\nabla}\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}\big|_{T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{hor}}}\!=\!0\!:T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{hor}}\longrightarrow\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}.

(2.9)

If in addition
\mathcal{N}
is a complex vector bundle (and
\nabla
is a complex linear connection), then

\widetilde{\nabla}\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}\big|_{T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{ver}}}\!\circ\!\mathfrak{i}\!=\!\mathfrak{i}\widetilde{\nabla}\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}\big|_{T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{ver}}}\!:T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{ver}}\longrightarrow\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}.

(2.10)

Corollary 2.3
.

Suppose
(V,J)
is an almost complex manifold,
\pi\!:\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!V
is a complex line bundle,

\nabla
is a connection in
\mathcal{N}
, and
\widetilde{\nabla}\!\equiv\!\pi^{*}\nabla
.
If

\Phi\!:(\mathcal{N}\!-\!V)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\approx}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}\big|_{\mathcal{N}-V},\qquad\Phi(v,c)=(v,cv),

then
\Phi^{*}\widetilde{\nabla}\!-\!\textnormal{d}
is a
(1,0)
-form on
\mathcal{N}\!-\!V

with respect to the almost complex structure
J_{\nabla}
on
\mathcal{N}\!-\!V

determined by
J
and
\nabla
.

Proof.

The 1-form
\Phi^{*}\widetilde{\nabla}\!-\!\textnormal{d}
is given by

\big\{\!\Phi^{*}\widetilde{\nabla}\!-\!\textnormal{d}\big\}1=\Phi^{-1}\!\circ\!\widetilde{\nabla}(\Phi\!\circ\!1)=\Phi^{-1}\!\circ\!\widetilde{\nabla}\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}.

(2.11)

The almost complex structure
J_{\nabla}
restricts to
\{\textnormal{d}\pi\}^{*}J
on
T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{hor}}

and to
\pi^{*}\mathfrak{i}
on
T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{ver}}
.
The claim thus follows from Lemma
2.2
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.2
.

Suppose
U
is an open subset of
V
and
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n}\!\in\!\Gamma(U;\mathcal{N})
is
a frame for
\mathcal{N}
over
U
.
Let
\theta^{k}_{l}\!\in\!\Gamma(U;T^{*}U)
be such that

\nabla\xi_{l}=\sum_{k=1}^{k=n}\xi_{k}\theta^{k}_{l}\equiv\sum_{k=1}^{k=n}\theta^{k}_{l}\!\otimes\!\xi_{k}\in\Gamma\big(U;T^{*}U\!\otimes\!\mathcal{N}\big)\quad\forall~l\!=\!1,\ldots,n.

(2.12)

The frame
\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{n}
determines an identification
\mathcal{N}|_{U}\!=\!U\!\times\!\mathbb{R}^{n}

so that

\begin{split}\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}(v)&=\sum_{l=1}^{l=n}c_{l}\xi_{l}(x),\\
T_{v}\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{hor}}&=\big\{\!\big(\dot{x},-\sum_{l=1}^{l=n}c_{l}\theta^{1}_{l}(\dot{x}),\ldots-\sum_{l=1}^{l=n}c_{l}\theta^{n}_{l}(\dot{x})\!\big)\!:\dot{x}\!\in\!T_{x}V\big\}\end{split}\quad\forall\,v\!\equiv\!(x,c_{1},\ldots,c_{n})\!\in\!\mathcal{N}|_{U};

(2.13)

see the proof of
[
38
, Lemma 1.1]
.

For each
l\!=\!1,\ldots,n
, let
\widetilde{\xi}_{l}\!=\!\pi^{*}\xi_{l}\!\in\!\Gamma(\mathcal{N}|_{U};\pi^{*}\mathcal{N})
.
By the definition of
\widetilde{\nabla}
and (
2.12
),

\widetilde{\nabla}\widetilde{\xi}_{l}=\sum_{k=1}^{k=n}(\theta^{k}_{l}\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\pi)\!\otimes\!\widetilde{\xi}_{k}\quad\forall~l\!=\!1,\ldots,n.

Thus,

\widetilde{\nabla}\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}\big|_{(x,c_{1},\ldots,c_{n})}=\sum_{l=1}^{l=n}\sum_{k=1}^{k=n}c_{l}(\theta^{k}_{l}\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\pi)\!\otimes\!\widetilde{\xi}_{k}+\sum_{l=1}^{l=n}(\textnormal{d}c_{l})\!\otimes\!\widetilde{\xi}_{l}.

(2.14)

Along with the second statement in (
2.13
), this gives (
2.9
).

The first summand on the right-hand side of (
2.14
) vanishes on
T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{ver}}
.
If
\mathcal{N}
is a complex vector bundle, the above applies with
\mathbb{R}
replaced by
\mathbb{C}
.
The second summand on the right-hand side of (
2.14
) is
\mathbb{C}
-linear on
T\mathcal{N}^{\textnormal{ver}}

in this case.
This gives (
2.10
).
∎

Remark 2.4
.

By the proof of Lemma
2.2
, the 1-form in (
2.11
) is given by

(\Phi^{*}\widetilde{\nabla}\!-\!\textnormal{d})\big|_{(x,z)}=\theta_{1}^{1}|_{x}\!+\!\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{z}\,.

Thus, the curvature
F^{\Phi^{*}\widetilde{\nabla}}
of the connection
\Phi^{*}\widetilde{\nabla}

on
(\mathcal{N}\!-\!V)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
is given by

F^{\Phi^{*}\widetilde{\nabla}}=\textnormal{d}(\pi^{*}\theta_{1}^{1})=\pi^{*}F^{\nabla}\,.

3
SC symplectic divisors

For
N\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, let

[N]=\{1,\ldots,N\}\,.

If
\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!V
is a vector bundle,
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}
, and
V^{\prime}\!\subset\!V
, we define

\mathcal{N}^{\prime}|_{V^{\prime}}\!=\!\mathcal{N}|_{V^{\prime}}\cap\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
.

3.1
Definitions

Let
X
be a (smooth) manifold.
For a collection
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
of submanifolds of
X
and
I\!\subset\!S
, let

V_{I}\equiv\bigcap_{i\in I}\!V_{i}\subset X,\quad V_{I,\partial}=\bigcup_{I^{\prime}\supsetneq I}\!\!V_{I^{\prime}},\quad V_{I}^{\circ}=V_{I}\!-\!V_{I,\partial}\,.

Such a collection
is called
transverse
if any subcollection
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in I}
of these submanifolds
intersects transversely, i.e. the homomorphism

T_{x}X\oplus\bigoplus_{i\in I}T_{x}V_{i}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in I}T_{x}X,\qquad\big(v,(v_{i})_{i\in I}\big)\longrightarrow(v\!+\!v_{i})_{i\in I}\,,

(3.1)

is surjective for all
x\!\in\!V_{I}
.
By the Inverse Function Theorem,
each subspace
V_{I}\!\subset\!X
is then a submanifold of
X

of codimension

\textnormal{codim}_{X}V_{I}=\sum_{i\in I}\textnormal{codim}_{X}V_{i}

and the homomorphisms

\begin{split}\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{i}\big|_{V_{I}}\quad&\forall~I\!\subset\!S,\qquad\mathcal{N}_{V_{I-i}}V_{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{i}\big|_{V_{I}}\quad\forall~i\!\in\!I\!\subset\!S,\\
&\bigoplus_{i\in I-I^{\prime}}\!\!\!\mathcal{N}_{V_{I-i}}V_{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{V_{I^{\prime}}}V_{I}\quad\forall~I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S\end{split}

(3.2)

induced by inclusions of the tangent bundles are isomorphisms.

As detailed in
[
14
, Section 2.1]
,
a transverse collection
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
of oriented submanifolds of
an oriented manifold
X

of even codimensions induces an orientation on each submanifold
V_{I}\!\subset\!X

with
|I|\!\geq\!2
; we call it
the intersection orientation of
V_{I}
.
If
V_{I}
is zero-dimensional, it is a discrete collection of points in
X

and the homomorphism (
3.1
) is an isomorphism at each point
x\!\in\!V_{I}
;
the intersection orientation of
V_{I}
at
x\!\in\!V_{I}

then corresponds to a plus or minus sign, depending on whether this isomorphism
is orientation-preserving or orientation-reversing.
We call the original orientations of

X\!=\!V_{\emptyset}
and
V_{i}\!=\!V_{\{i\}}

the intersection orientations

of these submanifolds
V_{I}
of
X
with
|I|\!<\!2
.

Suppose
(X,\omega)
is a symplectic manifold and
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
is a transverse collection
of submanifolds of
X
such that each
V_{I}
is a symplectic submanifold of
(X,\omega)
.
Each
V_{I}
then carries an orientation induced by
\omega|_{V_{I}}
,
which we call the
\omega
-orientation
.
If
V_{I}
is zero-dimensional, it is automatically a symplectic submanifold of
(X,\omega)
;
the
\omega
-orientation of
V_{I}
at each point
x\!\in\!V_{I}
corresponds to the plus sign
by definition.
By the previous paragraph, the
\omega
-orientations of
X
and
V_{i}
with
i\!\in\!I

also induce intersection orientations on all
V_{I}
.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
(X,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold.
A
simple crossings
(or
SC
)
symplectic divisor

in
(X,\omega)
is a finite transverse union

V\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S}V_{i}
of closed submanifolds of
X
of codimension 2 such that

V_{I}
is a symplectic submanifold of
(X,\omega)
for every
I\!\subset\!S

and the intersection and
\omega
-orientations of
V_{I}
agree.

The singular locus
V_{\partial}\!\subset\!V
of an SC symplectic divisor

V\!\subset\!X
is the union

V_{\partial}\equiv\bigcup_{I\subset S,|I|\geq 2}\!\!\!\!\!\!V_{I}~.

An SC symplectic divisor
V
with
|S|\!=\!1
is a smooth symplectic divisor in the usual sense.
If
(X,\omega)
is a 4-dimensional symplectic manifold,
a finite transverse union
V\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S}V_{i}
of closed symplectic submanifolds of
X

of codimension 2 is an SC symplectic divisor if all points of the pairwise intersections

V_{i_{1}}\!\cap\!V_{i_{2}}
with
i_{1}\!\neq\!i_{2}
are positive.
By
[
14
, Example 2.7]
, it is not sufficient to consider
the deepest (non-empty) intersections in higher dimensions.

Definition 3.2
.

Let
X
be a manifold and
V\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S}V_{i}
be a finite transverse union of
closed submanifolds of
X
of codimension 2.
A
symplectic structure on
V
in
X
is a symplectic form
\omega

on
X
such that
V_{I}
is a symplectic submanifold of
(X,\omega)
for all
I\!\subset\!S
.

For
X
and
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
as in Definition
3.2
,
we denote by
\textnormal{Symp}(X,\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S})
the space of all symplectic structures
on
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
in
X
and by

\textnormal{Symp}^{+}\big(X,\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}\big)\subset\textnormal{Symp}\big(X,\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}\big)

the subspace of the symplectic forms
\omega
such that
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}

is an SC symplectic divisor in
(X,\omega)
.

3.2
Regularizations

Let
V
be a smooth manifold with a 2-form
\omega
and

(L_{i},\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})_{i\in I}
be a finite collection of Hermitian line bundles over
V
.
If each
(\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})
is compatible with a fiberwise symplectic form
\Omega_{i}
on
L_{i}

and

(\mathcal{N},\Omega,\nabla)\equiv\bigoplus_{i\in I}\big(L_{i},\Omega_{i},\nabla^{(i)}\big),

then the 2-form (
2.4
) is given by

\widetilde{\omega}=\omega_{(\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})_{i\in I}}\equiv\pi^{*}\omega+\frac{1}{2}\bigoplus_{i\in I}\pi_{I;i}^{*}\textnormal{d}\big((\Omega_{i})_{\nabla^{(i)}}(\zeta_{L_{i}},\cdot)\big),

(3.3)

where
\pi_{I;i}\!:\mathcal{N}\!\longrightarrow\!L_{i}
is the component projection map.

If in addition
\Psi\!:V^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow\!V
is a smooth map and

(L_{i}^{\prime},\rho_{i}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime(i)})_{i\in I}
is a finite collection of Hermitian line
bundles over
V^{\prime}
, we call a (fiberwise) vector bundle isomorphism

\widetilde{\Psi}\!:\bigoplus_{i\in I}L^{\prime}_{i}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in I}L_{i}

covering
\Psi
a
product Hermitian isomorphism
if

\widetilde{\Psi}\!:(L_{i}^{\prime},\rho_{i}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime(i)})\longrightarrow\Psi^{*}(L_{i},\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})

is an isomorphism of Hermitian line bundles over
V^{\prime}
for every
i\!\in\!I
.

If
V
is a symplectic submanifold of a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
,
we denote the restriction of
\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V}
to a subbundle
L\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V

by
\omega|_{L}
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
X
be a manifold,
V\!\subset\!X
be a submanifold, and

\mathcal{N}_{X}V=\bigoplus_{i\in I}L_{i}

be a fixed splitting into oriented rank 2 subbundles.
If
\omega
is a symplectic form on
X
such that
V
is a symplectic submanifold
and
\omega|_{L_{i}}
is nondegenerate for every
i\!\in\!I
, then
an
\omega
-regularization for
V
in
X
is a tuple
((\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})_{i\in I},\Psi)
,
where
(\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})
is an
\omega|_{L_{i}}
-compatible Hermitian structure on
L_{i}

for each
i\!\in\!I
and
\Psi
is a regularization for
V
in
X

in the sense of Definition
2.1
, such that

\Psi^{*}\omega=\omega_{(\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})_{i\in I}}\big|_{\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi)}.

Suppose
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
is a transverse collection of codimension 2 submanifolds of
X
.
For each
I\!\subset\!S
, the last isomorphism in (
3.2
) with
I^{\prime}\!=\!\emptyset

provides
a natural decomposition

\pi_{I}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}\!=\!\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{V_{I-i}}V_{I}\longrightarrow V_{I}

(3.4)

of the normal bundle of
V_{I}
in
X
into oriented rank 2 subbundles.
We take this decomposition as given for the purposes of applying Definition
3.3
.
If in addition
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I
, let

\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}\equiv\bigoplus_{i\in I-I^{\prime}}\!\!\!\mathcal{N}_{V_{I-i}}V_{I}=\mathcal{N}_{V_{I^{\prime}}}V_{I}\longrightarrow V_{I}

(3.5)

be the bundle projection.
There are canonical identifications

\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}=\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I^{\prime}}|_{V_{I}},\quad\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}=\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}=\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I^{\prime}}\qquad\forall~I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\![N].

(3.6)

The first equality in the second statement above
is used in particular in (
3.10
).

Definition 3.4
.

Let
X
be a manifold and
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
be a transverse collection
of submanifolds of
X
.
A
system of regularizations for

\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
in
X
is a tuple

(\Psi_{I})_{I\subset S}
, where
\Psi_{I}
is a regularization for
V_{I}
in
X

in the sense of Definition
2.1
, such that

\Psi_{I}\big(\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}\!\cap\!\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I})\big)=V_{I^{\prime}}\!\cap\!\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{I})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{I})\!\cap\!\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{J})=\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{I\cup J})

(3.7)

for all
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
and
J\!\subset\!S
.

Given a system of regularizations as in Definition
3.4

and
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
, let

\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}=\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}\!\cap\!\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I}),\qquad\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}\equiv\Psi_{I}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}\longrightarrow V_{I^{\prime}}\,.

(3.8)

The map
\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}
is a regularization for
V_{I}
in
V_{I^{\prime}}
.
As explained in
[
14
, Section 2.2]
,
\Psi_{I}
determines
an isomorphism

\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}\!:\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I^{\prime}}\big|_{V_{I^{\prime}}\cap\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{I})}

(3.9)

of vector bundles covering
\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}
and
respecting the natural decompositions of

\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}\!=\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I^{\prime}}|_{V_{I}}
and
\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I^{\prime}}
.
By the last assumption in Definition
2.1
,

\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}\big|_{\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}|_{V_{I}}}\!=\!\textnormal{id}\!:\,\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I^{\prime}}|_{V_{I}}

under the canonical identification of
\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}
with
\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I^{\prime}}|_{V_{I}}
.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
X
be a manifold and
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
be a transverse
collection of submanifolds of
X
.

(1)

A
regularization for
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
in
X

is a system of regularizations
(\Psi_{I})_{I\subset S}

for
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
in
X
such that

\begin{split}&\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I})\subset\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}},\quad\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}\big(\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I})\big)=\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I^{\prime}})\big|_{V_{I^{\prime}}\cap\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{I})},\\
&\hskip 130.08621pt\Psi_{I}=\Psi_{I^{\prime}}\circ\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}|_{\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I})}\end{split}

(3.10)

for all
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
.

(2)

Suppose in addition that
V=\bigcup_{i\in S}V_{i}
is an SC symplectic divisor in
(X,\omega)
.
An
\omega
-regularization for
V
in
X
is a tuple

(\mathcal{R}_{I})_{I\subset S}\equiv\big((\rho_{I;i},\nabla^{(I;i)})_{i\in I},\Psi_{I}\big)_{I\subset S}

such that
\mathcal{R}_{I}
is an
\omega
-regularization for
V_{I}
in
X
for each
I\!\subset\!S
,

(\Psi_{I})_{I\subset S}
is a regularization for
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
in
X
,
and the induced vector bundle isomorphisms (
3.9
)
are product Hermitian isomorphisms for all
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
.

If
(\Psi_{I})_{I\subset S}
is a regularization for
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
in
X
,
then

\begin{split}&\hskip 72.26999pt\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime},\pi_{I;I^{\prime\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime\prime}}|_{\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}\subset\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}},\\
&\Psi_{I;I^{\prime\prime}}=\Psi_{I^{\prime};I^{\prime\prime}}\circ\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}\,,\quad\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime\prime}}=\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I^{\prime};I^{\prime\prime}}\circ\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}\big|_{\pi_{I;I^{\prime\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime\prime}}|_{\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime}}}\end{split}

(3.11)

for all
I^{\prime\prime}\!\subset\!I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
.

An almost complex structure
J
on
X
preserving
TV_{I}\!\subset\!TX|_{V_{I}}
and
an
\omega
-regularization
\mathcal{R}_{I}
for
V_{I}
in
X
as in Definition
3.3

determine an almost complex structure
J_{\mathcal{R};I}
on the total space of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}

via the connection
\nabla^{(I)}\!\equiv\!\bigoplus_{i\in I}\nabla^{(I;i)}
.
We call an almost complex structure
J
on
X

compatible
with
an
\omega
-regularization
(\mathcal{R}_{I})_{I\subset S}

as in Definition
3.5
(2)

if

J(TV_{I})\subset TV_{I}\quad\hbox{and}\quad J\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\Psi_{I}=\textnormal{d}\Psi_{I}\!\circ\!J_{\mathcal{R};I}\big|_{\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I})}\qquad\forall\,I\!\subset\!S\,.

The notion of regularization of Definition
3.5
(2)

readily extends to families of symplectic forms;
see
[
14
, Definition 2.12(2)]
.
We define the spaces
\textnormal{Aux}(X,V)
of pairs
(\omega,\mathcal{R})
and
\textnormal{AK}(X,V)
of triples
(\omega,\mathcal{R},J)

as in Section
2.2
.
By
[
14
, Theorem 2.17]
, the map (
2.7
) is a weak homotopy equivalence
in the present setting as well.
On the other hand, it is still straightforward to show that
the map (
2.8
) is also a weak homotopy equivalence in the present setting.

3.3
Constructions

We now construct the bundles
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)
and
TX(-\log V)
for an SC symplectic divisor
V

in a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
.
We fix an
\omega
-regularization
\mathcal{R}
for
V
in
X
as in
Definition
3.5
(2)
.
For the purposes of constructing a complex structure on
TX(-\log V)
, we also
fix an almost complex structure
J
on
X
compatible with
\omega
and
\mathcal{R}
.

For
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
, let
\pi_{I}
,
\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}
,
\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}
,
\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}
,
and
\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}

be as in (
3.4
), (
3.5
), (
3.8
), and (
3.9
).
In what follows, we write an element
v_{I}\!\equiv\!(v_{i})_{i\in I}
of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}
as

v_{I}=(v_{I;I^{\prime}},v_{I;I-I^{\prime}})\quad\hbox{with}\quad v_{I;I^{\prime}}\!\equiv\!(v_{i})_{i\in I-I^{\prime}}\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}~~\hbox{and}~~v_{I;I-I^{\prime}}\!\equiv\!(v_{i})_{i\in I^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}.

We denote by
\nabla^{(I)}
and
\nabla^{(I;I^{\prime})}

the connections on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}
and
\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}

induced by the connections
\nabla^{(I;i)}
on the direct summands of these vector bundles.
Let

\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(I)};v_{I}}\!:T_{\pi_{I}(v_{I})}V_{I}\longrightarrow T_{v_{I}}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(I;I^{\prime})};v_{I;I^{\prime}}}\!:T_{\pi_{I;I^{\prime}}(v_{I;I^{\prime}})}V_{I}\longrightarrow T_{v_{I;I^{\prime}}}\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}

be the corresponding injective homomorphisms as in (
2.6
).
Define

\displaystyle\Pi_{I}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}\longrightarrow\bigotimes_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{V_{I-i}}\!V_{I},\qquad\Pi_{I}\big((v_{i})_{i\in I}\big)=\bigotimes_{i\in I}v_{i}\,,

\displaystyle U_{I}^{\circ}=\Psi_{I}\big(\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I})|_{V_{I}^{\circ}}\big)=\textnormal{Im}\,\Psi_{I}-\!\bigcup_{J\not\subset I}\!\!V_{J}\,;

(3.12)

the last equality follows from (
3.7
) and (
3.10
).
For every
I\!\subset\!S
, let

\begin{split}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I}(V)&=\big\{\Psi_{I}^{-1}|_{U_{I}^{\circ}}\big\}^{\!*}\pi_{I}^{\,*}\bigg(\!\bigotimes_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{V_{I-i}}V_{I}\!\bigg)\longrightarrow U_{I}^{\circ},\\
T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V)&=\Big(\!\big\{\Psi_{I}^{-1}|_{U_{I}^{\circ}}\big\}^{\!*}\pi_{I}^{\,*}TV_{I}\Big)\!\oplus\!\big(\!U_{I}^{\circ}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{I}\!\big)\longrightarrow U_{I}^{\circ}.\end{split}

(3.13)

The complex structures
\mathfrak{i}_{\rho_{I;i}}
on

\mathcal{N}_{V_{I-i}}V_{I}\!=\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{i}|_{V_{I}}
encoded in
\mathcal{R}
determine
a complex structure on the complex line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I}(V)
.
The almost complex structure
J|_{TV_{I}}
on
V_{I}
and the standard complex structure on
\mathbb{C}^{I}

determine a complex structure on the vector bundle
T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V)
.

Let
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
.
If
x\!\in\!U_{I}^{\circ}\!\cap\!U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}
, then

\displaystyle x=\Psi_{I}(v_{I})=\Psi_{I^{\prime}}\big(\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}(v_{I;I^{\prime}},v_{I;I-I^{\prime}})\!\big)\quad\hbox{with}\quad

\displaystyle v_{I}=\big(v_{I;I^{\prime}},v_{I;I-I^{\prime}}\big)\!\equiv\!\big(\!(v_{i})_{i\in I-I^{\prime}},(v_{i})_{i\in I^{\prime}}\big)\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}\!\oplus\!\mathcal{N}_{I;I-I^{\prime}}~~\hbox{s.t.}~~v_{i}\!\neq\!0~\forall\,i\!\in\!I\!-\!I^{\prime}.

Since
\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}
is a product Hermitian isomorphism, it follows that the map

\displaystyle\theta_{I^{\prime}I}\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I}(V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I^{\prime}}(V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}},

(3.14)

\displaystyle\theta_{I^{\prime}I}\big(x,v_{I},\Pi_{I}(v_{I;I^{\prime}},w_{I;I-I^{\prime}})\!\big)=\Big(x,\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}(v_{I}),\Pi_{I^{\prime}}\big(\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}(v_{I;I^{\prime}},w_{I;I-I^{\prime}})\!\big)\!\Big),

is a well-defined isomorphism of complex line bundles.
The map

\displaystyle\vartheta_{I^{\prime}I}\!:T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}},

(3.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\vartheta_{I^{\prime}I}\Big(\!(x,v_{I},w)\!\oplus\!\big(x,(c_{i})_{i\in I}\big)\!\!\Big)=\Big(\!x,\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}(v_{I}),\textnormal{d}_{v_{I;I^{\prime}}}\Psi_{I;I^{\prime}}\!\big(\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(I;I^{\prime})};v_{I;I^{\prime}}}(w)\!+\!\!\sum_{i\in I-I^{\prime}}\!\!\!c_{i}v_{i}\!\big)\!\!\Big)&\\
\!\oplus\!\big(x,(c_{i})_{i\in I^{\prime}}\big)&,\end{split}

is similarly a well-defined isomorphism of vector bundles.
Since
J
is an
\mathcal{R}
-compatible almost complex structure on
X
, this isomorphism is
\mathbb{C}
-linear.
By (
3.11
),

\begin{split}\theta_{I^{\prime\prime}I}\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}&=\theta_{I^{\prime\prime}I^{\prime}}\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}\!\circ\!\theta_{I^{\prime}I}\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}},\\
\vartheta_{I^{\prime\prime}I}\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}&=\vartheta_{I^{\prime\prime}I^{\prime}}\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}\!\circ\!\vartheta_{I^{\prime}I}\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}\cap U_{I^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}\end{split}

(3.16)

for all
I^{\prime\prime}\!\subset\!I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I
.

Let
I,K\!\subset\!S
.
By (
3.7
) and (
3.10
),
U_{I}^{\circ}\!\cap\!U_{K}^{\circ}\!\subset\!U_{I\cup K}^{\circ}
.
If
I\!\not\subset\!K
, the maps

\begin{split}\theta_{IK}\!=\!\theta_{I(I\cup K)}|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\!\circ\!\theta_{K(I\cup K)}^{~-1}|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};K}(V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}&\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I}(V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}},\\
\vartheta_{IK}\!=\!\vartheta_{I(I\cup K)}|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\!\circ\!\vartheta_{K(I\cup K)}^{~-1}|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\!:T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{K}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}&\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\end{split}

are thus well-defined isomorphisms of complex vector bundles.
By (
3.16
), the collections
\{\theta_{IK}\}_{I,K\subset S}

and
\{\vartheta_{IK}\}_{I,K\subset S}

satisfy the cocycle condition.
The first collection thus determines a complex line bundle

\begin{split}&\pi\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V)\!=\!\bigg(\bigsqcup_{I\subset S}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I}(V)\!\!\bigg)\!\Big/\!\!\sim\,\,\longrightarrow X,\quad\pi\big([x,v_{I},\Pi_{I}(w_{I})]\big)=x,\\
&\hskip 14.45377pt\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I}(V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\ni\theta_{IK}(v)\sim v\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};K}(V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\quad\forall~I,K\subset\!S.\end{split}

(3.17)

The second collection similarly determines a complex vector bundle

\begin{split}&\pi\!:T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\!=\!\bigg(\bigsqcup_{I\subset S}T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V)\!\!\bigg)\!\Big/\!\!\sim\,\,\longrightarrow X,\quad\pi\Big(\!\big[\!(x,v_{I},w)\!\oplus\!\big(x,(c_{i})_{i\in I}\big)\!\big]\!\Big)=x,\\
&\hskip 28.90755ptT_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\ni\vartheta_{IK}(v)\sim v\in T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{K}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{U_{I}^{\circ}\cap U_{K}^{\circ}}\quad\forall~I,K\subset\!S.\end{split}

(3.18)

The smooth section
s_{\mathcal{R}}
of the complex line bundle (
3.17
) given by

s_{\mathcal{R}}(x)=\big[x,v_{I},\Pi_{I}(v_{I})\big]\quad\forall~x\!=\!\Psi_{I}(v_{I})\!\in\!U_{I}^{\circ},~I\!\subset\!S,

(3.19)

satisfies the properties stated in Proposition
1.1
(1)
.
The smooth bundle homomorphism (
1.3
) defined by

\iota_{\mathcal{R}}\Big(\!\big[(x,v_{I},w)\!\oplus\!\big(x,(c_{i})_{i\in I}\big)\!\big]\!\Big)=\textnormal{d}_{v_{I}}\Psi_{I}\!\Big(\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(I)};v_{I}}(w)\!+\!\!\sum_{i\in I}c_{i}v_{i}\!\Big)\quad\forall~x\!=\!\Psi_{I}(v_{I})\!\in\!U_{I}^{\circ},~I\!\subset\!S,

(3.20)

satisfies the properties stated in Theorem
1.2
(1)
(2)
.
By the same reasoning as at the end of Section
2.2
,
the bundles (
3.17
) and (
3.18
) also satisfy
the properties in Proposition
1.1
(2)
and Theorem
1.2
(3)
.
Along with Proposition
1.1
(2)
,
Lemma
3.6
below implies the claim of Proposition
1.1
(3)

for SC symplectic divisors.

Lemma 3.6
.

Suppose
V
and
V^{\prime}
are SC symplectic divisors in a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
so that

V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}\!\subset\!X
is also an SC symplectic divisor
and
V\!\cap\!V^{\prime}
contains no open subspace of
V
.
An
\omega
-regularization

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\equiv\big((\rho_{I;i},\nabla^{(I;i)})_{i\in I},\widetilde{\Psi}_{I}\big)_{I\subset S\sqcup S^{\prime}}

for
V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}
in
X

as in Definition
3.5
(2)
determines

\omega
-regularizations
\mathcal{R}
for
V
in
X
and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
for
V^{\prime}
in
X

and an isomorphism

\psi_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}\!:\big(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};X}(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime};X}(V^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}\big)

(3.21)

natural with respect to the restrictions to the open subsets of
X
.

Proof.

Let
V\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S}\!V_{i}
and
V^{\prime}\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S^{\prime}}\!V_{i}^{\prime}
.
If
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\!=\!(\mathcal{R}_{I})_{I\subset S\sqcup S^{\prime}}
, then

\mathcal{R}\!\equiv\!(\mathcal{R}_{I})_{I\subset S}
is an
\omega
-regularization for
V
in
X

and
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\!\equiv\!(\mathcal{R}_{I})_{I\subset S^{\prime}}
is an
\omega
-regularization for
V^{\prime}
in
X
.
By the assumptions,

\widetilde{V}_{I\sqcup K}\!\equiv\!(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})_{I\sqcup K}=V_{I}\!\cap\!V_{K}^{\prime},\qquad\mathcal{N}_{X}\widetilde{V}_{I\sqcup K}=\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{I\sqcup K}}\!\oplus\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{K}^{\prime}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{I\sqcup K}}\quad\forall~I\!\subset\!S,~K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}.

For
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S^{\prime}
(resp.
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S\!\sqcup\!S^{\prime}
),
we denote by
U_{I}^{\prime\circ}\!\subset\!X
and
\theta^{\prime}_{I^{\prime}I}

(resp.
\widetilde{U}_{I}^{\circ}\!\subset\!X
and
\widetilde{\theta}_{I^{\prime}I}
)
the analogues of the open subsets
U_{I}^{\circ}
and the transition maps
\theta_{I^{\prime}I}

in (
3.14
) for the trivialization
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
).
Thus,

U_{I}^{\circ}=\bigcup_{K\subset S^{\prime}}\!\!\!\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}~~\forall~I\!\subset\!S\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad U_{K}^{\prime\circ}=\bigcup_{I\subset S}\!\!\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}~~\forall~K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}.

For
I\!\subset\!S
and
K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}
, define

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}^{\prime};IK}\!:\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};I\sqcup K}(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I}(V)\big|_{\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime};K}(V^{\prime})\big|_{\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}},

\displaystyle\begin{split}\psi_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}^{\prime};IK}\big(x,v_{I\sqcup K},\Pi_{I}(w_{I\sqcup K})\!\big)&=\Big(x,\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{I\sqcup K;I}(v_{I\sqcup K}),\Pi_{I}\big(\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{I\sqcup K;I}(w_{I\sqcup K})\!\big)\!\!\Big)\\
&\hskip 50.58878pt\!\otimes\!\Big(x,\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{I\sqcup K;K}(v_{I\sqcup K}),\Pi_{K}\big(\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{I\sqcup K;K}(w_{I\sqcup K})\!\big)\!\!\Big).\\
\end{split}

By (
3.11
) for the regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
,

\begin{split}&\big\{\vartheta_{I^{\prime}I}\!\otimes\!\vartheta_{K^{\prime}K}^{\prime}\big\}\!\circ\!\psi_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}^{\prime};IK}\!=\!\psi_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}^{\prime};I^{\prime}K^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\widetilde{\vartheta}_{(I^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime})(I\sqcup K)}\!:\\
&\hskip 36.135pt\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};I\sqcup K}(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})\big|_{\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}\cap\widetilde{U}_{I^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};I^{\prime}}(V)\big|_{\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}\cap\widetilde{U}_{I^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime};K^{\prime}}(V^{\prime})\big|_{\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}\cap\widetilde{U}_{I^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\end{split}

for all
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
and
K^{\prime}\!\subset\!K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}
.
Along with (
3.17
), this implies that
the collection
\{\psi_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}^{\prime};IK}\}

induces a well-defined bundle homomorphism (
3.21
).
∎

3.4
Proof of Theorem
1.2
(4)

We now establish (
1.4
) under the assumption that
V
is an SC symplectic divisor.
The reasoning in the general case is identical; see the end of Section
4.3
.
As noted after Theorem
1.2
, (
1.4
) implies (
1.5
)
if
V
is an SC symplectic divisor.

Suppose
X
is a smooth manifold,
V\!\subset\!X
is a submanifold, and

\psi\!:E\!\longrightarrow\!F
is a homomorphism of complex vector bundles over
X
that vanishes
on a complex subbundle
L^{\prime}\!\subset\!E|_{V}
.
The restriction of
\psi
to an extension of
L^{\prime}
to a complex subbundle
L
of
E

over a neighborhood
U

of
V\!\subset\!X
then determines a section
\psi_{L^{\prime}}
of
the complex vector bundle
L^{*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!F
over
U
that vanishes on
V

and well-defined derivative bundle homomorphisms

\textnormal{D}_{x}\psi_{L^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V|_{x}\longrightarrow L_{x}^{\prime*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!(\textnormal{cok}\,\psi_{x})\quad\hbox{and}\quad\textnormal{D}_{x}\psi_{L^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V|_{x}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\!L_{x}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\textnormal{cok}\,\psi_{x}

(3.22)

for each
x\!\in\!V
; these homomorphisms do not depend on the extension of
L^{\prime}
.
Lemma
3.7
below, proved at the end of this section, is the key topological input
we use to establish (
1.4
).

Lemma 3.7
.

Suppose
X
is a smooth manifold,
V\!\subset\!X
is a closed submanifold of codimension 2
with a complex structure on
\mathcal{N}_{X}Y
,

\psi\!:E\!\longrightarrow\!F
is a homomorphism of complex vector bundles over
X
,
and
L\!\longrightarrow\!X
is a complex vector bundle so that

L|_{V}\!=\!\ker\!\big(\psi\!:E|_{V}\!\longrightarrow\!F|_{V}\big)\subset E|_{V}.

If
\psi
is an isomorphism over
X\!-\!V
,
the first homomorphism in (
3.22
) with
L^{\prime}\!=\!L|_{V}
is
\mathbb{C}
-linear
for every
x\!\in\!V
,
and the second homomorphism (
3.22
) is surjective,
then there exists an isomorphism

\widetilde{\psi}\!:E\oplus\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\longrightarrow F\!\oplus\!L

(3.23)

of complex vector bundles over
X
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
V
and
V^{\prime}\!\equiv\!\bigcup_{i\in S^{\prime}}\!V_{i}^{\prime}
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
, and
\mathcal{R}

be as in Lemma
3.6
.
There exists a unique vector bundle homomorphism

\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\!:T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}X\big(\!-\log(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})\!\big)\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)

(3.24)

so that
\iota_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}}\!\circ\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
.
For every
K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}
,

(1)

the kernel of
\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
over
V_{K}^{\prime\circ}

is a trivial subbundle
L_{K}^{\prime}\!\approx\!V_{K}^{\prime\circ}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{K}
;

(2)

the cokernel of
\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
over
V_{K}^{\prime\circ}

is canonically isomorphic to
\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{K}^{\prime\circ}
;

(3)

the composition of the above isomorphism with
the first homomorphism in (
3.22
) with
\psi\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
and

L^{\prime}\!=\!L_{K}^{\prime}
,

\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{K}^{\prime\circ}|_{x}\xrightarrow{{\textnormal{D}_{x}(\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}})_{L_{K}^{\prime}}}}(L_{K}^{\prime})_{x}^{*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\big(\textnormal{cok}\,\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\big)_{\!x}\xrightarrow{{~\approx~}}(L_{K}^{\prime})_{x}^{*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{K}^{\prime\circ}|_{x}\,,

is
\mathbb{C}
-linear with respect to the complex structures on
its domain and target determined by the regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
for every

x\!\in\!V_{K}^{\prime\circ}
;

(4)

the second homomorphism in (
3.22
) with

\psi\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
and
L^{\prime}\!=\!L_{K}^{\prime}
,

\textnormal{D}_{x}(\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}})_{L_{K}^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{K}^{\prime\circ}|_{x}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\!L_{K}^{\prime}|_{x}\longrightarrow\big(\textnormal{cok}\,\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\big)_{\!x}

is surjective for every
x\!\in\!V_{K}^{\prime\circ}
.

Suppose
J
is an almost complex structure on
X
compatible with
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
(and thus with
\mathcal{R}
).
Since
\iota_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}}\!\circ\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}

and the homomorphisms
\iota_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
and
\iota_{\mathcal{R}}
are
\mathbb{C}
-linear isomorphisms over
X\!-\!V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}
,
the homomorphism (
3.24
) is
\mathbb{C}
-linear with respect
to the complex structures
\mathfrak{i}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}},J}
on the domain and
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R},J}
on the target.
The isomorphism in Lemma
3.8
(2)

is
\mathbb{C}
-linear with respect to the complex structures
on its target and domain determined by
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
.
If
V^{\prime}\!\subset\!X
is a smooth symplectic divisor, the bundle homomorphism

\psi\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
thus satisfies the conditions of Lemma
3.7

with
V
replaced by
V^{\prime}
and
L\!=\!X\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
.
The conclusion of this lemma is the claim of (
1.4
).

Proof of Lemma
3.8
.

We continue with the notation in the proof of Lemma
3.6

and denote by
\widetilde{\vartheta}_{I^{\prime}I}
the analogues of the transition maps
\vartheta_{I^{\prime}I}

in (
3.15
) for the regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
.
For
I\!\subset\!S
and
K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}
, define

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};IK}\!:T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}\big(\!-\log(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})\!\big)\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}},

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};IK}\Big(\!(x,v_{I\sqcup K},w)\!\oplus\!\big(x,(c_{i})_{i\in I\sqcup K}\big)\!\!\Big)\\
&\hskip 36.135pt=\Big(\!x,\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{I\sqcup K;I}(v_{I\sqcup K}),\textnormal{d}_{v_{I\sqcup K;I}}\widetilde{\Psi}_{I\sqcup K;I}\big(\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(I\sqcup K)};v_{I\sqcup K}}(w)\!+\!\sum_{i\in K}c_{i}v_{i}\!\big)\!\!\Big)\!\oplus\!\big(x,(c_{i})_{i\in I}\big).\end{split}

By (
3.11
) for the regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
,

\begin{split}&\vartheta_{I^{\prime}I}\!\circ\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};IK}\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};I^{\prime}K^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\widetilde{\vartheta}_{(I^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime})(I\sqcup K)}\!:T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}\big(\!-\log(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})\!\big)\big|_{\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}\cap\widetilde{U}_{I^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\\
&\hskip 252.94499pt\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I^{\prime}}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}\cap\widetilde{U}_{I^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\end{split}

for all
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S
and
K^{\prime}\!\subset\!K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}
.
Along with (
3.18
), this implies that
the collection
\{\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};IK}\}

induces a well-defined bundle homomorphism (
3.24
).

It is immediate from the definitions and (
3.11
) for the regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}

that
\iota_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}}\!\circ\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
.
If
I\!\subset\!S
,
K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}
, and
x\!\in\!V_{K}^{\prime}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}

with
x\!=\!\widetilde{\Psi}_{I\cup K}(v_{I\sqcup K;K})\!=\!\Psi_{I}(v_{I\sqcup K;K})
, then

\ker(\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};IK})_{x}=\big\{\!(x,v_{I\sqcup K;K},0)\!\big\}\!\oplus\!\big(\{x\}\!\times\!\{0\}^{I}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{K}\big).

The homomorphism

\displaystyle T_{\mathcal{R}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V)\big|_{V_{K}^{\prime}\cap\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{K}^{\prime\circ}\big|_{V_{K}^{\prime}\cap\widetilde{U}_{I\sqcup K}^{\circ}},

\displaystyle(x,v_{I\sqcup K;K},w)\!\oplus\!\big(x,(c_{i})_{i\in I\sqcup K}\big)\longrightarrow\textnormal{d}_{v_{I\sqcup K;K}}\Psi_{I}\!\big(h_{\nabla^{(I)};v_{I\sqcup K;K}}(w)\!\big)\!+\!T_{x}V_{K}^{\prime\circ},

induces an isomorphism
\textnormal{cok}(\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}})_{x}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{K}^{\prime\circ}|_{x}
.
The composition of this isomorphism with
the second homomorphism in (
3.22
) with
\psi\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
and

L^{\prime}\!=\!L_{K}^{\prime}
is given by

\textnormal{D}_{x}(\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}})_{L_{K}^{\prime}}\big(\!(w_{i})_{i\in K}\!\otimes\!\big(x,(c_{i})_{i\in K}\big)\!\big)=\sum_{i\in K}c_{i}w_{i}\,.

In particular, this homomorphism is surjective, as claimed.
∎

Proof of Lemma
3.7
.

Let
\Psi\!:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow\!X
be a regularization for
V
in
X
as in Definition
2.1
and

U\!=\!\Psi(\mathcal{N}^{\prime})
.
Let
\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\!\longrightarrow\!X
be the complex line bundle (
2.5
)
and
E^{\prime}\!\subset\!E|_{V}
be a complex subbundle complementary to
L|_{V}
.
Extend
E^{\prime}
to a subbundle of
E|_{U}
, which we still denote by
E^{\prime}
.
By the assumptions of the lemma,
\psi
is injective on
E^{\prime}
and
\psi(E^{\prime})\!\subset\!F|_{U}

is a complex subbundle.
Let
Q\!\subset\!F|_{U}
be a complex subbundle complementary to
\psi(E^{\prime})
.
After shrinking
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}
if necessary, we can extend the identification of
L|_{V}
with a subbundle
of
E|_{V}
to an identification of
L|_{U}
with a subbundle of
E|_{U}
so that

\psi(L|_{U})\!\subset\!Q
.
Since
\psi
vanishes on
L|_{V}
, the derivative of the associated section of
L^{*}|_{U}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!Q

induces a homomorphism

\textnormal{D}\psi_{L}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V\longrightarrow L^{*}|_{V}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!Q|_{V}\subset(L^{*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!F)\big|_{V}

of real vector bundles over
V
.
By the assumptions on (
3.22
) with
L^{\prime}\!=\!L|_{V}
, this homomorphism is
\mathbb{C}
-linear
and induces an isomorphism

\textnormal{D}\psi_{L}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!L|_{V}\longrightarrow Q|_{V}\subset F|_{V}

of an isomorphism of complex vector bundles over
V
.
Using parallel transport, we identify
L|_{U}
and
Q

with the vector bundles
\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{*}\pi^{*}L
and
\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{*}\pi^{*}Q
, respectively.

Choose a smooth function
\beta\!:X\!\longrightarrow\![0,1]
such that

\beta\!=\!1
on a neighborhood
U^{\prime}\!\subset\!U
of
V
and
\textnormal{supp}\,\beta\!\subset\!U
.
We define (
3.23
) over
X\!-\!\textnormal{supp}\,\beta\!\subset\!X\!-\!V
and
on
E|_{U}\!=\!E^{\prime}\!\oplus\!(L|_{U})
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}(e,w)=\big(\psi(e),w\big)\quad\forall~(e,w)\in\big(E\!\oplus\!\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\big)\big|_{X-\textnormal{supp}\,\beta}\!=\!\big(E\!\oplus\!L\big)\big|_{X-\textnormal{supp}\,\beta},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}(e^{\prime}\!+\!e^{\prime\prime},0)=\big(\psi(e^{\prime})\!+\!\psi(e^{\prime\prime}),-\beta(x)e^{\prime\prime}\big)\quad\forall~e^{\prime}\!\in\!E^{\prime}_{x},~e^{\prime\prime}\!\in\!L_{x},~x\!\in\!U.

We extend this definition to
(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!L)|_{U}
by

\begin{split}\widetilde{\psi}\big(0,(x,v,w)\!\big)&=\big(x,v,\big(\beta(x)D\psi_{L}(v\!\otimes\!w),(1\!-\!\beta(x)\!)w\big)\!\big)\in\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{*}\pi^{*}(Q\!\oplus\!L)\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt\forall~(x,v,w)\!\in\!\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\big)|_{U-V}=\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{*}\pi^{*}L\big|_{U-V},\\
\widetilde{\psi}\big(0,(x,v,w)\!\big)&=\big(x,v,\big(\beta(x)D\psi_{L}(w),0\big)\!\big)\in\{\Psi^{-1}\}^{*}\pi^{*}(Q\!\oplus\!L)\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt\forall~(x,v,w)\!\in\!\big(\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\big)\big|_{U^{\prime}}\!=\!\big\{\Psi^{-1}\big\}^{*}\pi^{*}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V\!\otimes\!L)\big|_{U^{\prime}}.\end{split}

The bundle homomorphism
\widetilde{\psi}
is well-defined and smooth.
It remains to verify that the homomorphism

\widetilde{\psi}\!:\big(L\!\oplus\!\mathcal{O}_{X}(V)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!L\big)|_{U}\longrightarrow Q\!\oplus\!(L|_{U})

(3.25)

is injective over
\textnormal{supp}\,\beta\!\subset\!U
if
\textnormal{supp}\,\beta
is sufficiently small.

Over
U^{\prime}
, the homomorphism (
3.25
) is given by

\begin{split}&\hskip 21.68121pt\widetilde{\psi}\!:\big\{\Psi^{-1}\big\}^{*}\pi^{*}\!\big(L\!\oplus\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V\!\otimes\!L\big)\big|_{U^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\big\{\Psi^{-1}\big\}^{*}\pi^{*}\!(Q\!\oplus\!L),\\
&\widetilde{\psi}\big(x,v,(e^{\prime\prime},v^{\prime}\!\otimes\!w)\!\big)=\big(\psi(x,v,e^{\prime\prime}),0\big)\!+\!\big(x,v,\big(D\psi_{L}(v^{\prime}\!\otimes\!w),-e^{\prime\prime}\big)\!\big),\end{split}

and is thus injective.
Over
\textnormal{supp}\,\beta\!-\!V\!\subset\!U\!-\!V
, (
3.25
) is given by

\begin{split}&\hskip 43.36243pt\widetilde{\psi}\!:\big\{\Psi^{-1}\big\}^{*}\pi^{*}\!\big(L\!\oplus\!L\big)\big|_{\textnormal{supp}\,\beta-V}\longrightarrow\big\{\Psi^{-1}\big\}^{*}\pi^{*}\!(Q\!\oplus\!L),\\
&\widetilde{\psi}\big(x,v,(e^{\prime\prime},w)\!\big)=\big(\psi(x,v,e^{\prime\prime}),0\big)\!+\!\big(x,v,\big(\beta(x)D\psi_{L}(v\!\otimes\!w),-\beta(x)e^{\prime\prime}\!+\!(1\!-\!\beta(x)\!)w\big)\!\big).\end{split}

(3.26)

Choose norms on
L|_{U}
and
Q
.
Let
\mathcal{C}\!:V\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
and

\varepsilon\!:(U,V)\!\longrightarrow\!(\mathbb{R},0)
be smooth functions such that

\begin{split}\mathcal{C}\big(\pi(v)\!\big)\big|\textnormal{D}\psi_{L}(v\!\otimes\!w)\big|&\geq|v||w|,\\
\big|\psi(\Psi(v),v,w)\!-\!(\Psi(v),v,\textnormal{D}\psi_{L}(v\!\otimes\!w)\!)\big|&\leq\varepsilon(v)||v||w|\end{split}\qquad\forall~(v,w)\!\in\!\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\!\oplus\!L|_{V}\,.

If
(x,v,(e^{\prime\prime},w)\!)
with
x\!=\!\Psi(v)\!\in\!\textnormal{supp}\,\beta\!-\!V

lies in the kernel of (
3.26
), then

\displaystyle\beta(x)e^{\prime\prime}=(1\!-\!\beta(x)\!)w,\quad D\psi_{L}\big(v\!\otimes\!(\beta(x)w\!+\!e^{\prime\prime})\!\big)=D\psi_{L}\big(v\!\otimes\!e^{\prime\prime}\big)-\psi(x,v,e^{\prime\prime}),

\displaystyle|v|\big|\beta(x)w\!+\!e^{\prime\prime}\big|\leq\mathcal{C}\big(\pi(v)\!\big)\big|\textnormal{D}\psi_{L}\big(v\!\otimes\!(\beta(x)w\!+\!e^{\prime\prime})\!\big)\!\big|\leq\mathcal{C}\big(\pi(v)\!\big)\varepsilon(v)||v||e^{\prime\prime}|.
