If
\mathcal{C}(\pi(v))\varepsilon(v)\!<\!1
, this implies that
e^{\prime\prime},w\!=\!0
.
Thus, the homomorphism (
3.25
) is injective everywhere over
U

if the support of
\beta
is sufficiently small.
We conclude that
\widetilde{\psi}
is an isomorphism everywhere over
X
.
∎

4
NC symplectic divisors: local perspective

Arbitrary normal crossings (or NC) divisors are spaces that are locally SC divisors.
This local perspective, reviewed below,
makes it straightforward to define NC divisors and regularizations for them.
It is also readily applicable to local statements,
such as Theorem
1.2
(4)
and Lemma
1.3
.

For a set
S
, denote by
\mathcal{P}(S)
the collection of subsets of
S
.
If in addition
i\!\in\!S
, let

\mathcal{P}_{i}(S)=\big\{I\!\in\!\mathcal{P}(S)\!:\,i\!\in\!S\big\}.

4.1
Definitions

We begin by extending the definitions of Section
3.1
to the general NC setting.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
(X,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold.
A subspace
V\!\subset\!X
is an
NC symplectic divisor in
(X,\omega)

if for every
x\!\in\!X
there exist an open neighborhood
U
of
x
in
X
and
a finite transverse collection
\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S}
of closed submanifolds of
U

of codimension 2 such that

V\!\cap\!U=\bigcup_{i\in S}\!V_{i}

is an SC symplectic divisor in
(U,\omega|_{U})
.

Every NC divisor
V\!\subset\!X
is a closed subspace;
its
singular locus

V_{\partial}\!\subset\!V
consists of the points
x\!\in\!V

such that there exists a chart
(U,\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S})
as in Definition
4.1

and
I\!\subset\!S
with
|I|\!=\!2
and
x\!\in\!V_{I}
.
Figure
1
shows an NC divisor
V
, a chart around a singular point of
V
,
and a chart around a smooth point of
V
.

-3,-1)(11,1)

3)5-90180

Figure 1:
A non-SC normal crossings divisor.

For each chart
(U,\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S})
as in Definition
4.1

and each
x\!\in\!U
, let

S_{x}=\big\{i\!\in\!S\!:\,x\!\in\!V_{i}\big\}\,.

The cardinality
|x|\!\equiv\!|S_{x}|
is independent of the choice of a chart around
x
.
For each
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, let

V^{(r)}\equiv\big\{x\!\in\!X\!:|x|\!\geq\!r\big\}.

(4.1)

If
(U^{\prime},\{V_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in S^{\prime}})
is another chart for
V
in
X
and
x\!\in\!U\!\cap\!U^{\prime}
,
there exist a neighborhood
U_{x}
of
x
in
U\!\cap\!U^{\prime}
and a bijection

h_{x}\!:S_{x}\longrightarrow S_{x}^{\prime}\qquad\hbox{s.t.}\quad V_{i}\!\cap\!U_{x}=V_{h_{x}(i)}^{\prime}\!\cap\!U_{x}~~\forall\,i\!\in\!S_{x}\,.

(4.2)

We also denote by
h_{x}
the induced bijection
\mathcal{P}(S_{x})\!\longrightarrow\!\mathcal{P}(S_{x}^{\prime})
.
By (
4.2
),

\mathcal{N}_{V_{I-i}}V_{I}\big|_{V_{I}\cap U_{x}}=\mathcal{N}_{V_{h_{x}(I)-h_{x}(i)}^{\prime}}V_{h_{x}(I)}^{\prime}\big|_{V_{h_{x}(I)}^{\prime}\cap U_{x}}\qquad\forall\,i\!\in\!I\!\subset\!S_{x}.

(4.3)

We denote by
\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(X,V)
the space of all symplectic structures
\omega
on
X

such that
V
is an NC symplectic divisor in
(X,\omega)
.

4.2
Regularizations

Suppose
V\!\subset\!X
is an NC divisor,

(U,\{V_{i}\}_{i\in S})
and
(U^{\prime},\{V_{i}^{\prime}\}_{i\in S^{\prime}})
are charts for
V
in
(X,\omega)
, and

\big(\mathcal{R}_{I}\big)_{I\subset S}\equiv\big((\rho_{I;i},\nabla^{(I;i)})_{i\in I},\Psi_{I}\big)_{I\subset S}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\big(\mathcal{R}_{I}^{\prime}\big)_{I\subset S^{\prime}}\equiv\big((\rho_{I;i}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime(I;i)})_{i\in I},\Psi_{I}^{\prime}\big)_{I\subset S^{\prime}}

are an
\omega|_{U}
-regularization for
\bigcup_{i\in S}V_{i}
in
U

and an
\omega|_{U^{\prime}}
-regularization for
\bigcup_{i\in S^{\prime}}V_{i}^{\prime}
in
U^{\prime}
, respectively.
We define

\big(\mathcal{R}_{I}\big)_{I\subset S}\cong_{X}\big(\mathcal{R}_{I}^{\prime}\big)_{I\subset S^{\prime}}

if for every
x\!\in\!U\!\cap\!U^{\prime}
there exist
U_{x}
and
h_{x}
as in (
4.2
)
such that

\begin{split}&\big(\rho_{I;i},\nabla^{(I;i)}\big)\big|_{V_{I}\cap U_{x}}=\big(\rho_{h(I);h(i)}^{\prime},\nabla^{\prime(h(I);h(i))}\big)\big|_{V_{h(I)}\cap U_{x}}\quad\forall~i\!\in\!I\!\subset\!S_{x}\qquad\hbox{and}\\
&\hskip 28.90755pt\Psi_{I}=\Psi_{I^{\prime}}^{\prime}~~\hbox{on}~~\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{I})|_{V_{I}\cap U_{x}}\cap\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{h(I)}^{\prime})|_{V_{h(I)}\cap U_{x}}\quad\forall~I\!\subset\!S_{x}.\end{split}

(4.4)

Definition 4.2
.

Let
(X,\omega)
be a symplectic manifold,
V\!\subset\!X
be an NC symplectic divisor,
and
(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
be a collection of charts for
V
in
X

as in Definition
4.1
.
An
\omega
-regularization for
V
in
X

(with respect to the atlas
\mathcal{A}
) is a collection

\mathcal{R}\equiv(\mathcal{R}_{y;I})_{y\in\mathcal{A},I\subset S_{y}}\equiv\big((\rho_{y;I;i},\nabla^{(y;I;i)})_{i\in I},\Psi_{y;I}\big)_{y\in\mathcal{A},I\subset S_{y}}

such that
(\mathcal{R}_{y;I})_{I\subset S_{y}}
is an
\omega|_{U_{y}}
-regularization
for
V_{y}
in
U_{y}
as in Definition
3.5
(2)

for each
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}

and

\big(\mathcal{R}_{y;I}\big)_{I\subset S_{y}}\cong_{X}\big(\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime};I}\big)_{I\subset S_{y^{\prime}}}\qquad\forall\,y,y^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{A}\,.

(4.5)

We call an almost complex structure
J
on
X
compatible with a regularization
\mathcal{R}

as in Definition
4.2
if
J|_{U_{y}}
is compatible with the regularization

(\mathcal{R}_{y;I})_{y\in\mathcal{A},I\subset S_{y}}
for
V_{y}
in
U_{y}
for each
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}

as defined at the end of Section
3.2
.
In particular, every open stratum
V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r+1)}
is an almost complex submanifold
of
X
with respect to an
\mathcal{R}
-compatible almost complex structure on
X
.

There are natural notions of equivalence classes of regularizations on the level of germs
and families of such equivalence classes; see
[
14
, Section 4.1]
.
We denote by
\textnormal{Aux}(X,V)
the space of pairs
(\omega,\mathcal{R})
consisting of
\omega\!\in\!\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(X,V)

and the equivalence class of an
\omega
-compatible regularization
\mathcal{R}
for
V
in
X
.
Let
\textnormal{AK}(X,V)
be the space of triples
(\omega,\mathcal{R},J)
consisting of
(\omega,\mathcal{R})\!\in\!\textnormal{Aux}(X,V)

and an almost complex structure
J
on
X
compatible with
\omega
and
\mathcal{R}
.
By
[
15
, Theorem 4.5]
, the map (
2.7
) is again a weak homotopy equivalence
in the present setting.
It remains straightforward to show that
the map (
2.8
) is also a weak homotopy equivalence in the present setting.

4.3
Constructions

In this section, we extend the constructions of Section
3.3

to arbitrary NC divisors in the local perspective.
We then note that the proof of Theorem
1.2
(4)
in Section
3.4

readily extends to the general NC case.

Suppose
V
is an NC symplectic divisor in a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
,

(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
is a collection of charts for
V
in
X

as in Definition
4.1
, and

\mathcal{R}\!\equiv\!(\mathcal{R}_{y})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
is an
\omega
-regularization with respect to the atlas
\mathcal{A}

in the sense of Definition
4.2
.
For
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
and
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S_{y}
,
let
V_{y}\!\equiv\!V\!\cap\!U_{y}
,
U_{y;I}^{\circ}\!\subset\!U_{y}
be as in (
3.12
),

\theta_{y^{\prime};I^{\prime}I}\!=\!\theta_{I^{\prime}I}
be as in (
3.14
), and

\vartheta_{y^{\prime};I^{\prime}I}\!=\!\vartheta_{I^{\prime}I}
be as in (
3.15
).
Let

\pi_{y}\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y})\longrightarrow U_{y}\qquad\textnormal{and}\qquad\pi_{y}\!:T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{y}(-\log V_{y})\longrightarrow U_{y}

be the complex line bundle (
3.17
) and the logarithmic tangent bundle (
3.18
)
determined by the
\omega|_{U_{y}}
-regularization
\mathcal{R}_{y}
for
the NC divisor
V_{y}
for
(U_{y},\omega|_{U_{y}})
.
Let
s_{\mathcal{R};y}
and

\iota_{\mathcal{R};y}\!:T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{y}(-\log V_{y})\longrightarrow TU_{y}

be the associated section of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y})

and the vector bundle homomorphism (
1.3
), respectively.

Suppose
y,y^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
.
For
x\!\in\!U_{y}\!\cap\!U_{y^{\prime}}
, let
U_{yy^{\prime};x}\!\equiv\!U_{x}\!\subset\!U_{y}\!\cap\!U_{y^{\prime}}
and

h_{y^{\prime}y;x}\!\equiv\!h_{x}\!:S_{y;x}\!\equiv\!S_{x}\longrightarrow S_{y^{\prime};x}\!\equiv\!S_{x}

be as in (
4.2
).
By (
4.3
),

\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;S_{y;x}-i}}V_{y;S_{y;x}}\big|_{V_{y;S_{y;x}}\cap U_{yy^{\prime};x}}=\mathcal{N}_{V_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime};x}-h_{y^{\prime}y;x}(i)}}V_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime};x}}\big|_{V_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime};x}}\cap U_{yy^{\prime};x}}\\
\quad\forall~i\!\in\!S_{y;x}.

(4.6)

We can choose
U_{yy^{\prime};x}
sufficiently small so that

U_{yy^{\prime};x}\subset U_{y;S_{y;x}}^{\circ}\!\cap\!U_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime};x}}^{\circ}

(4.7)

and
U_{x}\!\equiv\!U_{yy^{\prime};x}
satisfies (
4.4
).
By (
3.13
), (
4.7
), and (
4.6
),
there are canonical identifications

\begin{split}\theta_{y^{\prime}y;x}&:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};S_{y;x}}(V_{y})|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle=}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y}|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}};S_{y;x}}\big(V_{y}\!\cap\!U_{yy^{\prime};x}\big)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\approx}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}};S_{y^{\prime};x}}(V_{y^{\prime}})|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}},\\
\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y;x}&:T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{S_{y;x}}^{\circ}(-\log V_{y})|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle=}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}}}U_{yy^{\prime};x}\big(\!-\log(V_{y}\!\cap\!U_{yy^{\prime};x})\!\big)\\
&\hskip 231.26378pt\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\approx}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}}U_{S_{y^{\prime};x}}^{\circ}(-\log V_{y^{\prime}})|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}}\,.\end{split}

(4.8)

Suppose
x^{\prime}\!\in\!U_{yy^{\prime};x}
.
By (
4.7
) and the uniqueness of
h_{y^{\prime}y;x^{\prime}}
,

S_{y;x^{\prime}}\subset S_{y;x},\qquad S_{y^{\prime};x^{\prime}}\subset S_{y^{\prime};x},\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad h_{y^{\prime}y;x^{\prime}}=h_{y^{\prime}y;x}|_{S_{y;x^{\prime}}}.

Combining these statements with (
3.14
), (
3.15
), and (
4.4
),
we obtain

\begin{split}&\theta_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime};x^{\prime}}S_{y^{\prime};x}}\!\circ\!\theta_{y^{\prime}y;x}\!=\!\theta_{y^{\prime}y;x^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\theta_{y;S_{y;x^{\prime}}S_{y;x}}\!:\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};S_{y;x}}(V_{y})|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}\cap U_{yy^{\prime};x^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}};S_{y^{\prime};x^{\prime}}}(V_{y^{\prime}})|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}\cap U_{yy^{\prime};x^{\prime}}},\\
&\vartheta_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime};x^{\prime}}S_{y^{\prime};x}}\!\circ\!\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y;x}\!=\!\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y;x^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\vartheta_{y;S_{y;x^{\prime}}S_{y;x}}\!:\\
&\hskip 72.26999ptT_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{S_{y;x}}^{\circ}(-\log V_{y})|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}\cap U_{yy^{\prime};x^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}}U_{S_{y^{\prime};x^{\prime}}}^{\circ}(-\log V_{y^{\prime}})|_{U_{yy^{\prime};x}\cap U_{yy^{\prime};x^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

Along with (
3.17
) and (
3.18
),
this implies that the collections
\{\theta_{y^{\prime}y;x}\}_{x\in U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}
and

\{\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y;x}\}_{x\in U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}
determine bundle isomorphisms

\begin{split}\theta_{y^{\prime}y}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}&\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}};U_{y^{\prime}}}(V_{y^{\prime}})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}},\\
\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y}:T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{y}(-\log V_{y})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}&\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}}U_{y^{\prime}}(-\log V_{y^{\prime}})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}},\end{split}

(4.9)

respectively.

Suppose
y^{\prime\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
is another element and
x\!\in\!U_{y}\!\cap\!U_{y^{\prime}}\!\cap\!U_{y^{\prime\prime}}
.
Let

U_{yy^{\prime}y^{\prime\prime};x}=U_{yy^{\prime};x}\!\cap\!U_{yy^{\prime};x}\!\cap\!U_{y^{\prime}y^{\prime\prime};x}.

By the uniqueness of
h_{y^{\prime\prime}y;x}
,

h_{y^{\prime\prime}y;x}\!=\!h_{y^{\prime\prime}y^{\prime};x}\!\circ\!h_{y^{\prime}y;x}\!:S_{y;x}\longrightarrow S_{y^{\prime\prime};x}\,.

This implies that the identifications (
4.8
) satisfy

\begin{split}\theta_{y^{\prime\prime}y;x}\!=\!\theta_{y^{\prime\prime}y^{\prime};x}\!\circ\!\theta_{y^{\prime}y;x}\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};S_{y;x}}(V_{y})|_{U_{yy^{\prime}y^{\prime\prime};x}}&\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime\prime}};S_{y^{\prime\prime};x}}(V_{y^{\prime\prime}})|_{U_{yy^{\prime}y^{\prime\prime};x}},\\
\vartheta_{y^{\prime\prime}y;x}\!=\!\vartheta_{y^{\prime\prime}y^{\prime};x}\!\circ\!\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y;x}\!:T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{S_{y;x}}^{\circ}(-\log V_{y})|_{U_{yy^{\prime}y^{\prime\prime};x}}&\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{S_{y^{\prime\prime};x}}^{\circ}(-\log V_{y^{\prime\prime}})|_{U_{yy^{\prime}y^{\prime\prime};x}}.\end{split}

Thus, the collections
\{\theta_{y^{\prime}y}\}_{y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}

and
\{\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y}\}_{y,y^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}}
satisfy the cocycle condition.
The first collection thus determines a complex line bundle

\begin{split}&\pi\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V)\!=\!\bigg(\bigsqcup_{y\in\mathcal{A}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y})\!\!\bigg)\!\Big/\!\!\sim\,\,\longrightarrow X,\quad\pi|_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y})}=\pi_{y},\\
&\hskip 14.45377pt\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}\ni v\sim\theta_{y^{\prime}y}(v)\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}};U_{y^{\prime}}}(V_{y^{\prime}})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}\quad\forall~~y,y^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{A}.\end{split}

(4.10)

The second collection similarly determines a vector bundle

\begin{split}&\pi\!:T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\!=\!\bigg(\bigsqcup_{y\in\mathcal{A}}T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{y}(-\log V_{y})\!\!\bigg)\!\Big/\!\!\sim\,\,\longrightarrow X,\quad\pi|_{T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{y}(-\log V_{y})}=\pi_{y},\\
&T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{y}(-\log V_{y})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}\ni v\sim\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y}(v)\in T_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}}U_{y^{\prime}}(-\log V_{y^{\prime}})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}\quad\forall~y,y^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{A}.\end{split}

(4.11)

By (
3.19
), (
3.20
), and (
4.4
),

\begin{split}&~~\theta_{y^{\prime}y}\!\circ\!s_{\mathcal{R};y}\!=\!s_{\mathcal{R};y^{\prime}}\!:U_{y}\!\cap\!U_{y^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}};U_{y^{\prime}}}(V_{y^{\prime}})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}},\\
&\iota_{\mathcal{R};y}\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R};y^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y}\!:T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{y}(-\log V_{y})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow TX\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

The collections
\{s_{\mathcal{R};y}\}_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
and
\{\iota_{\mathcal{R};y}\}_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
thus
determine a section
s_{\mathcal{R}}
of the line bundle (
4.10
) and
a bundle homomorphism
\iota_{\mathcal{R}}
as in (
1.3
).
Since the sections
s_{\mathcal{R};y}
and the homomorphisms
\iota_{\mathcal{R};y}
satisfy
the properties of
s_{\mathcal{R}}
and
\iota_{\mathcal{R}}
stated in
Proposition
1.1
(1)
and Theorem
1.2
(1)
, so do
the just constructed sections
s_{\mathcal{R}}
and
\iota_{\mathcal{R}}
.

Suppose
J
is an
\mathcal{R}
-compatible almost complex structure on
X
.
For each
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
,

J_{y}\!\equiv\!J|_{U_{y}}
is then an
\mathcal{R}_{y}
-compatible almost complex structure on
U_{y}

and determines a complex structure on the vector bundle

T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{I}^{\circ}(-\log V_{y})\longrightarrow U_{I}^{\circ}

for every
I\!\subset\!S_{y}
.
Since
J_{y}\!=\!J_{y^{\prime}}
on
U_{yy^{\prime};x}
, (
4.4
) implies that
the bundle identification
\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y;x}
in (
4.8
) is
\mathbb{C}
-linear.
Thus,
J
determines a complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R};J}
on the vector bundle (
4.11
)
which restricts to the complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};J_{y}}
on the vector bundle

T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}U_{y}(-\log V_{y})\longrightarrow U_{y}

for every
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
.
Since the bundle homomorphism
\iota_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}
is
\mathbb{C}
-linear with respect to
the complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};J_{y}}
on its domain and the complex structure
J_{y}

on its target, we obtain Theorem
1.2
(2)
.

By the same reasoning as at the end of Section
2.2
,
the bundles (
4.10
) and (
4.11
) also satisfy
the properties in Proposition
1.1
(2)
and Theorem
1.2
(3)
.

Suppose
V^{\prime}
is another NC symplectic divisor in
(X,\omega)
so that

V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}\!\subset\!X
is also an NC symplectic divisor
and
V\!\cap\!V^{\prime}
contains no open subspace of
V
.
An atlas for
V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}
in
X
is a collection of the form

(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}\sqcup S_{y}^{\prime}})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
so that

(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
and

(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}^{\prime}})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
are atlases for
V
and
V^{\prime}
,
respectively.
An
\omega
-regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\!\equiv\!(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
for such an atlas for

V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}
restricts to
\omega
-regularizations

\mathcal{R}\equiv(\mathcal{R}_{y})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\equiv(\mathcal{R}_{y}^{\prime})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}

for the associated atlases for
V
and
V^{\prime}
so that
the
\omega|_{U_{y}}
-regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}
for
(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})\!\cap\!U_{y}
in
U_{y}

restricts to
the
\omega|_{U_{y}}
-regularizations
\mathcal{R}_{y}
for
V\!\cap\!U_{y}
in
U_{y}

and
\mathcal{R}_{y}^{\prime}
for
V^{\prime}\!\cap\!U_{y}
.
We denote by
\theta_{y^{\prime}y}^{\prime}
and
\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y}^{\prime}
(resp.
\widetilde{\theta}_{y^{\prime}y}
and
\widetilde{\vartheta}_{y^{\prime}y}
)
the analogues of the transition maps
\theta_{y^{\prime}y}
and
\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y}
in (
4.9
)
for the regularization
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
(resp.
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
).
For each
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
, let

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathcal{R}_{y}\mathcal{R}_{y}^{\prime}}\!:\big(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y}\!\cup\!V_{y}^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y}),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y}^{\prime};U_{y}}(V_{y}^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}_{y}^{\prime}}\big),

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathcal{R}_{y}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}}\!:T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}}U_{y}\big(\!-\log(V_{y}\!\cup\!V_{y}^{\prime})\!\big)\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}_{y}}X(-\log V_{y})

be the bundle isomorphism (
3.21
) and
the bundle homomorphism (
3.24
) determined by the regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}
.

Since the maps (
3.21
) and (
3.24
)
are natural with respect to the restrictions to open subsets,

\begin{split}&\big\{\theta_{y^{\prime}y}\!\otimes\!\theta_{y^{\prime}y}^{\prime}\big\}\!\circ\!\psi_{\mathcal{R}_{y}\mathcal{R}_{y}^{\prime}}\!=\!\psi_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\widetilde{\theta}_{y^{\prime}y}\!:\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y};U_{y}}(V_{y}\!\cup\!V_{y}^{\prime})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}};U_{y^{\prime}}}(V_{y^{\prime}})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}\!\otimes\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime};U_{y^{\prime}}}(V_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}},\\
&\vartheta_{y^{\prime}y}\!\circ\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}_{y}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}}\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y^{\prime}}}\!\circ\!\widetilde{\vartheta}_{y^{\prime}y}\!:T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}}U_{y}\big(\!-\log(V_{y}\!\cup\!V_{y}^{\prime})\!\big)\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}_{y^{\prime}}}U_{y^{\prime}}(-\log V_{y^{\prime}})\big|_{U_{y}\cap U_{y^{\prime}}}.\end{split}

The collection
\{\psi_{\mathcal{R}_{y}\mathcal{R}_{y}^{\prime}}\}_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
thus determines an isomorphism

\psi_{\mathcal{R}\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}\!:\big(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};X}(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}}\big)\!\otimes\!\big(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime};X}(V^{\prime}),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}\big)

of complex line bundles over
X
.
In light of Proposition
1.1
(2)
,
this establishes Proposition
1.1
(3)
.

The collection
\{\iota_{\mathcal{R}_{y}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}}\}_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
similarly determines
a homomorphism

\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\!:T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}X\big(\!-\log(V\!\cup\!V^{\prime})\!\big)\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)

of vector bundles over
X
.
By the properties of the homomorphisms
\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\!\equiv\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}_{y}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y}}

stated in Lemma
3.8
,
this homomorphism satisfies the same properties with

K\!\subset\!S^{\prime}
,
V_{K}^{\prime\circ}
, and
V_{K}^{\prime\circ}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{K}

replaced by
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
,
V^{\prime(r)}\!-\!V^{\prime(r+1)}
, and
some rank
r
complex vector bundle over
V^{\prime(r)}\!-\!V^{\prime(r+1)}
, respectively.
By the same reasoning as in the paragraph after Lemma
3.8
,

\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
is
\mathbb{C}
-linear with respect to the complex structures
\mathfrak{i}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}},J}

on its domain and
\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R},J}
on its target determined by an
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
-compatible almost
complex structure
J
on
X
.
Furthermore, the
\mathbb{C}
-linear homomorphism
\psi\!=\!\iota_{\mathcal{R}\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}
satisfies the conditions
of Lemma
3.7
if
V^{\prime}
is smooth
(in which case
V^{\prime(1)}\!-\!V^{\prime(2)}\!=\!V^{\prime}
).
In light of Theorem
1.2
(3)
,
this establishes Theorem
1.2
(4)
.

5
Almost complex and symplectic blowups

Let
X
be a smooth manifold and
V\!\subset\!X
be an
NC smooth divisor
,
i.e. a subspace admitting a collection
(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
of charts
as in Definition
4.1
with each
V_{y;i}\!\subset\!V\!\cap\!\!U_{y}

being a smooth submanifold of
U_{y}
of real codimension 2.
We fix such a collection.
Let
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
be such that
V^{(r+1)}\!=\!\emptyset
.
We also fix a
Hermitian regularization

\mathcal{R}\equiv(\mathcal{R}_{y})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}\equiv\big(\!(\rho_{y;I;i},\nabla^{(y;I;i)})_{i\in I},\Psi_{y;I}\big)_{y\in\mathcal{A},I\subset S_{y}}

(5.1)

for
V
in
X
, i.e. a tuple satisfying the conditions of
Definitions
3.5
(2)
and
4.2

that do not involve the symplectic form
\omega
.

By shrinking the open sets
U_{y}
, we may assume that
|S_{y}|\!\leq\!r
for all
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
,

\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{y;S_{y}})\!=\!U_{y}
if
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
with
|S_{y}|\!=\!r
,

\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{y;S_{y}})\!\cap\!\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime}}})\subset\Psi_{y;S_{y}}\big(\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{y;S_{y}})|_{V_{y;S_{y}}\cap V_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime}}}}\big)\!\cup\!\Psi_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime}}}\big(\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime}}})|_{V_{y;S_{y}}\cap V_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime}}}}\big),

and there exists an open neighborhood
U_{r}^{\prime}
of
V^{(r)}\!\subset\!X
so that

U_{r}^{\prime}\!\cap\!U_{y^{\prime}}\!=\!\emptyset
for all
y^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{A}
with
|S_{y^{\prime}}|\!<\!r
.
Let

\mathcal{A}_{r}=\big\{y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}\!:|S_{y}|\!=\!r\big\},\qquad\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}=(\mathcal{A}\!-\!\mathcal{A}_{r})\!\sqcup\!\big\{\!(y,i)\!:y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r},~i\!\in\!S_{y}\big\}.

5.1
Smooth complex blowup

Since
V^{(r+1)}\!=\!\emptyset
,
V^{(r)}\!\subset\!X
is a smooth submanifold.
Let

\pi_{r}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\longrightarrow V^{(r)}

(5.2)

be its normal bundle.
By (
4.5
), the Hermitian metrics
\rho_{y;S_{y};i}
and
the connections
\nabla^{(y;S_{y};i)}
in the complex line bundles
\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;S_{y}-i}}V_{y;S_{y}}

with
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
and
i\!\in\!S_{y}
determine
a complex structure, a Hermitian metric
\rho_{r}
, and
a compatible connection
\nabla^{(r)}
on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
.
Furthermore, the map

\Psi_{r}\!:\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}\!\equiv\!\bigcup_{y\in\mathcal{A}_{r}}\!\!\!\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{y;S_{y}})\longrightarrow X,\qquad\Psi_{r}(v)=\Psi_{y;S_{y}}(v)~~\forall\,v\!\in\!\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{y;S_{y}}),\,y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r},

(5.3)

is a well-defined regularization for
V^{(r)}
in
X
in the sense of
Definition
2.1
.
We note that the complex vector bundle
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
does not necessarily split
as a sum of complex line bundles.

We denote by
\mathbb{E}\!\equiv\!\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})
the complex projectivization of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}

and by

\widetilde{\pi}_{0}\!:\gamma\equiv\big\{\!(\ell,v)\!\in\!\mathbb{E}\!\times\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!:v\!\in\!\ell\big\}\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}

(5.4)

the complex tautological line bundle.
Let

\pi\!:\mathbb{E}\longrightarrow V^{(r)},\qquad\pi(\ell)=\pi_{r}(v)~~\hbox{if}~(\ell,v)\!\in\!\gamma,

(5.5)

be the bundle projection.
The connection
\nabla^{(r)}
and the Hermitian metric
\rho_{r}
on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
determine
a splitting

\begin{split}T(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}-V^{(r)}}\approx\pi_{r}^{*}TV^{(r)}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}-V^{(r)}}\!\oplus\!\big\{\!(v,w)\!\in\!\pi_{r}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}-V^{(r)}}\!:w\!\in\!\mathbb{C}v\big\}&\\
\!\oplus\!\big\{\!(v,w)\!\in\!\pi_{r}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}-V^{(r)}}\!:\rho_{r}(v,w)\!=\!0\big\}&\end{split}

(5.6)

of the vector bundle
T(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}-V^{(r)}}

so that the middle summand above is identified with
the tangent bundle to the orbits of the
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-action on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}

and the last summand is its complement in the vertical tangent bundle of
\pi_{r}

restricted to
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r)}
.
By
[
38
, Lemma 1.1]
, the above splitting is
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-invariant.
It thus induces a splitting

T\mathbb{E}\approx\pi^{*}TV^{(r)}\!\oplus\!\big(\!\ker\textnormal{d}\pi\big)

(5.7)

so that the last summand above corresponds to the vertical tangent subbundle of
\pi

and a complex structure on the last summand.

Since
\gamma\!\subset\!\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
, the Hermitian metric
\rho_{r}
and
the compatible connection
\nabla^{(r)}
on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
determine
a Hermitian structure
(\widetilde{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)})
on the complex line bundle
\gamma
and
a splitting

T\gamma\approx\widetilde{\pi}_{0}^{*}T\mathbb{E}\!\oplus\!\widetilde{\pi}_{0}^{*}\gamma

(5.8)

so that the last summand above corresponds to the vertical tangent bundle of
\widetilde{\pi}_{0}
.
The composition of this splitting with the splitting (
5.7
) restricts to
the splitting (
5.6
) under the identification

\big(\gamma\!-\!\mathbb{E},\pi\!\circ\!\widetilde{\pi}_{0}|_{\gamma-\mathbb{E}}\big)=\big(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r)},\pi_{r}|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}-V^{(r)}}\big),\qquad(\ell,v)\longrightarrow v.

(5.9)

We define the smooth complex blowup
\pi\!:\widetilde{X}\!\longrightarrow\!X

of
X
along
V^{(r)}
with respect to
\Psi_{r}
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}=\big\{\!(\ell,v)\!\in\!\gamma\!:v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}\big\},~~\widetilde{X}\equiv\big(\!(X\!-\!V^{(r)})\!\sqcup\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}\big)\!\big/\!\!\sim,~~\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}\!-\!\mathbb{E}\ni(\ell,v)\sim\Psi_{r}(v)\in X\!-\!V^{(r)},

\displaystyle\pi([\widetilde{x}])=\begin{cases}\widetilde{x},&\hbox{if}~\widetilde{x}\!\in\!X\!-\!V^{(r)};\\
\Psi_{r}(v),&\hbox{if}~\widetilde{x}\!\equiv\!(\ell,v)\!\in\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}.\end{cases}

The exceptional divisor
\mathbb{E}
is a codimension 2 submanifold of
\widetilde{X}

with a smooth regularization

\widetilde{\Psi}_{0}\!:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\widetilde{X},\qquad\widetilde{\Psi}_{0}(v)=[v].

5.2
Almost complex blowup

Suppose now that
J
is an almost complex structure on
X
,

V\!\subset\!X
is an NC almost complex divisor,
each
V_{y;i}\!\subset\!V\!\cap\!\!U_{y}
is
an almost complex submanifold of
(U_{y},J|_{U_{y}})
of real codimension 2,
and the almost complex structure
J
on
X
is
\mathcal{R}
-compatible in
the sense defined at the end of Section
4.2
.
The smooth submanifold
V^{(r)}\!\subset\!X
is then almost complex.
The induced complex structure on its normal bundle agrees with the fiberwise complex structure
determined by the complex line bundles
\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;S_{y}-i}}V_{y;S_{y}}

with
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
and
i\!\in\!S_{y}
.

Along with the fiberwise complex structure and the connection
\nabla^{(r)}
on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
,

J|_{V^{(r)}}
determines a complex structure
J_{\mathcal{R};r}
on the total space
of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
such that

J\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\Psi_{r}=\textnormal{d}\Psi_{r}\!\circ\!J_{\mathcal{R};r}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}}\,.

(5.10)

Along with the splitting (
5.7
),

J|_{V^{(r)}}
determines an almost complex structure
\widetilde{J}_{\mathbb{E}}
on
\mathbb{E}
so that
the bundle projection (
5.5
) is
(J,\widetilde{J}_{\mathbb{E}})
-holomorphic.
Along with the splitting (
5.8
),
\widetilde{J}_{\mathbb{E}}
in turn determines
an almost complex structure
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R};0}
on the total space of
\gamma
.
By the sentence containing (
5.9
),
the restrictions of the almost complex structures
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R};0}
to
\gamma\!-\!\mathbb{E}

and
J_{\mathcal{R};r}
to
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r)}
agree under the identification (
5.9
).
Furthermore, the projection

\pi_{2}\!:\gamma\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}

to the second component in (
5.4
) is
(J_{\mathcal{R};r},\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R};0})
-holomorphic.

We define an almost complex structure
\widetilde{J}
on the blowup
\widetilde{X}
of
X
constructed
in Section
5.1
by

\widetilde{J}_{[\widetilde{x}]}=\begin{cases}J_{\widetilde{x}},&\hbox{if}~\widetilde{x}\!\in\!X\!-\!V^{(r)};\\
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R};0}|_{\widetilde{x}},&\hbox{if}~\widetilde{x}\!\in\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}.\end{cases}

By the conclusion of the previous paragraph and (
5.10
),
the definitions of
\widetilde{J}
agree on
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}\!-\!\mathbb{E}
.
The exceptional divisor
\mathbb{E}
is an almost complex submanifold of
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{J})
.
The almost complex structure
\widetilde{J}

is compatible with the Hermitian regularization

(\widetilde{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)},\widetilde{\Psi}_{0})
for
\mathbb{E}
in
\widetilde{X}
.

Let
\overline{V}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}
be the proper transform of
V
,
i.e. the closure of
V\!-\!V^{(r)}
, and

\widetilde{V}=\mathbb{E}\!\cup\!\overline{V}.

We show below that
\widetilde{V}

is an NC almost complex divisor in
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{J})
with a collection

(\widetilde{U}_{y},\{\widetilde{V}_{y;i}\}_{i\in\widetilde{S}_{y}})_{y\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}
of charts and a regularization

\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}\equiv(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y;I})_{y\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}},I\subset\widetilde{S}_{y}}\equiv\big(\!(\widetilde{\rho}_{y;I;i},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(y;I;i)})_{i\in I},\widetilde{\Psi}_{y;I}\big)_{y\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}},I\subset\widetilde{S}_{y}}

(5.11)

obtained from the atlas
(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}})_{y\in\mathcal{A}}
and the regularization
\mathcal{R}

for
V
in
X
.
If
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}\!-\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
, then

U_{y}\subset X\!-\!V^{(r)}=\widetilde{X}\!-\!\mathbb{E}.

In this case, we simply take

\widetilde{S}_{y}=S_{y},\quad\big(\widetilde{U}_{y},\{\widetilde{V}_{y;i}\}_{i\in\widetilde{S}_{y}}\big)=(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}}\big),\quad\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}_{y;I}\big)_{I\subset\widetilde{S}_{y}}=\big(\mathcal{R}_{y;I}\big)_{I\subset S_{y}}.

Suppose
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
.
Let
\widetilde{U}_{y}\!\equiv\!\pi^{-1}(U_{y})
be the blowup of
U_{y}
along
V^{(r)}\!\cap\!U_{y}
.
Since
\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{y;S_{y}})\!=\!U_{y}
, we can identify
U_{y}
with
\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{y;S_{y}})
via
\Psi_{r}

and
\widetilde{U}_{y}
with
\pi^{-1}(U_{y})\!\subset\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}
via
\widetilde{\Psi}_{0}
.
For each
i\!\in\!S_{y}
, let
\overline{V}_{y;i}\!\subset\!\widetilde{U}_{y}
be the proper transform of
V_{y;i}

and define

\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}=\{0\}\!\sqcup\!\big(S_{y}\!-\!\{i\}\!\big),~~\mathcal{N}_{y;i}=\bigoplus_{j\in S_{y}-i}\!\!\!\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;S_{y}-i}}V_{y;S_{y}},~~\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}=\widetilde{U}_{y}\!-\!\gamma|_{\mathbb{P}\mathcal{N}_{y;i}},~~\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);0}=\mathbb{E}\!-\!\mathbb{P}\mathcal{N}_{y;i}.

For
j\!\in\!S_{y}\!-\!i
, let
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);j}=\overline{V}_{y;j}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}
.
We note that

V_{y;i}=\mathcal{N}_{y;i}\!\cap\!\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{y;S_{y}}),\qquad\overline{V}_{y;i}=\gamma\big|_{\mathbb{P}\mathcal{N}_{y;i}}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{y},\quad\hbox{and}\quad\overline{V}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}=\bigcup_{j\in S_{y}-i}\!\!\!\!\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);j}

(5.12)

under the above identifications.
Since
\{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);j}\}_{j\in\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}}
is a transverse collection of
codimension 2 almost complex submanifolds of
\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}
,

\widetilde{V}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}
is an SC almost complex divisor in
\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}
.
Thus,
\widetilde{V}
is an NC almost complex divisor in
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{J})
and

(\widetilde{U}_{y},\{\widetilde{V}_{y;i}\}_{i\in\widetilde{S}_{y}})_{y\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}
is an atlas of local charts
for
\widetilde{V}
.

5.3
Regularizations

For
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
and
I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!S_{y}
, let

\mathcal{N}_{y;I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{N}_{y;I;I^{\prime}}\subset\mathcal{N}_{U_{y}}V_{y;I}\!=\!\bigoplus_{i\in I}\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;I-i}}V_{y;I}

be the analogues of the subspaces
\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}^{\prime}\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}_{I;I^{\prime}}\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V_{I}

for the regularization
(\Psi_{y;I})_{I\subset S_{y}}

of
\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}}
in
U_{y}
as defined in Section
3.2
.
Suppose
i\!\in\!S_{y}
and
I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
.
If
0\!\in\!I
(and so
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\!\subset\!\mathbb{E}
), then

\begin{split}\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}}\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}=&\gamma\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}\!\oplus\!\big(\gamma^{*}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{U_{y}}V_{y;I-0}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}\big)\\
\subset&\big\{\!\pi|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}\!\big\}^{\!*}\mathcal{N}_{y;S_{y};I-0}\!\oplus\!\big(\gamma^{*}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\big\{\!\pi|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}\!\big\}^{\!*}\mathcal{N}_{y;S_{y};S_{y}-I}\big)\,.\end{split}

In this case, we define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}\!:\big\{\!\big(\mathbb{C}v^{\prime},v,u\!\otimes\!w\big)\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}}\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\!:v\!+\!u(v)w\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{y;S_{y}}^{\prime}\!\big\}\longrightarrow\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}\subset\big\{\!\pi|_{\mathbb{E}}\!\big\}^{\!*}\mathcal{N}_{U_{y}}V_{y;S_{y}},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}\big(\mathbb{C}v^{\prime},v,u\!\otimes\!w\big)=\big(\mathbb{C}(v^{\prime}\!+\!u(v^{\prime})w),v\!+\!u(v)w\big).

Suppose
0\!\not\in\!I
.
Thus,

\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I}\!\equiv\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\!\cap\!\mathbb{E}
is a smooth submanifold
of
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}
with normal bundle
\gamma|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I}}
.
The smooth regularization

\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I;I}\!:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I;I}^{\prime}\!\equiv\!\big\{\!(\ell,v)\!\in\!\gamma\!:v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{y;S_{y};I}^{\prime}\big\}\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I},\quad\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I;I}(\ell,v)=(\ell,v),

of
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I}
in
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}
is surjective.
Let

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};(y,i);I}(\mathbb{E})\equiv\{\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I;I}^{~-1}\}^{\!*}\big\{\!\widetilde{\pi}_{0}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I;I}^{\prime}}\big\}^{\!*}\gamma\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}

be the analogue of the complex line bundle (
2.5
)
determined by
\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I;I}
and the fiberwise complex structure of
\gamma
.
In this case,

\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}}\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}=\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};(y,i);I}(\mathbb{E})^{*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{U_{y}}V_{y;I}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}=\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};(y,i);I}(\mathbb{E})^{*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{y;S_{y};S_{y}-I}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}.

We define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}\!:\big\{\!\big(x,(\mathbb{C}v^{\prime},v),u\big)\!\otimes\!w\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}}\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\!:v\!+\!u(v)w\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{y;S_{y}}^{\prime}\!\big\}\longrightarrow\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)},

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}\big(\!(x,(\mathbb{C}v^{\prime},v),u)\!\otimes\!w\big)=\big(\mathbb{C}(v^{\prime}\!+\!u(v^{\prime})w),v\!+\!u(v)w\big).

Since
(\Psi_{y;I})_{I\subset S_{y}}
is a regularization
for
\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}}
in
U_{y}
,

(\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I})_{I\subset\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}}
is a regularization
for
\{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);j}\}_{j\in\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}}
in
\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}
.
Since the collection
(\Psi_{y;I})_{y\in\mathcal{A},I\subset S_{y}}
satisfies the last condition
in (
4.4
),
so does the collection
(\widetilde{\Psi}_{y;I})_{y\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}},I\subset\widetilde{S}_{y}}
.

Let
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
as before.
The Hermitian structures
(\widetilde{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)})
on
\gamma
and

(\rho_{y;I;j},\nabla^{(y;I;j)})
on
\mathcal{N}_{V_{y};I-j}V_{y;I}
with
j\!\in\!I\!\subset\!S_{y}

determine Hermitian structures
(\widehat{\rho}_{(y,i);I;j},\widehat{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;j)})

on the complex line bundles

\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I-j}}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}=\begin{cases}\gamma|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}},&\hbox{if}~j\!=\!0\!\in\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)};\\
\gamma^{*}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;S_{y}-j}}V_{y;S_{y}}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}},&\hbox{if}~0,j\!\in\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)},\,j\!\neq\!0;\\
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}};(y,i);I}(\mathbb{E})^{*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{V_{y};I-j}V_{y;I}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}},&\hbox{if}~j\!\in\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)},\,0\!\not\in\!I.\end{cases}

The almost complex structure
\widetilde{J}|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}
and
the connection
\widehat{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;j)}

determine an almost complex structure
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}

on the total space of the normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}

of
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}
in
\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}
.
If
0\!\not\in\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
, Corollary
2.3
implies that
the isomorphism

\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}-\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{U_{y}}V_{y;I}\big|_{V_{y;I}-V_{y;S_{y}}},\quad u\!\otimes\!(v,w)\longrightarrow u(v)w,

(5.13)

intertwines
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}
with the almost complex structure
J_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}

determined by the almost complex structure
J|_{V_{y;I}}
and
the connections
\nabla^{(y;I;j)}
with
j\!\in\!I
.
Since the regularization
\Psi_{y;I}
intertwines
J_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}
and
J
,
it follows that the regularization
\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}
intertwines
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}
and
\widetilde{J}

if
0\!\not\in\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
.
The same is the case for
I\!=\!\{0\}
by the definition of
\widetilde{J}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}}
.
Since the differentials
\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I;I-0}
with

0\!\in\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
are product Hermitian isomorphisms,
it follows that they intertwine the almost complex structures
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}

and
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I-0}
.
Thus, the regularization
\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}
intertwines
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}
and
\widetilde{J}

for all
I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
, while
\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I;I^{\prime}}

intertwines
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}
and
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I^{\prime}}
for all

I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
.

Below we define smooth functions
f_{(y,i);I}\!:\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}

so that the metrics

\widetilde{\rho}_{(y,i);I;j}\equiv\begin{cases}f_{(y,i);I}\widehat{\rho}_{(y,i);I},&\hbox{if}~j\!\in\!I,~j\!\neq\!0;\\
(1/f_{(y,i);I})\widehat{\rho}_{(y,i);I},&\hbox{if}~j\!=\!0\!\in\!I;\end{cases}

(5.14)

on the complex line bundles
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I-j}}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}

are preserved by the isomorphisms
\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I;I^{\prime}}
with

j\!\in\!I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
, after shrinking the domain
of
\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}
.
The connection

\widetilde{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;j)}\equiv\begin{cases}\widehat{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;j)}\!+\!f_{(y,i);I}^{-1}\partial_{\widetilde{J}}f_{(y,i);I},&\hbox{if}~j\!\in\!I,~j\!\neq\!0;\\
\widehat{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;j)}\!-\!f_{(y,i);I}^{-1}\partial_{\widetilde{J}}f_{(y,i);I},&\hbox{if}~j\!=\!0\!\in\!I;\end{cases}

(5.15)

on
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I-j}}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}
is compatible with
\widetilde{\rho}_{(y,i);I;j}
.
Along with
\widetilde{J}|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}
, it determines
the same almost complex structure
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}
on
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I-j}}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}

as
\widehat{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;j)}

(because the two connections differ by a
(1,0)
-form).
Since the isomorphisms
\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I;I^{\prime}}
preserve the metrics
\widetilde{\rho}_{(y,i);I;j}

and the almost complex structures
\widetilde{J}_{\mathcal{R}_{y};I}
, it follows that they preserve
the connections
\widetilde{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;j)}
as well.

We choose the functions
f_{(y,i);I}
so that

\displaystyle f_{(y,i);I}(x)=f_{(y,i^{\prime});I}(x)\quad\forall~x\!\in\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\!\cap\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i^{\prime});I},\,I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)},\widetilde{S}_{(y,i^{\prime})},\,i,i^{\prime}\!\in\!S_{y},\,y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r},

(5.16)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f_{(y,i);I}(x)=f_{(y^{\prime},h_{y^{\prime}y;x}(i));h_{y^{\prime}y;x}(I)}(x)~~\forall~x\!\in\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\!\cap\!\widetilde{V}_{(y^{\prime},h_{y^{\prime}y;x}(i));h_{y^{\prime}y;x}(I)},\,i\!\in\!S_{y},\,y,y^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r},\,\end{split}

(5.17)

with
h_{y^{\prime}y;x}
as above (
4.6
).
For
I\!\not\ni\!0
, we choose
f_{(y,i);I}
so that the isomorphism (
5.13
)
identifies the restriction of
\widetilde{\rho}_{(y,i);I;j}
to

\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I-j}}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}-U_{r}^{\prime}}

with the restriction of the metric
\rho_{y;I;j}
to
\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;I-j}}V_{y;I}|_{V_{y;I}-U_{r}^{\prime}}
.
Along with the assumption on
U_{r}^{\prime}
,
this implies that the resulting collection
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
in (
5.11
)
satisfies the first condition in (
4.4
).

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
.
There exist a smooth function
h\!:\mathbb{P}^{r-1}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
\delta\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{+}

such that

(1)

h
is invariant under the permutations of the homogeneous coordinates on
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}

and under the standard
(S^{1})^{r}
-action on
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
;

(2)

for all
s\!\in\![r]
,
[Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{s}]\!\in\!\mathbb{P}^{s-1}
,
and
[Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}]\!\in\!\mathbb{P}^{r-1}

with
\sum\limits_{i=s+1}^{r}\!\!\!\!|Z_{i}|^{2}\!\leq\!\delta\sum\limits_{i=1}^{s}\!|Z_{i}|^{2}
,

\frac{h(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r})}{h(Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{s},0,\ldots,0)}=\frac{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r}|^{2}}{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{s}|^{2}}.

(5.18)

This lemma is established in Section
5.6
.
Let
h\!:\mathbb{P}^{r-1}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
\delta\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
be
as in Lemma
5.1
.
Define

W_{y;I}=\Big\{\!\big[(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}\big]\!\in\!\mathbb{P}\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}|_{V_{y;S_{y}}}\!:\sum_{i\in S_{y}-I}\!\!\!\rho_{y;S_{y};i}(v_{i})\!<\!\delta\sum_{i\in I}\rho_{y;S_{y};i}(v_{i})\Big\}~~\forall~I\!\subset\!S_{y},\,y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}.

We can identify each fiber of (
5.2
) with
\mathbb{C}^{r}

respecting the splittings and the metrics.
This induces an identification of each fiber of
\pi\!:\mathbb{E}\!\longrightarrow\!V^{(r)}

with
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
.
By the invariance properties of
h
, the composition of this identification with
h

is independent of the choice of the former.
Thus, we obtain a smooth function
h_{\mathbb{E}}\!:\mathbb{E}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
so that

\frac{h_{\mathbb{E}}\big([(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}]\big)}{h_{\mathbb{E}}\big([(v_{i})_{i\in I}]\big)}=\frac{\rho_{r}\big([(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}]\big)}{\rho_{r}\big([(v_{i})_{i\in I}]\big)}\quad\forall~[(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}]\!\in\!W_{y;I},\,I\!\subset\!S_{y},\,y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}.

(5.19)

For
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
,
i\!\in\!S_{y}
, and
I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
so that
0\!\in\!I
,
let
U_{y;I}\!\subset\!\widetilde{U}_{y}
be an open neighborhood of
\widetilde{V}_{y;I-0}

so that
U_{y;I}\!\cap\!\mathbb{E}\!=\!W_{y;I-0}
.
We define

f_{(y,i);I}=h_{\mathbb{E}}|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}}.

(5.20)

By (
5.19
),
the restrictions of the differentials
\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I;I^{\prime}}
with

0\!\in\!I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
to
\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}^{-1}(U_{y;I})

preserve the metrics (
5.14
).
By (
5.20
), the functions
f_{(y,i);I}

satisfy (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) whenever
0\!\in\!I
.

Let
\beta\!:\mathbb{R}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0}
and
\varepsilon\!:V^{(r)}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
be smooth functions so that

\displaystyle\beta(t)=\begin{cases}1,&\hbox{if}~t\!\leq\!1;\\
0,&\hbox{if}~t\!\geq\!2;\end{cases}\qquad\hbox{and}

(5.21)

\displaystyle\bigcup_{y\in\mathcal{A}_{r}}\!\!\!\big\{\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}|_{V_{y;S_{y}}}\!:\rho_{y;S_{y};i}(v)\!<\!2\varepsilon\big(\pi_{r}(\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}})\!\big)~\forall\,i\!\in\!S_{y}\big\}\subset U_{r}^{\prime}\subset U_{r}\!=\!\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}.

Define

\displaystyle U_{r}^{\prime\prime}=\bigcup_{y\in\mathcal{A}_{r}}\!\!\!\big\{\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}|_{V_{y;S_{y}}}\!:\rho_{y;S_{y};i}(v_{i})\!<\!\varepsilon\big(\pi_{r}(\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}})\!\big)~\forall\,i\!\in\!S_{y}\big\}\subset U_{r}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime},

\displaystyle U_{y;I}=\big\{\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}|_{V_{y;S_{y}}}\!:\rho_{y;S_{y};i}(v_{i})\!<\!\varepsilon\big(\pi_{r}(\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}})\!\big)~\forall\,i\!\in\!I\big\}~~\forall~I\!\subset\!S_{y},\,y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r};

\displaystyle\beta_{y}\!:\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}|_{V_{y;S_{y}}}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0},\quad\beta_{y}\big(\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}\big)=\prod_{j\in S_{y}}\!\!\beta\big(\rho_{y;S_{y};j}(v_{j})/\varepsilon\big(\pi_{r}(\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}})\!\big)\!\big)\quad\forall\,y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}.

By the first condition in (
4.4
),
\beta_{y}\!=\!\beta_{y^{\prime}}
on

\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}|_{V_{y;S_{y}}}\!\cap\!\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}|_{V_{y^{\prime};S_{y^{\prime}}}}
for all
y,y^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
.
Thus, the function

\beta_{r}\!:\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0},\qquad\beta_{r}(v)=\beta_{y}(v)\quad\forall~v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}|_{V_{y;S_{y}}},\,y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r},

is well-defined and smooth.
It satisfies

\beta_{r}|_{U_{r}^{\prime\prime}}=1,\quad\beta_{r}|_{\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}-U_{r}^{\prime}}=0,\quad\beta_{r}\big(\!(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}\big)\!=\!\beta_{r}\big(\!(v_{i})_{i\in I}\big)~~\forall~[(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}]\!\in\!U_{y;I},\,I\!\subset\!S_{y},\,y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}.

(5.22)

For
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
,
i\!\in\!S_{y}
, and
I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
so that
0\!\not\in\!I
,
define

\begin{split}f_{(y,i);I}\big(\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I;I}(v)\!\big)&=\beta_{r}\big(\pi_{2}(v)\!\big)h_{\mathbb{E}}(\widetilde{\pi}_{0}(v))\!+\!\big(1\!-\!\beta_{r}\big(\pi_{2}(v)\!\big)\!\big)\widetilde{\rho}_{0}(v)\\
&\hskip 144.54pt\forall\,v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I;I}^{\prime}\!\subset\!\gamma\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);0\sqcup I}}.\end{split}

(5.23)

By (
5.19
) and the last property in (
5.22
),
the restrictions of the differentials
\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I;I^{\prime}}
with

I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
such that
0\!\not\in\!I

to
\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}^{-1}(U_{y;I}\!\cap\!U_{y;0\sqcup I})

preserve the metrics (
5.14
).
By (
5.19
) and the first property in (
5.22
),
the restrictions of the differentials
\mathfrak{D}\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I;I^{\prime}}
with

I^{\prime}\!\subset\!I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
such that
0\!\in\!I

to
\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}^{-1}(U_{y;I}\!\cap\!U_{r}^{\prime\prime})

preserve the metrics (
5.14
).
By (
5.23
),
the functions
f_{(y,i);I}
satisfy (
5.16
) and (
5.17
) whenever
0\!\not\in\!I
.
By the middle property in (
5.22
), the isomorphism (
5.13
)
identifies the restriction of
\widetilde{\rho}_{(y,i);I;j}
to

\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I-j}}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}|_{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}-U_{r}^{\prime}}

with the restriction of the metric
\rho_{y;I;j}
to
\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;I-j}}V_{y;I}|_{V_{y;I}-U_{r}^{\prime}}

whenever

j\!\neq\!0\!\not\in\!I
.

By
[
14
, Lemma 5.8]
, we can shrink the domains
\textnormal{Dom}(\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I})

of
\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}
with
i\!\in\!S_{y}
and
I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}

to open neighborhoods
\mathcal{N}_{(y,i);I}^{\prime\prime}
of
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\!\subset\!\textnormal{Dom}(\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I})

so that

\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}(\mathcal{N}_{(y,i);I}^{\prime\prime})\subset\begin{cases}U_{y;I}\!\cap\!U_{r}^{\prime\prime},&\hbox{if}~0\!\in\!I;\\
U_{y;I},&\hbox{if}~0\!\not\in\!I;\end{cases}

and the collection
(\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}|_{\mathcal{N}_{(y,i);I}^{\prime\prime}})_{I\subset\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}}

is still a regularization for
\{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);j}\}_{j\in\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}}

in
\widetilde{U}_{y,i}
.
Replacing
\widetilde{\Psi}_{y;I}
with
\widetilde{\Psi}_{y;I}|_{\mathcal{N}_{y;I}^{\prime\prime}}
in (
5.11
)
whenever
y\!\in\!\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\!-\!\mathcal{A}
,
we obtain an almost complex regularization for
\widetilde{V}
in
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{J})
.

5.4
Proof of Lemma
1.3

The substance of the last claim of Lemma
1.3
is that the canonical identification

T_{\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}}\widetilde{X}(-\log\widetilde{V})\big|_{\widetilde{X}-\mathbb{E}}=T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\big|_{X-V^{(r)}}

(5.24)

extends smoothly to a bundle homomorphism as in (
1.8
) and that
this bundle homomorphism is an isomorphism.
For each
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
,
i\!\in\!S_{y}
, and
I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
with
0\!\in\!I
,
we verify this over the open subspace
U_{(y,i);I}^{\circ}\!\subset\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}
defined
as in (
3.12
) via the regularization
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}

constructed in Section
5.3
.

Let
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
,
i\!\in\!S_{y}
, and
I\!\subset\!\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
be as above,

[v]\!\equiv\![(v_{i})_{i\in S_{y}-I}]\!\in\!\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}
with
v_{i}\!\neq\!0

for all
i\!\in\!S_{y}\!-\!I
, and
u\!\in\!(\mathbb{C}v)^{*}\!-\!\{0\}
.
Let
a\!\in\!\mathbb{C}^{*}
and
v^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{y;S_{y};S_{y}-I}
be sufficiently small.
Let

h_{\widetilde{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;0)};av}\!:T_{[v]}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\longrightarrow T_{av}\gamma\quad\hbox{and}\quad h_{\nabla^{(r)};av}\!:T_{[v]}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}\longrightarrow T_{av}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})

be the injective homomorphisms determined by the connections
\widetilde{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;0)}

and
\nabla^{(r)}
as in (
2.6
).
The isomorphism (
5.7
) gives

T_{[v]}\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);I}=T_{\pi(v)}V^{(r)}\!\oplus\!(\mathbb{C}v)^{*}\!\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\!(\mathbb{C}v)^{\perp},

where
(\mathbb{C}v)^{\perp}\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}_{y;S_{y};I}|_{\pi(v)}
is the
\rho_{r}
-orthogonal complement
of
\mathbb{C}v
.
By (
5.15
) and the sentence containing (
5.9
),

\begin{split}h_{\widetilde{\nabla}^{((y,i);I;0)};av}\big(w,u\!\otimes\!v^{\perp}\big)&=h_{\nabla^{(r)};av}(w)\!+\!u(av)v^{\perp}\!+\!\theta(w,u\!\otimes\!v^{\perp})(av)\\
&=h_{\nabla^{(r)};av}(w)\!+\!a\big(u(v)v^{\perp}\!+\!\theta(w,u\!\otimes\!v^{\perp})v\big)\end{split}

for some 1-form
\theta
on
\mathbb{E}
.
With the notation as in (
3.15
), we thus obtain

\begin{split}&\vartheta_{(y,i);(I-0)I}\big(\!\big(\widetilde{\Psi}_{(y,i);I}(av,u\!\otimes\!v^{\prime}),(av,u\!\otimes\!v^{\prime}),(w,u\!\otimes\!v^{\perp})\!\big),(c_{i})_{i\in I}\!\big)\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt=\vartheta_{y;(I-0)S_{y}}\big(\!\big(\Psi_{y;S_{y}}(av\!+\!u(v)v^{\prime}),av\!+\!u(v)v^{\prime},w\big),(c_{i})_{i\in S_{y}}\big),\end{split}

with
c_{i}\!\equiv\!c_{i}([v];w,u\!\otimes\!v^{\perp},c_{0})\in\!\mathbb{C}
for
i\!\in\!S_{y}\!-\!I
defined by

\sum_{i\in S_{y}-I}\!\!\!c_{i}v_{i}=u(v)v^{\perp}\!+\!\big(\theta(w,u\!\otimes\!v^{\perp})\!+\!c_{0}\big)v.

The identification (
5.24
) thus extends smoothly over
[v]
as
the vector space isomorphism

\displaystyle T_{[v]}\mathbb{E}\!\oplus\!\mathbb{C}^{I}\longrightarrow T_{\pi([v])}V^{(r)}\!\oplus\!\mathbb{C}^{S_{y}-I}\!\oplus\!\mathbb{C}^{I-0},

\displaystyle\big(w,(c_{i})_{i\in I}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\textnormal{d}_{[v]}\pi(w),\big(c_{i}([v];w,c_{0})\!\big)_{i\in S_{y}-I},(c_{i})_{i\in I-0}\big).

5.5
Symplectic setting

Suppose now that
\omega
is a symplectic form on
(X,V)

and
\mathcal{R}
is an
\omega
-regularization for
V
in
X
in the sense of
Definition
4.2
.
The smooth submanifold
V^{(r)}\!\subset\!X
is then symplectic.
Let
\omega_{r}\!\equiv\!\widetilde{\omega}
be the closed 2-form on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
determined by

\omega|_{V^{(r)}}
,
\Omega\!\equiv\!\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{r}}
,
and
\nabla^{(r)}
as in (
2.4
).
Let
J
be an
\mathcal{R}
-compatible almost complex structure on
X
,

\pi\!:\big(\widetilde{X}\!\equiv\!\big(\!(X\!-\!V^{(r)})\!\sqcup\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime}\big)\!\big/\!\!\sim,\widetilde{J}\big)\longrightarrow(X,J)

be the corresponding almost complex blowup with the exceptional divisor
\mathbb{E}\!\equiv\!\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})

as in Section
5.2
, and
\overline{V}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}
be the proper transform of
V
.
Suppose also that there exists
\epsilon\!\in\!(0,1)
such that

\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)\!\equiv\!\big\{v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!:\rho_{r}(v)\!<\!2\epsilon\big\}\subset\Psi_{r}^{-1}(U_{r}^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}.

(5.25)

This is automatically the case if
V^{(r)}
is compact.

The subgroup
S^{1}\!\subset\!\mathbb{C}^{*}
acts on
\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
by

u\!:\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)\!\times\!\mathbb{C},\qquad u\!\cdot\!(v,c)=(uv,c/u).

(5.26)

This
S^{1}
-action preserves the submanifolds

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(\epsilon)&\equiv\big\{\!(v,c)\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\!:\rho_{r}(v)\!-\!|c|^{2}\!=\!\epsilon\big\}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\epsilon}\equiv\big\{\!(v,0)\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}:\rho_{r}(v)\!=\!\epsilon\big\}.\end{split}

We identify
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\epsilon}
with the
\sqrt{\epsilon}
-sphere bundle of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}

in the obvious way.
Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon)=\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(\epsilon)/S^{1},\quad\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\!=\!\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\epsilon}/S^{1},\quad X_{\epsilon}=\big(\!\big(X\!-\!\Psi_{r}(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(\epsilon)})\!\big)\!\sqcup\!\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon)\!\big)\!\big)\!\big/\!\!\sim,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon)\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\ni\big[v,\sqrt{\rho_{r}(v)\!-\!\epsilon}\big]\sim\Psi_{r}(v)\in X\!-\!\Psi_{r}(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(\epsilon)})\quad\forall~v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)\!-\!\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(\epsilon)}.

(5.27)

By the Symplectic Reduction Theorem
[
6
, Theorem 23.1]
,
there is a unique symplectic form
\omega_{r;\epsilon}
on the smooth manifold
\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon)

so that

q_{\epsilon}^{*}\omega_{r;\epsilon}=\big(\pi_{1}^{*}\omega_{r}\!+\!\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{\mathbb{C}}\big)\!\big|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(\epsilon)},

(5.28)

where
q_{\epsilon}\!:\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(\epsilon)\!\longrightarrow\!\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon)
is the quotient projection,

\pi_{1},\pi_{2}\!:\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon),\mathbb{C}

are the component projections, and
\omega_{\mathbb{C}}
is the standard symplectic form on
\mathbb{C}
.
Since
\Psi_{r}^{*}\omega\!=\!\omega_{r}
on
\mathcal{N}_{r}(2\epsilon)
and the
S^{1}
-action (
5.26
) preserves
the 2-form
\pi_{1}^{*}\omega_{r}\!+\!\pi_{2}^{*}\omega_{\mathbb{C}}
,
(
5.28
) implies that the identification (
5.27
) intertwines
\omega_{r;\epsilon}

and
\omega
.
We thus obtain a symplectic form
\omega_{\epsilon}
on
X_{\epsilon}
such that

\omega_{\epsilon}\big|_{X-\Psi_{r}(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(\epsilon)})}=\omega\big|_{X-\Psi_{r}(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(\epsilon)})}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\omega_{\epsilon}\big|_{\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon)}=\omega_{r;\epsilon}\,.

It restricts to a symplectic form on
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\!\subset\!X_{\epsilon}
.

The
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
-fiber bundle
\pi_{\epsilon}\!:\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\!\longrightarrow\!V^{(r)}
is canonically identified
with
\pi\!:\mathbb{E}\!\longrightarrow\!V^{(r)}
.
This identification canonically lifts to an identification of the complex line bundle

\widetilde{\pi}_{0}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}=\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\epsilon}\!\times_{S^{1}}\!\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\epsilon}/S^{1}\!\equiv\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon},\qquad(v,c)\sim\big(uv,c/u)~~\forall\,(v,c)\!\in\!\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\epsilon}\!\times\!\mathbb{C},\,u\!\in\!S^{1},

with the tautological line bundle
\gamma\!\subset\!\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
as in (
5.4
).
The differential

\textnormal{d}q_{\epsilon}\!:\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\epsilon}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\!=\!\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}(\epsilon)}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\epsilon}\longrightarrow q_{\epsilon}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}

is an isomorphism of complex line bundles.
It intertwines the fiberwise symplectic form
\omega_{\mathbb{C}}
with the fiberwise symplectic form

\omega_{\epsilon}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}}=\omega_{r;\epsilon}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}}

on
\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}
induced by
\omega_{\epsilon}
as below Definition
2.1
.
Thus, the complex orientation on
\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}
agrees with the orientation
induced by the symplectic form
\omega_{\epsilon}
.
It is straightforward to see that the map

\widetilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon;0}\!:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon;0}^{\prime}\!\equiv\!\big\{[v,c]\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\!:|c|^{2}\!<\!\epsilon\big\}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon)\subset X_{\epsilon},\quad\widetilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon;0}\big([v,c]\big)=\big[\sqrt{1\!+\!|c|^{2}/\epsilon}\,v,c\big],

is a well-defined smooth regularization for
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}
in
X_{\epsilon}
.

Remark 5.2
.

Via the above identification of the complex line bundles
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{X}_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}
and
\gamma
,
the Hermitian metric
\rho_{r}
and connection
\nabla^{(r)}
on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}

determine a connection
\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)}
on
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{X}_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}
,
as in Section
5.1
.
The standard Hermitian metric on
\mathbb{C}
determines a Hermitian metric
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}

on
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{X}_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}
compatible with
\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)}
and the fiberwise symplectic
form
\widetilde{\omega}_{\epsilon}|_{\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{X}_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}}
.
The metric
\widetilde{\rho}_{0}
corresponds to the Hermitian metric
\epsilon^{-1}\pi^{*}\rho_{r}|_{\gamma}
via
the above identification of the complex line bundles
\mathcal{N}_{\widetilde{X}_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}
and
\gamma
.
For the record, we show in Section
5.6
that

(\!(\widetilde{\rho}_{0},\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)}),\widetilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon;0})
is an
\omega_{\epsilon}
-regularization for
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}

in
X_{\epsilon}
in the sense of Definition
3.3
.

The open subspaces
X\!-\!\Psi_{r}(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(\epsilon)})
of
X
and

X_{\epsilon}\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}
of
X_{\epsilon}
are canonically identified.
Let
\overline{V}_{\epsilon}\!\subset\!X_{\epsilon}
be the closure of
V\!-\!\Psi_{r}(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(\epsilon)})

and
\widetilde{V}_{\epsilon}\!=\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\!\cup\!\overline{V}_{\epsilon}
.
We now show that
\widetilde{V}_{\epsilon}
is an NC symplectic divisor in
(X_{\epsilon},\omega_{\epsilon})

with a collection
(\widetilde{U}_{y},\{\widetilde{V}_{y;i}\}_{i\in\widetilde{S}_{y}})_{y\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}
of charts.
If
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}\!-\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
, then

U_{y}\subset X\!-\!V^{(r)}=X_{\epsilon}\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}.

In this case, we again take

\widetilde{S}_{y}=S_{y}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\big(\widetilde{U}_{y},\{\widetilde{V}_{y;i}\}_{i\in\widetilde{S}_{y}}\big)=(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}}\big).

As before, we identify
\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
with
\Psi_{r}(\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime})\!\subset\!X
via
\Psi_{r}
.
Let

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r;\epsilon}^{\prime}=\big(\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}\!-\!\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(\epsilon)}\big)\!\cup\!\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon)\subset X_{\epsilon}

and
\widetilde{\pi}_{r;\epsilon}\!:\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r;\epsilon}^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow\!V^{(r)}
be the projection induced by
\pi_{r}
.
Suppose
y\!\in\!\mathcal{A}_{r}
.
For each
i\!\in\!S_{y}
,
define
\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}
,
\mathcal{N}_{y;i}
, and
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);0}
as at the end
of Section
5.2
,
with
\mathbb{E}
replaced by
\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}
, and set

\widetilde{U}_{y}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r;\epsilon}^{\prime}|_{V_{y;S_{y}}}\equiv\widetilde{\pi}_{r;\epsilon}^{-1}(V_{y;S_{y}}),\qquad\overline{V}_{y;i}=\gamma|_{\mathbb{P}\mathcal{N}_{y;i}}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{y}\,.

For
j\!\in\!S_{y}\!-\!i
, let
\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);j}=\overline{V}_{y;j}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}

as before.
We again have (
5.12
), with
\overline{V}
replaced by
\overline{V}_{\epsilon}
in the last statement.
In this case,

\{\widetilde{V}_{(y,i);j}\}_{j\in\widetilde{S}_{(y,i)}}
is a transverse collection of codimension 2
symplectic submanifolds of
(\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)},\omega_{r;\epsilon})

so that their intersection and symplectic orientations agree.
Thus,
\widetilde{V}_{\epsilon}\!\cap\!\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)}
is an SC symplectic divisor
in
(\widetilde{U}_{(y,i)},\omega_{r;\epsilon})

in the sense of Definition
3.1
,

\widetilde{V}_{\epsilon}
is an NC symplectic divisor in
(X_{\epsilon},\omega_{\epsilon})
, and

(\widetilde{U}_{y},\{\widetilde{V}_{y;i}\}_{i\in\widetilde{S}_{y}})_{y\in\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}}
is an atlas of local charts
for
\widetilde{V}_{\epsilon}
.

Let
f_{\epsilon}\!:\mathbb{R}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}
be a smooth function so that

f_{\epsilon}^{\prime}(t)>0~~\forall\,t\!\in\!\mathbb{R},\qquad f_{\epsilon}(t)=\begin{cases}\sqrt{\epsilon\!+\!t^{2}/\epsilon},&\hbox{if}~t\!\leq\!\epsilon/2;\\
t,&\hbox{if}~t\!\geq\!5\sqrt{\epsilon}/4.\end{cases}

The map

\widetilde{\pi}_{\epsilon}\!:\widetilde{X}\longrightarrow X_{\epsilon},\quad\widetilde{\pi}_{\epsilon}(\widetilde{x})=\begin{cases}\widetilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon;0}(\widetilde{x})\!\in\!\mathcal{Z}(\epsilon),&\hbox{if}~\widetilde{x}\!\in\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime},~\rho_{r}(\widetilde{x})\!<\!\epsilon^{2}/4;\\
\Psi_{r}\big(f_{\epsilon}(\sqrt{\rho_{r}(\widetilde{x})})\frac{\widetilde{x}}{\sqrt{\rho_{r}(\widetilde{x})}}\big),&\hbox{if}~\widetilde{x}\!\in\!\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{0}^{\prime},~\rho_{r}(\widetilde{x})\!>\!0;\\
\widetilde{x}\!\in\!X\!-\!\Psi_{r}(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(25\epsilon/16)})),&\hbox{if}~\widetilde{x}\!\in\!X\!-\!\Psi_{r}(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{r}(25\epsilon/16)});\end{cases}

is then an orientation-preserving diffeomorphism.
It identifies the NC almost complex divisor
\widetilde{V}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}

with the NC symplectic divisor
\widetilde{V}_{\epsilon}\!\subset\!X_{\epsilon}
.
Thus,

\widetilde{\pi}_{\epsilon}^{*}\omega_{\epsilon}\in\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{V}).

Let
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
be a regularization for
\overline{V}
in
(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{J})

obtained as in Section
5.3

from the regularization
\mathcal{R}
for
V
in
(X,\omega)
and thus in
(X,J)
.
Repeated applications of
[
14
, Theorem 3.1]
,
starting from the deepest strata of
\overline{V}\!\cap\!\mathbb{E}
, provide
a smooth family
(\mu_{\tau})_{\tau\in[0,1]}
of 1-forms on
\widetilde{X}
so that

\bullet

\widetilde{\omega}_{\epsilon;\tau}\!\equiv\!\widetilde{\pi}_{\epsilon}^{*}\omega_{\epsilon}\!+\!\textnormal{d}\mu_{\tau}\in\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{V})

for all
\tau\!\in\![0,1]
;

\bullet

\mu_{0}\!=\!0
and
\textnormal{supp}\,\mu_{\tau}\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}_{0}^{\prime}
for all
\tau\!\in\![0,1]
;

\bullet

a tuple
\widehat{\mathcal{R}}
obtained from
\widetilde{\mathcal{R}}
by restricting the domains of the maps
\widetilde{\Psi}_{y;I}

with
y\!\in\!\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\!-\!(\mathcal{A}\!-\!\mathcal{A}_{r})
is an
\widetilde{\omega}_{\epsilon;1}
-regularization for
\widetilde{V}

in
\widetilde{X}
.

The bundle isomorphism (
1.8
) thus determines a homotopy class of
isomorphisms (
1.10
)
between the log tangent bundles associated with the deformation equivalence classes of
\omega

in
\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(X,V)
and of
\widetilde{\pi}_{\epsilon}^{*}\omega_{\epsilon}
in
\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(\widetilde{X},\widetilde{V})
.

The (deformation equivalence class of the) NC symplectic divisor
\widetilde{V}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}

constructed above does not depend on the choices of
J
,
f_{\epsilon}
, and
\epsilon
.
Since the projection (
2.7
) is a weak homotopy equivalence,
it does not depend on the choices of the regularization
\mathcal{R}
and

\omega\!\in\!\textnormal{Symp}^{+}(X,V)
in the given equivalence class if
V^{(r)}
is compact.
Thus, if
V^{(r)}
is compact, an NC symplectic divisor structure
[\omega]
on
V\!\subset\!X

determines an NC symplectic divisor structure
[\widetilde{\omega}]
on
\widetilde{V}\!\subset\!\widetilde{X}
.

5.6
Proofs of technical statements

We conclude our discussion of blowups with proofs of the claims of
Lemma
5.1
and Remark
5.2
.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

Suppose
r\!\geq\!2
and the claim is true with
r
replaced by
r\!-\!1
.
We denote by
\mathbb{S}_{r}
the group of permutations of the homogeneous coordinates of
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}

and by
\tau_{r}\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{r}
the transposition of the last two coordinates.
We identify
\mathbb{P}^{r-2}
with the subspace
(Z_{r}\!=\!0)
of
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
.
It is preserved by the subgroup
\mathbb{S}_{r-1}\!\subset\!\mathbb{S}_{r}
and
by the
(S^{1})^{r}
-action on
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
.

Let
h\!:\mathbb{P}^{r-2}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
and
\delta\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
be a smooth function and a positive number
satisfying the conditions in the lemma with
r
replaced by
r\!-\!1
.
Let
U\!\subset\!\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
be an open neighborhood of
\mathbb{P}^{r-2}
preserved by
the subgroup
\mathbb{S}_{r-1}\!\subset\!\mathbb{S}_{r}
and by the
(S^{1})^{r}
-action
so that

U\!\cap\!\tau_{r}(U)\subset\big\{[Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}]\!\in\!\mathbb{P}^{r-1}\!:|Z_{r-1}|^{2}\!+\!|Z_{r}|^{2}\!\leq\!\delta\big(|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r-2}|^{2}\big)\!\big\}.

(5.29)

We extend
h
over
U
by

\widetilde{h}\!:U\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+},\qquad\widetilde{h}\big([Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}]\big)=h\big([Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r-1},0]\big)\frac{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r}|^{2}}{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r-1}|^{2}}\,.

For each permutation
g\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{r}
of the homogeneous coordinates of
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
, define

\widetilde{h}_{g}\!:g(U)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+},\qquad\widetilde{h}_{g}\big([Z]\big)=\widetilde{h}\big([g^{-1}Z]\big).

By the invariance assumptions on
U
and
h
,
\widetilde{h}_{g_{1}}\!=\!\widetilde{h}_{g_{2}}

if
g_{1}^{-1}g_{2}\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{r-1}
.

Suppose
[Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}]\!\in\!U\!\cap\!\tau_{r}(U)
.
By (
5.29
) and (
5.18
) with
r
replaced by
r\!-\!1
,

\begin{split}&\widetilde{h}_{\tau_{r}}\big([Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}]\big)=\widetilde{h}\big([Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r-2},Z_{r},Z_{r-1}]\big)\\
&\qquad=\widetilde{h}\big([Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r-2},0,0]\big)\frac{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r-2}|^{2}\!+\!|Z_{r}|^{2}}{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r-2}|^{2}}\frac{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r}|^{2}}{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r-2}|^{2}\!+\!|Z_{r}|^{2}}\\
&\qquad=\widetilde{h}\big([Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r-2},0,0]\big)\frac{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r-1}|^{2}}{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r-2}|^{2}}\frac{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r}|^{2}}{|Z_{1}|^{2}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!|Z_{r-1}|^{2}}=\widetilde{h}\big([Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{r}]\big).\end{split}

Thus,
\widetilde{h}_{\tau_{r}}\!=\!\widetilde{h}
on
U\!\cap\!\tau_{r}(U)
.
Along with the invariance assumptions on
U
and
h
,
this implies that
\widetilde{h}_{g_{1}}\!=\!\widetilde{h}_{g_{2}}
on
g_{1}(U)\!\cap\!g_{2}(U)

for all
g_{1},g_{2}\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{r}
.

We thus obtain a well-defined smooth function

H\!:W\!\equiv\!\bigcup_{g\in\mathbb{S}_{r}}\!\!g(U)\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+},\qquad H([Z])=\widetilde{h}_{g}([Z])~~\forall\,g\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{r},~[Z]\!\in\!g(U),

which is invariant under the
\mathbb{S}_{r}
- and
(S^{1})^{r}
-actions on
\mathbb{P}^{r-1}
and
satisfies the last property in the lemma for some
\delta\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
Let
\beta\!:\mathbb{P}^{r-1}\!\longrightarrow\![1,0]
be a smooth function which is invariant under these
two actions, restricts to 1 on a neighborhood of
\mathbb{P}^{r-2}
, and is supported in
W
.
The smooth function

\beta H\!+\!1\!-\!\beta\!:\mathbb{P}^{r-1}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}

then has the desired properties for some
\delta\!\in\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
∎

Proof of Remark
5.2
.

Let

\displaystyle\widehat{\omega}_{\epsilon}=\widetilde{\pi}_{0}^{*}\big(\omega_{\epsilon}|_{\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}}\big)\!+\!\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}}}\big(\omega_{\epsilon}|_{\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}}\big)_{\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)}},

\displaystyle\phi,\varphi\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)},\quad\phi(v)=\frac{v}{\sqrt{\rho_{r}(v)}},~~\varphi(v)=\sqrt{1+\rho_{r}(v)}\frac{v}{\sqrt{\rho_{r}(v)}},

\displaystyle m_{c}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)},\quad m_{c}(v)=cv,\qquad\forall\,c\!\in\!\mathbb{C}.

The composition of the restriction of
\widetilde{\Psi}_{\epsilon;0}
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon;0}^{\prime}\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}
with
the identification in (
5.27
) is given by

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon;0}^{\prime}\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\xrightarrow{{m_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\circ\varphi\circ m_{1/\epsilon}}}\mathcal{N}_{r}^{\prime}\!-\!V^{(r)}\xrightarrow{{~\Psi_{r}~}}X\!-\!V^{(r)}\,.

It thus remains to show that
\varphi^{*}(m_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}^{*}\omega_{r})\!=\!m_{\epsilon}^{*}\widehat{\omega}_{\epsilon}
on

m_{1/\epsilon}(\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{\epsilon;0}^{\prime}\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon})\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r)}
.

For each
v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r)}
, let

\pi_{v},\pi_{v}^{\perp}\!:\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\big|_{\widetilde{\pi}_{0}(v)}\!=\!(\mathbb{C}v)^{\perp}\!\oplus\!(\mathbb{C}v)\longrightarrow\mathbb{C}v,(\mathbb{C}v)^{\perp}

be the
\rho_{r}
-orthogonal projections.
Let

\pi_{\nabla}\!:T_{v}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\big|_{\pi_{r}(v)}

be the projection corresponding to the decomposition (
5.6
)
determined by the connection
\nabla^{(r)}
.
As noted below (
5.6
), this splitting also encodes
the decomposition associated with the connection
\widetilde{\nabla}^{(0)}
.
By the properties of a connection, the map
m_{c}
preserves the decomposition (
5.6
)
for any
c\!\in\!\mathbb{C}^{*}
;
see
[
38
, Lemma 1.1]
.
Since the connection
\nabla^{(r)}
is compatible with the Hermitian metric
\rho_{r}
,
its connection 1-form is purely imaginary in any Hermitian trivialization.
It follows that the maps
\phi
and
\varphi
also preserve the decomposition (
5.6
);
see the proof of
[
38
, Lemma 1.1]
.
Thus,

\pi_{\nabla}\!\circ\!m_{c}=m_{c}\!\circ\!\pi_{\nabla}~~\forall\,c\!\in\!\mathbb{C},\qquad\pi_{\nabla}\!\circ\!\phi=\phi\!\circ\!\pi_{\nabla},\qquad\pi_{\nabla}\!\circ\!\varphi=\varphi\!\circ\!\pi_{\nabla}\,.

(5.30)

We also note that

m_{c*}\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}\!=\!\zeta_{\mathcal{N}}\!\circ\!m_{c}\quad\hbox{and}\quad m_{c}^{*}\Omega=c^{2}\Omega\qquad\forall\,c\!\in\!\mathbb{R}

(5.31)

for any vector bundle
\mathcal{N}
and a fiberwise 2-form
\Omega
on
\mathcal{N}
.

Since
\widetilde{\pi}_{0}\!=\!q_{\epsilon}\!\circ\!m_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}\!\circ\!\phi\!\circ\!m_{1/\epsilon}
,

\omega_{\epsilon}|_{\mathbb{C}v}\!=\!\epsilon^{-1}\omega_{r}|_{\mathbb{C}v}
,
and
\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}}\!=\!\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}
on

\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}\!=\!\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r)}
,
the first identity in (
5.30
) and (
5.31
) give

\begin{split}m_{\epsilon}^{*}\widehat{\omega}_{\epsilon}&=\phi^{*}m_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}^{*}q_{\epsilon}^{*}\big(\omega_{\epsilon}|_{\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}}\big)\!+\!\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X_{\epsilon}}\mathbb{E}_{\epsilon}}}m_{\epsilon}^{*}\big(\epsilon^{-1}\omega_{r}|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!\circ\!\pi_{v}\big)_{\nabla^{(r)}}\\
&=\phi^{*}(m_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}^{*}\omega_{r})+\!\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}\big((m_{\sqrt{\epsilon}}^{*}\omega)|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!\circ\!\pi_{v}\!\circ\!\pi_{\nabla}\big).\end{split}

Thus, it is sufficient to assume that
\epsilon\!=\!1
and show that

\varphi^{*}\omega_{r}=\phi^{*}\omega_{r}\!+\!\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}\big(\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!\circ\!\pi_{v}\!\circ\!\pi_{\nabla}\big)

(5.32)

on
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\!-\!V^{(r)}
.

By the compatibility of
\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}
with the Hermitian metric
\rho_{r}

determining the projection
\pi_{v}
,

\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}(\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}})=\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}(\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!\circ\!\pi_{v}).

We also note that

\displaystyle\big(\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}(\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}})\!\big)\!\circ\!\phi_{*}=\frac{1}{\rho_{r}(v)}\big(\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}(\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}})\!\big),

\displaystyle\varphi_{*}\bigg(\frac{1\!+\!\rho_{r}(v)}{\rho_{r}(v)}\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!\!\bigg)=\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!\circ\!\varphi,\quad\varphi^{*}\big(\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\big)=\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!+\!\frac{1}{\rho_{r}(v)}\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!\circ\!\pi_{v}^{\perp}.

Along with the last two identities in (
5.30
) and

\omega_{r}=\pi_{r}^{*}\big(\omega|_{V^{(r)}}\big)\!+\!\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}\big(\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\!\circ\!\pi_{\nabla}\big),

the above statements give

\begin{split}\phi^{*}\omega_{r}&=\pi_{r}^{*}\big(\omega|_{V^{(r)}}\big)\!+\!\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\bigg(\frac{1}{\rho_{r}(v)}\big(\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\big)\!\circ\!\pi_{\nabla}\!\!\bigg),\\
\varphi^{*}\omega_{r}&=\pi_{r}^{*}\big(\omega|_{V^{(r)}}\big)\!+\!\frac{1}{2}\textnormal{d}\bigg(\frac{1\!+\!\rho_{r}(v)}{\rho_{r}(v)}\big(\iota_{\zeta_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}}\omega|_{\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}}\big)\!\circ\!\pi_{\nabla}\!\!\bigg).\end{split}

This establishes (
5.32
).
∎

6
NC symplectic divisors: global perspective

An NC divisor can also be viewed as the image of a transverse immersion
\iota
with certain properties.
Following
[
15
]
, we review the global analogues of the notions of
Sections
4.1
and
4.2
in
Sections
6.1
and
6.2
below.
This global perspective leads to a more succinct notion of regularizations for
NC divisors and fits better with global statements, such as Theorem
1.2
(5)
.
The local and global perspectives are shown to be equivalent in
[
15
, Lemma 3.5]
.

6.1
Definition

For a finite set
I
, denote by
\mathbb{S}_{I}
the symmetric group of permutations of the elements of
I
.
For
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, denote by
\mathbb{S}_{k}\!\equiv\!\mathbb{S}_{[k]}
the
k
-th symmetric group.
For
k^{\prime}\!\in\![k]
, there is a natural subgroup

\mathbb{S}_{k^{\prime}}\times\!\mathbb{S}_{[k]-[k^{\prime}]}\subset\mathbb{S}_{k}\,.

We denote its first factor by
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}
and the second by
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
.
For each
\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k}
and
i\!\in\![k]
, let
\sigma_{i}\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k-1}

be the permutation obtained from the bijection

[k]\!-\!\{i\}\longrightarrow[k]\!-\!\{\sigma(i)\},\qquad j\longrightarrow\sigma(j),

(6.1)

by identifying its domain and target with
[k\!-\!1]
in the order-preserving fashions.

For any map
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
and
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, let

\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}=\big\{(x,\widetilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{k})\!\in\!X\!\times\!(\widetilde{V}^{k}\!-\!\Delta_{\widetilde{V}}^{(k)})\!:\,\iota(\widetilde{v}_{i})\!=\!x~\forall\,i\!\in\![k]\big\},

(6.2)

where
\Delta_{\widetilde{V}}^{(k)}\!\subset\!\widetilde{V}^{k}
is the big diagonal
(at least two of the coordinates are the same).
Define

\displaystyle\iota_{k}\!:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\longrightarrow X,\qquad\iota_{k}(x,\widetilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{k})=x,

(6.3)

\displaystyle V_{\iota}^{(k)}=\iota_{k}(\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)})=\big\{x\!\in\!X\!:\,\big|\iota^{-1}(x)\big|\!\geq\!k\big\}.

(6.4)

For example,

\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(0)},V_{\iota}^{(0)}=X,\qquad\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(1)}\approx\widetilde{V},\qquad V_{\iota}^{(1)}=\iota(\widetilde{V}).

For
k^{\prime},k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
and
i\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
with
i,k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
, define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k^{\prime})},

\displaystyle\quad\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}(x,\widetilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{k})

\displaystyle=(x,\widetilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{k^{\prime}}),

(6.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\!:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k-1)},

\displaystyle\quad\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}(x,\widetilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{k})

\displaystyle=(x,\widetilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{i-1},\widetilde{v}_{i+1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{k}),

(6.6)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(i)}\!:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\widetilde{V},

\displaystyle\quad\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(i)}(x,\widetilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{k})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{v}_{i}.

(6.7)

For example,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!=\!\widetilde{\iota}_{k^{\prime}+1;k^{\prime}}^{(k^{\prime}+1)}\!\circ\!\ldots\!\circ\!\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(k)}\!

\displaystyle:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k^{\prime})},

\displaystyle\qquad\widetilde{\iota}_{k;1}\!\approx\!\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(1)}\!

\displaystyle:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(1)}\!\approx\!\widetilde{V},
