\displaystyle\widetilde{\iota}_{k;0}\!=\!\iota_{k}\!

\displaystyle:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(0)}\!=\!X,

\displaystyle\qquad\widetilde{\iota}_{1;0}\!\approx\!\iota\!

\displaystyle:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(1)}\!\approx\!\widetilde{V}\longrightarrow X.

We define an
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-action on
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
by requiring that

\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(i)}=\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(\sigma(i))}\!\circ\!\sigma\!:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}

(6.8)

for all
\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k}
and
i\!\in\![k]
.
The diagrams

\begin{split}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 48.5175pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-12.72722pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k-1)}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{split}

(6.9)

of solid arrows then commute; the entire first diagram commutes if
i\!>\!k^{\prime}
.

Example 6.1
.

If
V\!=\!\bigcup_{i\in S}\!V_{i}
is an SC symplectic divisor, then

\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\equiv\!\bigsqcup_{i\in S}\!V_{i}\longrightarrow X

restricts by the inclusion on each
V_{i}
, and

\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}\approx\bigsqcup_{I\subset S,\,|I|=k}\hskip-14.45377pt\big(V_{I}\!\times\!\vec{I}\big),

where
\vec{I}\!\subset\!I^{k}
is the subcollection of tuples with all entries distinct.
The action of
\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k}
on
\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}
is by reordering
the element of each tuple in
\vec{I}
:

(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})\longrightarrow(i_{\sigma^{-1}(1)},\ldots,i_{\sigma^{-1}(k)}).

The maps
\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k^{\prime})}

in (
6.5
) are given by

V_{I}\!\times\!\vec{I}\!\ni\!\big(x,(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k})\!\big)\longrightarrow\big(x,(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}})\!\big)\!\in\!V_{J}\!\times\!\vec{J},\qquad\textnormal{where}~\vec{J}=\vec{I}\!-\!\big\{i_{k^{\prime}+1},\ldots,i_{k}\big\}.

A smooth map
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\longrightarrow\!X
is an
immersion
if
the differential
\textnormal{d}_{x}\iota
of
\iota
at
x
is injective for all
x\!\in\!\widetilde{V}
.
This implies that

\textnormal{codim}\,\iota\equiv\dim X-\dim V\geq 0.

Such a map has a well-defined normal bundle,

\mathcal{N}\iota\equiv\iota^{*}TX\big/\textnormal{Im}(\textnormal{d}\iota)\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}\,.

(6.10)

If
\iota
is a closed immersion, then the subspace
V_{\iota}^{(k)}\!\subset\!X

and
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\!\subset\!X\!\times\!\widetilde{V}^{k}
are closed.

An immersion
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\longrightarrow\!X
is
transverse
if the homomorphism

T_{x}X\oplus\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}T_{\widetilde{v}_{i}}\widetilde{V}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}T_{x}X,\quad\big(w,(w_{i})_{i\in[k]}\big)\longrightarrow\big(w\!+\!\textnormal{d}_{\widetilde{v}_{i}}\iota(w_{i})\big)_{i\in[k]}\,,

is surjective for all
(x,\widetilde{v}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{v}_{k})\!\in\!\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
and
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
.
By the Inverse Function Theorem, in such a case

\bullet

each
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
is a smooth submanifold of
X\!\times\!\widetilde{V}^{k}
,

\bullet

the maps
\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}
in (
6.5
) and
the maps (
6.6
) are transverse immersions,

\bullet

the homeomorphisms
\sigma
of
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
determined by the elements of
\mathbb{S}_{k}

as in (
6.8
) are diffeomorphisms.

By the commutativity of the upper and middle triangles in the first diagram in (
6.9
),
the inclusion of
\textnormal{Im}(\textnormal{d}\iota_{k})
into
\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(i)*}\textnormal{Im}(\textnormal{d}\iota)
and
the homomorphism
\textnormal{d}\iota_{k-1}
induce homomorphisms

\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\longrightarrow\iota_{k}^{(i)*}\mathcal{N}\iota,\quad\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\qquad\forall~i\!\in\![k].

By the Inverse Function Theorem, the resulting homomorphisms

\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in[k]}\!\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(i)*}\mathcal{N}\iota\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(i)*}\mathcal{N}\iota~~~\forall\,i\!\in\![k]

(6.11)

are isomorphisms.
If
\widetilde{V}
is the disjoint union of submanifolds
V_{i}\!\subset\!X
,
they correspond to the first two isomorphisms in (
3.2
).
For
\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k}
and
i\!\in\![k]
,
the homomorphisms
\textnormal{d}\sigma
and
\textnormal{d}\sigma_{i}
of the second diagram in (
6.9
)
induces an isomorphism

D_{i}\sigma\!:\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(\sigma(i))}

(6.12)

covering
\sigma
.

If
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is any immersion between oriented manifolds of even dimensions,
the short exact sequence of vector bundles

0\longrightarrow T\widetilde{V}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{d}\iota}}{{\longrightarrow}}\iota^{*}TX\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}\iota\longrightarrow 0

(6.13)

over
\widetilde{V}
induces an orientation on
\mathcal{N}\iota
.
If in addition
\iota
is a transverse immersion,
the orientation on
\mathcal{N}\iota
induced by the orientations of
X
and
\widetilde{V}
induces
an orientation on
\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}
via the first isomorphism in (
6.11
).
The orientations of
X
and
\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}
then induce an orientation on
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}

via the short exact sequence (
6.13
) with
\iota\!=\!\iota_{k}
for all
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
,
which we call
the intersection orientation of
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
.
For
k\!=\!1
, it agrees with the original orientation of
\widetilde{V}
under the canonical identification

\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(1)}\!\approx\!\widetilde{V}
.

Suppose
(X,\omega)
is a symplectic manifold.
If
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is a transverse immersion such that
\iota_{k}^{*}\omega

is a symplectic form on
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
for all
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, then
each
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
carries an orientation induced by
\iota_{k}^{*}\omega
,
which we call the
\omega
-orientation.
By the previous paragraph, the
\omega
-orientations of
X
and
\widetilde{V}

also induce intersection orientations on all
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
.
By definition, the intersection and
\omega
-orientations of
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(1)}

are the same.

Proposition 6.2
.

(
[
15
, Proposition 3.6]
)
Suppose
(X,\omega)
is a symplectic manifold and
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is a transverse immersion of codimension 2. Then,
V=\iota(\widetilde{V})
is an NC symplectic divisor in
(X,\omega)

in the sense of Definition
4.1

if and only if
\iota_{k}^{*}\omega

is a symplectic form on
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
for all
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}

and the intersection and
\omega
-orientations of
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
are the same.

In the global description of an NC divisor
V\!\subset\!X
,
the singular locus
V_{\partial}\!\subset\!X
is
V_{\iota}^{(2)}
.

6.2
Regularizations

Suppose
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\longrightarrow\!X
is a transverse immersion and
k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}

with
k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
.
With the notation as in (
6.2
)-(
6.7
), define

\pi_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota=\!\bigoplus_{i\in[k]-[k^{\prime}]}\!\!\!\!\!\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\pi_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\!:\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\iota=\!\bigoplus_{i\in[k^{\prime}]}\!\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\,.

(6.14)

By the commutativity of the first diagram in (
6.9
),
the homomorphisms
\textnormal{d}\widetilde{\iota}_{k-1;k^{\prime}}
and
\textnormal{d}\iota_{k-1}
induce homomorphisms

\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\iota\longrightarrow\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{\,*}\mathcal{N}\iota_{k^{\prime}}.

By the Inverse Function Theorem, these homomorphisms are isomorphisms.
If
\widetilde{V}
is the disjoint union of submanifolds
V_{i}\!\subset\!X
,
they correspond to the last isomorphism in (
3.2
)
and the first identification in (
3.6
).
For each
\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k}
, the isomorphisms (
6.11
) and (
6.12
)
induce an isomorphism

D\sigma=(D_{i}\sigma)_{i\in[k]}\!:\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\approx\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota\equiv\bigoplus_{i\in[k]}\!\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i\in[k]}\!\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(\sigma(i))}\equiv\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota\approx\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}

(6.15)

lifting the action of
\sigma
on
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
.
The isomorphism (
6.15
) permutes the components of the direct sum
so that the subbundles

\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota,\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\iota\subset\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}

are invariant under the action of the subgroup

\mathbb{S}_{k^{\prime}}\!\times\!\mathbb{S}_{[k]-[k^{\prime}]}
of
\mathbb{S}_{k}
,
but not under the action of the full group
\mathbb{S}_{k}
.

Definition 6.3
.

A
regularization
for an immersion
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is a smooth map

\Psi\!:\mathcal{N}^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow\!X
from a neighborhood of
\widetilde{V}
in
\mathcal{N}\iota

such that for every
\widetilde{v}\!\in\!\widetilde{V}
,
there exist a neighborhood
U_{\widetilde{v}}
of
\widetilde{v}
in
\widetilde{V}
so that
the restriction of
\Psi
to
\mathcal{N}^{\prime}|_{U_{\widetilde{v}}}
is a diffeomorphism onto its image,

\Psi(\widetilde{v})\!=\!\iota(\widetilde{v})
, and the homomorphism

\mathcal{N}\iota|_{\widetilde{v}}=T_{\widetilde{v}}^{\textnormal{ver}}\mathcal{N}\iota\lhook\joinrel\relbar\joinrel\rightarrow T_{\widetilde{v}}\mathcal{N}\iota\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{d}_{\widetilde{v}}\Psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}T_{\widetilde{v}}X\longrightarrow\frac{T_{\widetilde{v}}X}{\textnormal{Im}(\textnormal{d}_{\widetilde{v}}\iota)}\equiv\mathcal{N}\iota|_{\widetilde{v}}

is the identity.

Definition 6.4
.

A
system of regularizations for
a transverse immersion
\iota\!:\!\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X

is a tuple
(\Psi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}
, where each
\Psi_{k}

is a regularization for the immersion
\iota_{k}
, such that

\displaystyle\Psi_{k}\big(\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota\!\cap\!\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\big)=V_{\iota}^{(k^{\prime})}\!\cap\!\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{k})

\displaystyle\forall~k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~k^{\prime}\!\in\![k],

(6.16)

\displaystyle\hskip 43.36243pt\Psi_{k}=\Psi_{k}\!\circ\!D\sigma\big|_{\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})}

\displaystyle\forall~k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k};

(6.17)

\displaystyle\quad\big\{x\!\in\!X\!:|\Psi_{k}^{-1}(x)|\!\geq\!k\big\}\subset\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{k+1})

\displaystyle\forall~k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}.

(6.18)

The stratification condition (
6.16
) replaces the first condition in (
3.7
)
and implies that there exists a smooth map

\begin{split}&\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\iota\!\equiv\!\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota\!\cap\!\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\longrightarrow\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k^{\prime})}\qquad\hbox{s.t.}\\
&\quad\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}}=\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}\,,\quad\Psi_{k}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\iota}=\iota_{k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\,;\end{split}

(6.19)

see Proposition 1.35 and Theorem 1.32 in
[
36
]
.
Similarly to (
3.9
),
\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
lifts to a (fiberwise) vector bundle isomorphism

\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\pi_{k;k^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\iota|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\iota}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}\iota_{k^{\prime}}\big|_{\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}})}.

(6.20)

This bundle isomorphism preserves the second splitting below in (
6.14
) and
is
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}
-equivariant and
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
-invariant.
The condition (
6.21
) below replaces (
3.10
) in the present setting.

Definition 6.5
.

A
refined regularization
for a transverse immersion
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X

is a system
(\Psi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}
of regularizations for
\iota

such that

\begin{split}&\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\subset\pi_{k;k^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\iota|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\iota},\quad\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\big(\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\big)=\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k^{\prime}})\big|_{\textnormal{Im}(\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}})},\\
&\hskip 108.405pt\Psi_{k}=\Psi_{k^{\prime}}\circ\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}|_{\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})}\end{split}

(6.21)

whenever
0\!\leq\!k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
.

If
(\Psi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}
is a refined regularization
for a transverse immersion
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
, then

\begin{split}&\hskip 72.26999pt\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\iota,\pi_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}^{c}\iota|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\iota}\subset\pi_{k;k^{\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\iota\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\iota},\\
&\Psi_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}=\Psi_{k^{\prime};k^{\prime\prime}}\circ\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\iota}\,,\quad\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}=\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k^{\prime};k^{\prime\prime}}\circ\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\big|_{\pi_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}^{*}\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}^{c}\iota|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}^{\prime}\iota}}\end{split}

(6.22)

whenever
0\!\leq\!k^{\prime\prime}\!\leq\!k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
.

Suppose
(X,\omega)
is a symplectic manifold and

\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is an immersion so that
\iota^{*}\omega

is a symplectic form on
V
.
The normal bundle

\mathcal{N}\iota\equiv\frac{\iota^{*}TX}{\textnormal{Im}(\textnormal{d}\iota)}\approx\big(\textnormal{Im}(\textnormal{d}\iota)\big)^{\omega}\equiv\big\{w\!\in\!T_{\iota(\widetilde{v})}X\!:\,\widetilde{v}\!\in\!\widetilde{V},\,\omega\big(w,\textnormal{d}_{x}\iota(w^{\prime})\big)\!=\!0~\forall\,w^{\prime}\!\in\!T_{\widetilde{v}}V\big\}

of
\iota
then inherits a fiberwise symplectic form
\omega|_{\mathcal{N}\iota}
from
\omega
.
We denote the restriction of
\omega|_{\mathcal{N}\iota}
to a subbundle
L\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}\iota

by
\omega|_{L}
.

Definition 6.6
.

Suppose
(X,\omega)
is a symplectic manifold,

\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is an immersion so that
\iota^{*}\omega
is a symplectic form on
V
,
and

\mathcal{N}\iota=\bigoplus_{i\in I}L_{i}

is a fixed splitting into oriented rank 2 subbundles.
If
\omega|_{L_{i}}
is nondegenerate for every
i\!\in\!I
, then
an
\omega
-regularization for
\iota
is a tuple
((\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})_{i\in I},\Psi)
,
where
(\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})
is an
\omega|_{L_{i}}
-compatible Hermitian structure on
L_{i}

for each
i\!\in\!I
and
\Psi
is a regularization for
\iota
, such that

\Psi^{*}\omega=\big(\iota^{*}\omega\big)_{(\rho_{i},\nabla^{(i)})_{i\in I}}\big|_{\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi)}.

Definition 6.7
.

Suppose
(X,\omega)
is a symplectic manifold and

\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is a transverse immersion of codimension 2
so that
\iota_{k}^{*}\omega
is a symplectic form on
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
for each
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
.
A
refined
\omega
-regularization for
\iota
is a tuple

\mathcal{R}\equiv\big(\mathcal{R}_{k}\big)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}\equiv\big((\rho_{k;i},\nabla^{(k;i)})_{i\in[k]},\Psi_{k}\big)_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}

(6.23)

such that
(\Psi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}
is a refined regularization for
\iota
,

\mathcal{R}_{k}
is an
\omega
-regularization for
\iota_{k}

with respect to the splitting (
6.15
) for every
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
,

\big(\rho_{k;i},\nabla^{(k;i)}\big)=\big\{D_{i}{\sigma}\big\}^{\!*}\big(\rho_{k;\sigma(i)},\nabla^{(k;\sigma(i))}\big)\quad\forall\,k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},\,\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k},\,i\!\in\![k],

(6.24)

and the induced vector bundle isomorphisms (
6.20
)
are product Hermitian isomorphisms for all
k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
.

An almost complex structure
J
on
X
that preserves
\textnormal{Im}\,\textnormal{d}\iota
determines
an almost complex structure
J_{\iota;k}
on
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
for every
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
,
with
J_{0}\!=\!J
.
The maps
\iota
,
\iota_{k}
,
\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}
,
\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}
,
\widetilde{\iota}_{k}^{(i)}
,
and
\sigma
from
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
respect these almost complex structures.
A refined
\omega
-regularization
\mathcal{R}
for
\iota

determines a fiberwise complex structure
\mathfrak{i}_{k}
on
\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}
and a splitting

T(\mathcal{N}\iota_{k})\approx\pi_{k;0}^{*}T\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}\!\oplus\!\pi_{k;0}^{*}\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\,,

which are preserved by the action of
\mathbb{S}_{k}
.
Along with
J_{\iota;k}
, they determine an almost complex structure
J_{\mathcal{R};k}
on
the total space of
\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}
.
We call an almost complex structure
J
on
X
compatible with an
\omega
-regularization
\mathcal{R}

as in (
6.23
) if

J\big(\textnormal{Im}\,\textnormal{d}\iota)\subset\textnormal{Im}\,\textnormal{d}\iota\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad J\!\circ\!\textnormal{d}\Psi_{k}=\textnormal{d}\Psi_{k}\!\circ\!J_{\mathcal{R};k}\big|_{\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})}~~\forall\,k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}.

(6.25)

Under the correspondence between the local and global perspectives provided by
Proposition
6.2
,
this notion is the global version of the
\mathcal{R}
-compatibility defined in Section
4.2
.

Remark 6.8
.

Let
(\Psi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}
be a refined regularization
for a transverse immersion
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
as in Definition
6.5
.
For
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, the limit set
\overline{\textnormal{Im}\,\Psi_{k}}\!-\!\textnormal{Im}\,\Psi_{k}
of
\Psi_{k}

is closed and disjoint from the closed subspace
V_{\iota}^{(k)}
of
X
.
There are thus disjoint open neighborhoods
W_{k}
of
V_{\iota}^{(k)}
and
W_{k}^{\prime}
of the limit set.
By
[
14
, Lemma 5.8]
, we can shrink the domains of
\Psi_{k}
so that

\textnormal{Im}\,\Psi_{k}\!\subset\!W_{k}
for every
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
and the new collection

(\Psi_{k})_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}
is still a refined regularization for
\iota
.
Each map
\Psi_{k}
is then closed (in addition to being open).

6.3
Constructions

We now describe the vector bundles

\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V)\!=\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(\iota)\longrightarrow X\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)\!=\!T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log\iota)\longrightarrow X,

the section
s_{\mathcal{R}}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(V)
, and
the vector bundle homomorphism
\iota_{\mathcal{R}}
on
T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log V)

constructed in Section
4.3
from the global perspective
on NC divisors and regularizations presented in Sections
6.1
and
6.2
.
We fix a symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
, a normalization
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X

of an NC symplectic divisor
V
as in Proposition
6.2
,
and a refined
\omega
-regularization
\mathcal{R}
for
\iota
in
X
as in (
6.23
).
For the purposes of constructing a complex structure on
T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log\iota)
, we also
fix an almost complex structure
J
on
X
compatible with
\omega
and
\mathcal{R}
.

With the notation as in (
6.2
) and (
6.4
), the restrictions

\Psi_{k}\!:\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\big|_{\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}-\textnormal{Im}(\iota_{k+1;k})}\longrightarrow W_{k}\!\equiv\!\Psi_{k}\Big(\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\big|_{\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}-\textnormal{Im}(\iota_{k+1;k})}\Big)

are not even
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-covering maps.
In other words, there could exist

v,v^{\prime}\in\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\big|_{\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}-\textnormal{Im}(\iota_{k+1;k})}\qquad\hbox{s.t.}\qquad\Psi_{k}(v)\!=\!\Psi_{k}(v^{\prime})\in X~~\hbox{and}~~D\sigma(v)\neq v^{\prime}~\forall\,\sigma\!\in\!\mathbb{S}_{k}\,;

see the left diagram in Figure
2
.
This makes it difficult to describe a vector bundle on
W_{k}

as a pushdown of a vector bundle on
\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\big|_{\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}-\textnormal{Im}(\iota_{k+1;k})}
.
For this reason, we shrink the last space to
an
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-invariant open subspace
\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota
so that

\Psi_{k}\!:\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota\longrightarrow U_{k}^{\circ}\!\equiv\!\Psi_{k}\big(\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota\big)

(6.26)

is an
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-covering map and
the collection
\{U_{k}^{\circ}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}

is an open covering of
X
.
Figure
2
illustrates this shrinking procedure.

Figure 2:
Shrinking regularizations.

For
k^{\prime},k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
with
k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
, let

\pi_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota\!\longrightarrow\!\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
be as in (
6.14
).
For a continuous function
\varepsilon\!:\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
, define

\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota(\varepsilon)=\big\{\!(v_{i})_{i\in[k]-[k^{\prime}]}\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota\!:\rho_{k;i}(v_{i})\!<\!\varepsilon^{2}(\pi_{k;k^{\prime}}(v)\!)~\forall i\!\in\![k]\!-\![k^{\prime}]\big\}.

By Remark
6.8
and the proof of
[
14
, Lemma 5.8]
,
there exists a smooth function
\varepsilon\!:X\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
such that

\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota(2^{k}\varepsilon\!\circ\!\iota_{k})\subset\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k}),\quad\varepsilon\big(\Psi_{k}(v)\!\big)=\varepsilon\big(\iota_{k}(\pi_{k;0}(v)\!)\!\big)~~\forall\,v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota(2^{k}\varepsilon\!\circ\!\iota_{k}),

(6.27)

and
\Psi_{k}(\overline{2^{k-1}\varepsilon\!\circ\!\iota_{k}})\!\subset\!X
is closed for every
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
.
If
V
is compact,
\varepsilon
can taken to be a constant.
Define

\begin{split}&\hskip 10.84006pt\widetilde{V}^{(k),\circ}_{\iota}=\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}\!-\!\bigcup_{\ell>k}\!\Psi_{\ell;k}\big(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{\ell;k}\iota(2^{\ell-1}\varepsilon\!\circ\!\iota_{\ell})}\big),\\
&\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota=\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota\big(2^{k}\varepsilon\!\circ\!\iota_{k}\big)|_{\widetilde{V}^{(k),\circ}_{\iota}},~~U_{k}^{\circ}=\Psi_{k}(\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota).\end{split}

(6.28)

By (
6.17
) and (
6.18
), the restriction (
6.26
)
is an
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-covering map.

For
k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
with
k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
, let

\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota=\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota\!\cap\!\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}^{-1}(\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota=\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k,k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota)=\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota)\!\cap\!\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota.

Since the map
\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
is
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}
-equivariant and
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
-invariant,
the subspace
\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k;0}\iota

is
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\times\!\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
-invariant.
The restriction

\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota

(6.29)

is an
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}
-equivariant
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
-covering map.
By the last equality in (
6.22
),

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\big(\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota\!\cap\!\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{k;0}\iota\big)=\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota\!\cap\!\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota,\quad\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}\big(\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota\!\cap\!\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{k;0}\iota\big)=\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime\prime};0}\iota\!\cap\!\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime\prime};0}\iota,\\
&\hskip 86.72377pt\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k^{\prime};k^{\prime\prime}}\big(\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota\!\cap\!\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime\prime}}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota\big)=\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime\prime};0}\iota\!\cap\!\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k^{\prime\prime};0}\iota,\end{split}

(6.30)

whenever
0\!\leq\!k^{\prime\prime}\!\leq\!k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
.

Suppose

\big\{\widetilde{\pi}_{k}\!:\widetilde{E}_{k}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\big\{\widetilde{F}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\widetilde{E}_{k}|_{\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota}\!\longrightarrow\!\widetilde{E}_{k^{\prime}}|_{\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota}\big\}_{k,k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},k^{\prime}\leq k}

is a collection of
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-equivariant (complex) vector bundles and
a collection of
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}
-equivariant
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
-invariant

(smooth) vector bundle maps that lift the covering maps (
6.29
),
restrict to an isomorphism on each fiber, and satisfy

\widetilde{F}_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{k^{\prime}}\iota\cap\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{k^{\prime\prime}}\iota}=\widetilde{F}_{k^{\prime};k^{\prime\prime}}\!\circ\!\widetilde{F}_{k;k^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{k^{\prime}}\iota\cap\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{k^{\prime\prime}}\iota}\qquad\forall\,k^{\prime\prime}\!\leq\!k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k.

(6.31)

By (
6.30
), the composition on the right-hand side above is well-defined.
Since the map (
6.29
) is an
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-covering
map,

E_{k}\!\equiv\!\widetilde{E}_{k}/\mathbb{S}_{k}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota/\mathbb{S}_{k}\!=\!U_{k}^{\circ}

is a vector bundle.
The maps
\widetilde{F}_{k;k^{\prime}}
induce vector bundle isomorphisms

F_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:E_{k}|_{U_{k}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\longrightarrow E_{k^{\prime}}|_{U_{k}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime}}^{\circ}}

covering the identity on
U_{k}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime}}^{\circ}
.
By (
6.31
),

F_{k;k^{\prime\prime}}\big|_{U_{k}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime}}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}=F_{k^{\prime};k^{\prime\prime}}\!\circ\!F_{k;k^{\prime}}\big|_{U_{k}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime}}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime\prime}}^{\circ}}.

We can thus form a vector bundle

\begin{split}&\pi\!:E\!\equiv\!\bigg(\bigsqcup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}\!\!\!\!E_{k}\bigg)\!\!\Big/\!\!\sim\longrightarrow X,\quad\pi\big([v]\big)=\Psi_{k}\big(\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(v)\!\big)~~\forall~k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~v\!\in\!E_{k},\\
&\qquad E_{k}\big|_{U_{k}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\ni w\sim F_{k;k^{\prime}}(w)\in E_{k^{\prime}}\big|_{U_{k}^{\circ}\cap U_{k^{\prime}}^{\circ}}\quad\forall~k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k\,.\end{split}

(6.32)

A collection
\{\widetilde{s}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}
of
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-equivariant sections of
the vector bundles
\widetilde{E}_{k}
such that

\widetilde{F}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\widetilde{s}_{k}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{k^{\prime}}\iota}=\widetilde{s}_{k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{k^{\prime}}\iota}\qquad\forall~k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k,

(6.33)

determines a section
s
on the corresponding vector bundle
E
in (
6.32
) so that

s\big([v]\big)=\big[\widetilde{s}_{k}(v)\!\big]\qquad\forall~k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~v\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota\,.

If
E^{\prime}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is a (complex) vector bundle, the
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-action on
\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota

lifts to an action on the vector bundle

\widetilde{\pi}_{k}^{\prime}\!:\widetilde{E}_{k}^{\prime}\!\equiv\!\Psi_{k}^{*}E^{\prime}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota

so that
\widetilde{E}_{k}^{\prime}/\mathbb{S}_{k}\!=\!E^{\prime}|_{U_{k}^{\circ}}
.
The covering maps (
6.29
) lift to

\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}
-equivariant
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
-invariant
vector bundle maps

\widetilde{F}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{\prime}\!:\widetilde{E}_{k}^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota}\longrightarrow\widetilde{E}_{k^{\prime}}^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota}

that restrict to an isomorphism on each fiber and satisfy (
6.31
)
with
\widetilde{F}
replaced by
\widetilde{F}^{\prime}
so that the corresponding vector bundle (
6.32
)
is canonically identified with
E^{\prime}
.
A collection

\big\{\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}\!:\widetilde{E}_{k}\!\longrightarrow\!\widetilde{E}_{k}^{\prime}\big\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}

of
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-equivariant vector bundle homomorphisms covering the identity on
\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota

such that

\widetilde{F}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{k^{\prime}}\iota}=\widetilde{\Phi}_{k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\widetilde{F}_{k;k^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{k^{\prime}}\iota}\qquad\forall~k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k,

(6.34)

determines a vector bundle homomorphism
\Phi\!:E\!\longrightarrow\!E^{\prime}
covering
the identity on
X
so that

\Phi\big([w]\!\big)=\big[\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}(w)\!\big]\qquad\forall~k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~w\!\in\!\widetilde{E}_{k}\,.

For every
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, let

\begin{split}\widetilde{\pi}_{k}\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};k}(\iota)&=\bigg(\!\!\pi_{k;0}^{*}\bigotimes_{i\in[k]}\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\!\bigg)\!\bigg|_{\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota}\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota,\\
\widetilde{\pi}_{k}\!:T_{\mathcal{R};k}(-\log\iota)&=\big(\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}T\widetilde{V}^{(k),{\circ}}_{\iota}\big)\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}\!\oplus\!\big(\mathcal{N}^{\circ}_{k;0}\iota\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{[k]}\big)\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota\,.\end{split}

(6.35)

The complex structures
\mathfrak{i}_{k;i}
on
\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}
encoded in
\mathcal{R}
determine
a complex structure on the complex line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};k}(\iota)
.
The almost complex structure
J_{\iota;k}
on
\widetilde{V}_{k}
, induced by
J
,
and the standard complex structure on
\mathbb{C}^{[k]}

determine a complex structure on the vector bundle
T_{\mathcal{R};k}(-\log\iota)
.
The
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-action on
\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota
naturally lifts to both bundles.

Let
k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
with
k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
and

\Pi_{k}\!:\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;0}\longrightarrow\bigotimes_{i\in[k]}\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)},\qquad\Pi_{k}\big(\!(v_{i})_{i\in[k]}\big)=\bigotimes_{i\in[k]}\!v_{i}\,.

We denote by
\nabla^{(k)}
and
\nabla^{(k;k^{\prime})}

the connections on
\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\!\approx\!\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota
and

\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\approx\!\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota

induced by the connections
\nabla^{(k;i)}
on the direct summands of these vector bundles.
We write an element
v\!\equiv\!(v_{i})_{i\in[k]}
of
\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota
as

v=(v_{k;k^{\prime}},v_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c})\quad\hbox{with}\quad v_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\equiv\!(v_{i})_{i\in[k]-[k^{\prime}]}\!\in\!\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota~~\hbox{and}~~v_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\!\equiv\!(v_{i})_{i\in[k^{\prime}]}\in\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}\iota

below.
Let

\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(k)};v}\!:T_{\pi_{k;0}(v)}\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\longrightarrow T_{v}(\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota)\quad\hbox{and}\quad\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(k;k^{\prime})};v_{k;k^{\prime}}}\!:T_{\pi_{k;k^{\prime}}(v_{k;k^{\prime}})}\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\longrightarrow T_{v_{k;k^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{N}_{k;k^{\prime}}\iota)

be the injective homomorphisms as in (
2.6
) corresponding
to the connections
\nabla^{(k)}
and
\nabla^{(k;k^{\prime})}
.

By the first equation in (
6.28
),

v_{i}\neq 0\qquad\forall~i\!\in\![k]\!-\![k^{\prime}],~(v_{j})_{j\in[k]}\!\in\!\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota\subset\bigoplus_{j\in[k]}\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(j)}.

Since
\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
is a product Hermitian isomorphism, the map

\displaystyle\widetilde{\theta}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};k}(\iota)\big|_{\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};k^{\prime}}(\iota)\big|_{\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota},

(6.36)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\theta}_{k;k^{\prime}}\big(v,\Pi_{k}(v_{k;k^{\prime}},w_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c})\!\big)=\Big(\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}(v),\Pi_{k^{\prime}}\big(\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}(v_{k;k^{\prime}},w_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c})\!\big)\!\Big),

is a well-defined homomorphism of complex line bundles that
lifts the covering map (
6.29
) and
restricts to an isomorphism on each fiber.
The map

\displaystyle\widetilde{\vartheta}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!:T_{\mathcal{R};k}(-\log\iota)\big|_{\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota}\longrightarrow T_{\mathcal{R};k}(-\log\iota)\big|_{\mathcal{N}^{k}_{k^{\prime};0}\iota},

(6.37)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widetilde{\vartheta}_{k;k^{\prime}}\big(\!(v,w)\!\oplus\!\big(v,(c_{i})_{i\in[k]}\big)\!\!\big)=\Big(\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}(v),\textnormal{d}_{v_{k;k^{\prime}}}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\big(\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(k;k^{\prime})};v_{k;k^{\prime}}}(w)\!+\!\!\sum_{i\in[k]-[k^{\prime}]}\!\!\!\!\!\!c_{i}v_{i}\!\big)\!\!\Big)&\\
\!\oplus\!\big(\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}(v_{k;k^{\prime}}),(c_{i})_{i\in[k^{\prime}]}\big)&,\end{split}

is similarly a well-defined homomorphism of vector bundles that
lifts the covering map (
6.29
) and
restricts to an isomorphism on each fiber.
Since
J
is an
\mathcal{R}
-compatible almost complex structure on
X
, this homomorphism is
\mathbb{C}
-linear.

By the commutativity of the diagrams in Figure
2
,
the bundle homomorphisms (
6.36
) and (
6.37
)
are
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}
-equivariant
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
-invariant
.
By (
6.22
), the collections
\{\widetilde{\theta}_{k;k^{\prime}}\}_{k^{\prime}\leq k}

and
\{\widetilde{\vartheta}_{k;k^{\prime}}\}_{k^{\prime}\leq k}
satisfy (
6.31
)
with
\widetilde{F}
replaced by
\widetilde{\theta}
and
\widetilde{\vartheta}
.
The first collection thus determines a complex line bundle

\begin{split}&\pi\!:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(\iota)\!=\!\bigg(\bigsqcup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}\!\!\!\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};k}(\iota)/\mathbb{S}_{k}\!\!\bigg)\!\Big/\!\!\!\sim\,\longrightarrow X,~~\pi\big([w]\big)=\Psi_{k}\big(\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(w)\!\big)~\forall\,k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},\,w\!\in\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};k}(\iota),\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt[w]\sim\big[\widetilde{\theta}_{k;k^{\prime}}(w)\!\big]\quad\forall~k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k,~w\!\in\!\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};k}(\iota)\big|_{\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota}.\end{split}

The second collection similarly determines a complex vector bundle

\begin{split}&\pi\!:T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log\iota)\!=\!\bigg(\bigsqcup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}}\!\!\!\!T_{\mathcal{R};k}(-\log\iota)/\mathbb{S}_{k}\!\!\bigg)\!\Big/\!\!\sim\,\,\longrightarrow X,\\
&\hskip 108.405pt\pi\big([w]\big)=\Psi_{k}\big(\widetilde{\pi}_{k}(w)\!\big)~\forall\,k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},\,w\!\in\!T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log\iota),\\
&\hskip-3.61371pt[w]\sim\big[\widetilde{\vartheta}_{k;k^{\prime}}(w)\!\big]\quad\forall~k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0},~k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k,~w\!\in\!T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log\iota)\big|_{\mathcal{N}^{k^{\prime}}_{k;0}\iota}.\end{split}

The smooth sections

\widetilde{s}_{k}\!:\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};k}(\iota),\qquad\widetilde{s}_{k}(v)=\big(v,\Pi_{k}(v)\!\big),

are
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-equivariant and satisfy (
6.33
) with
\widetilde{F}
replaced by
\widetilde{\theta}
.
They thus determine a section
s_{\mathcal{R}}
of the complex line bundle
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{R};X}(\iota)
.
The smooth bundle homomorphisms

\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}\!:T_{\mathcal{R};k}(-\log\iota)\longrightarrow\Psi_{k}^{*}TX,\quad\widetilde{\Phi}_{k}\Big(\!(v,w)\!\oplus\!\big(v,(c_{i})_{i\in[k]}\big)\!\!\Big)=\textnormal{d}_{v}\Psi_{k}\!\Big(\textnormal{h}_{\nabla^{(k)};v}(w)\!+\!\!\sum_{i\in[k]}c_{i}v_{i}\!\Big),

are
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-equivariant.
By (
6.29
), these bundle homomorphisms satisfy (
6.34
)
with
\widetilde{F}
replaced by
\widetilde{\vartheta}
.
They thus determine a vector bundle homomorphism

\iota_{\mathcal{R}}\!:T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log\iota)\longrightarrow TX\,.

6.4
Proof of Theorem
1.2
(5)

Let
(X,\omega)
,

V\!\subset\!X
,
\iota\!:\widetilde{V}\!\longrightarrow\!X
,
\mathcal{R}
, and
J
be as in Section
6.3
.
We denote the curvature 2-form of a connection
\nabla
on
a complex vector bundle
E\!\longrightarrow\!Y
over a smooth manifold by

F^{\nabla}\in\Gamma\big(Y;\Lambda^{2}(T^{*}Y)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\!\textnormal{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(E)\!\big).

For
i\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, we define

\displaystyle c_{i}(\nabla)\in\Gamma\big(Y;\Lambda^{2i}(T^{*}Y)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\!\textnormal{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(E)\!\big)\quad\hbox{and}\quad c(\nabla)\in\bigoplus_{i=0}^{\infty}\Gamma\big(Y;\Lambda^{2i}(T^{*}Y)\!\otimes_{\mathbb{R}}\!\textnormal{End}_{\mathbb{C}}(E)\!\big)

\displaystyle\hbox{by}\qquad 1\!+\!c_{1}(\nabla)\!+\!c_{2}(\nabla)\!+\!\ldots\equiv c(\nabla)\equiv\det\!{}_{\mathbb{C}}\!\Big(\!I\!+\!\frac{\mathfrak{i}}{2\pi}F^{\nabla}\!\Big).

By
[
29
, p206]
,

\big[c^{\nabla}\!(E)\big]=c(E)\in H^{2*}_{\textnormal{deR}}(Y)\!\equiv\!\bigoplus_{i=0}^{\infty}H^{2i}_{\textnormal{deR}}(Y).

(6.38)

We compare
c(TX)
and
c(TX(-\log V)\!)\!=\!c(TX(-\log\iota)\!)

at the level of differential forms, which can be done locally;
see Proposition
6.11
.
This essentially reduces the computation to the SC case.
The de Rham cohomology version of (
1.5
),
which is equivalent to (
1.5
) itself,
follows from (
6.38
),
Lemma
6.10
, and Proposition
6.11
.

A connection
\nabla
on a complex line bundle
\pi\!:L\!\longrightarrow\!Y
determines
a horizontal tangent subbundle
TL^{\textnormal{hor}}\!\subset\!TL
and
an
\mathbb{R}
-valued
angular 1-form

\alpha_{\nabla}
on
L\!-\!Y
.
The latter is characterized by

\ker\alpha_{\nabla}=\big(TL^{\textnormal{hor}}\!\oplus\!\mathbb{R}\zeta_{L}\big)\big|_{L-Y}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\alpha_{\nabla}\bigg(\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}\theta}\textnormal{e}^{\mathfrak{i}\theta}v\Big|_{\theta=0}\bigg)=1.

By the proof of
[
38
, Lemma 1.1]
,

\textnormal{d}\alpha_{\nabla}=\pi^{*}\eta_{\nabla}\big|_{L-Y}

(6.39)

for some 2-form
\eta_{\nabla}
on
Y
.

Remark 6.9
.

If
\nabla
is compatible with a Hermitian metric
\rho
on
L
,
then the connection 1-form
\theta_{1}^{1}
in the proof of Lemma
2.2

is purely imaginary.
In the local chart of this proof,
the angular 1-form
\alpha_{\nabla}
of
\nabla
is then given by

\alpha_{\nabla}\big|_{(y,z)}=-\mathfrak{i}\frac{\textnormal{d}z}{z}-\mathfrak{i}\pi^{*}\theta_{1}^{1}.

Thus,
\eta_{\nabla}\!=\!-\mathfrak{i}F^{\nabla}
above.

Let
\beta\!:\mathbb{R}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0}
be as in (
5.21
).
For a Hermitian metric
\rho
and a smooth function
\varepsilon\!:Y\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
, define

\beta_{\rho;\varepsilon}\!:L\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0},\qquad\beta_{\rho;\varepsilon}(v)=\beta\big(2\varepsilon(\pi(v)\!)^{-2}\rho(v)\!\big).

(6.40)

By (
6.39
), the 2-form

\tau_{\rho;\nabla;\varepsilon}\equiv-\frac{1}{2\pi}\textnormal{d}\big(\beta_{\rho;\varepsilon}\alpha_{\nabla}\big)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\Big(\!\textnormal{d}\beta_{\rho;\varepsilon}\!\wedge\!\alpha_{\nabla}\!+\!\beta_{\rho;\varepsilon}\pi^{*}\eta_{\nabla}\!\Big)

is well-defined on the entire total space of
L
.
This closed 2-form is compactly supported in the vertical direction and

\int_{\pi^{-1}(y)}\tau_{\rho;\nabla;\varepsilon}=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\bigg(\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\textnormal{d}\beta_{\rho;\varepsilon}\!\!\bigg)\bigg(\int_{S^{1}}\!\!\textnormal{d}\theta\!\!\bigg)=1\qquad\forall~y\!\in\!Y.

(6.41)

Thus,
\tau_{\rho;\nabla;\varepsilon}
represents the Thom class of the complex line bundle
\pi\!:L\!\longrightarrow\!Y
;
see
[
5
, p64]
.

For each
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
, let
\varepsilon_{k}\!:\widetilde{V}^{(k)}\!\longrightarrow\!\mathbb{R}^{+}
be the composition
of the function
\varepsilon
in (
6.28
) with
\iota_{k}
.
For each
i\!\in\![k]
, let

\pi_{k;i}\!:\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\!\approx\!\mathcal{N}_{k;0}\iota\longrightarrow\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}

be the component projection.
For
k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, we denote by

\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\in\!\Omega^{2k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{N}_{k;0})
the
k^{\prime}
-th elementary symmetric polynomial
on the set
\{\pi_{k;i}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;i};\nabla^{(k;i)};2\varepsilon_{k}}\}_{i\in[k]}
, i.e.

\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k^{\prime}}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i_{1},\ldots,i_{k^{\prime}}\in[k]\\
i_{1}<\ldots<i_{k^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\hskip-14.45377pt\big(\pi_{k;i_{1}}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;i_{1}};\nabla^{(k;i_{1})};2\varepsilon_{k}}\big)\!\wedge\!\ldots\!\wedge\!\big(\pi_{k;i_{k^{\prime}}}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;i_{k^{\prime}}};\nabla^{(k;{i_{k^{\prime}}})};2\varepsilon_{k}}\big).

This
2k^{\prime}
-form on the total space of
\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}
is
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-invariant and closed.
The
2k
-form

\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}\equiv\underset{i\in[k]}{\Lambda}\pi_{k;i}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;i};\nabla^{(k;i)};2\varepsilon_{k}}\equiv\big(\pi_{k;1}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;1};\nabla^{(k;1)};2\varepsilon_{k}}\big)\!\wedge\!\ldots\!\wedge\big(\pi_{k;k}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;k};\nabla^{(k;k)};2\varepsilon_{k}}\big)

is in addition compactly supported in the vertical direction.
By (
6.41
),
it represents the Thom class of the complex vector bundle

\pi_{k;0}\!:\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\!\longrightarrow\!\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
.

For an open subset
U\!\subset\!X
such that

\begin{split}&\hskip 72.26999pt\Psi_{k}^{-1}(U)=\widetilde{U}_{0}\!\sqcup\!\widetilde{U}_{1}\!\sqcup\!\ldots\!\sqcup\!\widetilde{U}_{\ell}\qquad\hbox{with}\\
&\widetilde{U}_{0}\subset\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\!-\!\overline{\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}(\varepsilon_{k})}\quad\hbox{and}\quad\Psi_{k}\!:\widetilde{U}_{i}\longrightarrow U~~\hbox{a diffeomorphism}~~\forall~i\!\in\![\ell],\end{split}

(6.42)

we define

\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}\big|_{U}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\!\big\{\!\!\{\Psi_{k}|_{\widetilde{U}_{i}}\}^{-1}\!\big\}^{\!*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}\in\Omega^{2k}(U;\mathbb{R}).

(6.43)

Since
\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}
vanishes on
\widetilde{U}_{0}
,
\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}|_{U}
does not depend
on an admissible decomposition of
\Psi_{k}^{-1}(U)
as above.
Thus,
\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}|_{U}
is well-defined and

\big(\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}|_{U}\big)\big|_{U\cap U^{\prime}}=\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}\big|_{U\cap U^{\prime}}=\big(\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}|_{U^{\prime}}\big)\big|_{U\cap U^{\prime}}

for all open subsets
U,U^{\prime}\!\subset\!X
with admissible decompositions.
By Lemma
6.12
at the end of this section, such open subsets cover
X
.
We thus obtain a closed
2k
-form
\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}
on
X
.
If
\mu
is a closed form on
X
, then

\int_{X}(\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k})\!\wedge\!\mu=\int_{\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}}\!\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}\!\wedge\!(\Psi_{k}^{*}\mu)=\int_{\widetilde{V}^{(k)}}\iota_{k}^{*}\mu\,;

(6.44)

the first equality above holds for any differential form
\mu
on
X
.
The next straightforward lemma is also proved at the end of this section.

Lemma 6.10
.

With the notation as above,

\tau_{k}\!\equiv\!\frac{1}{k!}\Psi_{k*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k}=\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V^{(k)}_{\iota}]_{X}\big),\quad\Psi_{k}^{*}\tau_{k^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}}=\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k^{\prime}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}}\qquad\forall~k,k^{\prime}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}.

(6.45)

Proposition 6.11
.

There exist connections
\nabla
and
\nabla^{\prime}
in the complex vector bundles

(TX,J)
and
(T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log\iota),\mathfrak{i}_{\mathcal{R},J})
so that

c(\nabla)=c(\nabla^{\prime})\big(1\!+\!\tau_{1}\!+\!\tau_{2}\!+\!\cdots\big).

(6.46)

Proof.

We construct
\nabla
and
\nabla^{\prime}
using the global perspective of
Section
6.3
.
For
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
and
i\!\in\![k]
, let

\beta_{k;i}\!\equiv\!\beta_{\rho_{k;i};\varepsilon_{k}}\!:\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}^{\geq 0}

be a smooth function as in (
6.40
) and

\Phi_{k;i}\!:\big(\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\!-\!\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\big)\!\times\!\mathbb{C}\longrightarrow\pi^{*}\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)},\qquad\Phi_{k;i}(v,c)=cv,

where
\pi\!:\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\!\longrightarrow\!\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
is the bundle projection.
We define a connection
\nabla^{\prime(k;i)}
in the trivial complex line bundle

\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
over
\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}
by

\nabla^{\prime(k;i)}=\beta_{k;i}\textnormal{d}\!+\!(1\!-\!\beta_{k;i})\Phi_{k;i}^{\,*}\pi^{*}\nabla^{(k;i)}\,;

the last summand above is well-defined because
\beta_{k;i}\!=\!1

in a neighborhood of the zero section
\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}
.
We note that

\begin{split}F^{\nabla^{{}^{\prime}(k;i)}}&=\beta_{k;i}F^{\textnormal{d}}\!+\!(1\!-\!\beta_{k;i})F^{\Phi_{k;i}^{\,*}\pi^{*}\nabla^{(k;i)}}\!+\!\textnormal{d}(1\!-\!\beta_{k;i})\!\wedge\!\big(\Phi_{k;i}^{\,*}\pi^{*}\nabla^{(k;i)}\!-\!\textnormal{d}\big)\\
&=0\!+\!(1\!-\!\beta_{k;i})\pi^{*}F^{\nabla^{(k;i)}}\!-\!\mathfrak{i}\,\textnormal{d}\beta_{k;i}\!\wedge\!\alpha_{\nabla^{(k;i)}}=\pi^{*}F^{\nabla^{(k;i)}}\!+\!2\pi\mathfrak{i}\tau_{\rho_{k;i};\nabla^{(k;i)};\varepsilon_{k}};\end{split}

(6.47)

the last two equalities follow from Remarks
2.4
and
6.9
.

For each
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{\geq 0}
, the connections
\nabla^{(k;i)}
on
the complex line bundles
\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}
determine a splitting

T\big(\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}\big)=\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}T\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}\!\oplus\!\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\iota}_{k;k-1}^{(i)}\,.

(6.48)

Let
r\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
be such that
V_{\iota}^{(r+1)}\!=\!\emptyset
.
By Definition
6.7
,
the vector bundle isomorphisms (
6.20
) are product Hermitian isomorphisms
for all
k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
.
Furthermore,
\varepsilon_{k}\!=\!\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
.
Starting with a
J
-compatible connection
on
TV_{\iota}^{(r)}
and possibly shrinking the domains of the regularizations
\Psi_{k}
,
we can thus inductively construct a connection
\nabla
on
TX
so that
\{\textnormal{d}\Psi_{k}\}^{*}\nabla

agrees with the restriction of the connection

\widetilde{\nabla}^{(k)}\equiv\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}\nabla^{T\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}}\!\oplus\!\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\!\big(\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}\nabla^{(k;i)}\!+\!\mathfrak{i}(\beta_{\rho_{k;i};2\varepsilon_{k}}\!-\!\beta_{\rho_{k;i};\varepsilon_{k}})\alpha_{\nabla^{(k;i)}}\!\big)

on (
6.48
) to
\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})
for every
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
.
By Remark
6.9
,
the curvature of the last summand
\widetilde{\nabla}^{(k;i)}
above satisfies

\begin{split}1\!+\!\frac{\mathfrak{i}}{2\pi}F^{\widetilde{\nabla}^{(k;i)}}&=\Big(1\!+\!\frac{\mathfrak{i}}{2\pi}\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}F^{\nabla^{(k;i)}}\!-\!\tau_{\rho_{k;i};\nabla^{(k;i)};\varepsilon_{k}}\Big)\!\big(1\!+\!\pi_{k;i}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;i};\nabla^{(k;i)};2\varepsilon_{k}}\big)\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt-\frac{1}{4\pi}\textnormal{d}\big(\!(1\!-\!\beta_{\rho_{k;i};\varepsilon_{k}})\alpha_{\nabla^{(k;i)}}\big)\!\wedge\!\textnormal{d}\big(\beta_{\rho_{k;i};2\varepsilon_{k}}\alpha_{\nabla^{(k;i)}}\big);\end{split}

the term on the last line above vanishes because
\beta_{\rho_{k;i};\varepsilon_{k}}\!\equiv\!1

on
\textnormal{supp}\,\beta_{\rho_{k;i};2\varepsilon_{k}}
.
Along with (
6.47
), this gives

\begin{split}\Psi_{k}^{*}c(\nabla)\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}&=\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}c\big(\nabla^{T\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}}\big)\!\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\!\!\big(\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}c(\nabla^{\prime(k;i)})\big(1\!+\!\pi_{k;i}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;i};\nabla^{(k;i)};2\varepsilon_{k}}\big)\!\big)\!\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}\\
&=\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}c\big(\nabla^{T\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}}\big)\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}\!\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{k}\!\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}c(\nabla^{\prime(k;i)})\!\!\bigg)\Psi_{k}^{*}\big(1\!+\!\tau_{1}\!+\!\tau_{2}\!+\!\ldots\big)\!\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota};\end{split}

(6.49)

the last equality follows from the second statement of Lemma
6.10
.

By (
6.24
), the connection

\widetilde{\nabla}^{\prime(k)}\equiv\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}\nabla^{T\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}\!\oplus\!\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}\widetilde{\nabla}^{\prime(k;i)}\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}

on the complex vector bundle (
6.35
) is
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-equivariant.
Since
\varepsilon_{k}\!=\!\varepsilon_{k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
and
\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
is a product Hermitian
isomorphisms,
the bundle isomorphisms (
6.24
) intertwine these connections.
Thus, they determine a connection
\nabla^{\prime}
on the complex vector bundle
T_{\mathcal{R}}X(-\log\iota)
.
Since

\Psi_{k}^{*}c(\nabla^{\prime})\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}=c\big(\widetilde{\nabla}^{\prime(k)}\big)=\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}c\big(\nabla^{T\widetilde{V}^{(k)}_{\iota}}\big)\big|_{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota}\prod_{i=1}^{k}\!\pi_{k;0}^{\,*}c(\nabla^{\prime(k;i)}),

the identity (
6.46
) follows from (
6.49
).
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
k\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}^{+}
.
Every point
x\!\in\!X
admits a neighborhood
U
as in (
6.42
).

Proof.

Let
\Psi_{k}^{-1}(x)\!=\!\{\widetilde{w}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{w}_{\ell}\}
.
For each
i\!\in\![\ell]
, let
U_{i}\!\subset\!\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}
be a neighborhood
of
\pi_{k;0}(\widetilde{w}_{i})
so that the restriction

\Psi_{k}\!:\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\big|_{U_{i}}\longrightarrow X

is a diffeomorphism onto its image; see Definition
6.3
.
By Remark
6.8
,

W\equiv X\!-\!\Psi_{k}\big(\overline{\mathcal{N}\iota_{k}(\varepsilon_{k})}\big|_{\widetilde{V}_{\iota}^{(k)}-U_{1}\cup\ldots\cup U_{\ell}}\big)

is an open subspace of
X
.
The open neighborhood

U\equiv W\cap\bigcap_{i=1}^{\ell}\!\Psi_{k}\big(\textnormal{Dom}(\Psi_{k})\big|_{U_{i}}\big)

of
x\!\in\!X
then satisfies the condition in (
6.42
).
∎

Proof of Lemma
6.10
.

Let
\mu
be a closed form on
X
.
We show that

\int_{V^{(k)}_{\iota}}\alpha=\int_{X}\tau_{k}\!\wedge\!\mu.

(6.50)

Since
\iota_{k}\!:\widetilde{V}^{(k)}\!\longrightarrow\!V^{(k)}
is a
k!
-covering map outside of
a codimension 2 subspace,

\int_{V^{(k)}_{\iota}}\alpha=\frac{1}{k!}\int_{\widetilde{V}^{(k)}}\iota_{k}^{*}\alpha\,.

Combining this with (
6.44
), we obtain (
6.50
) and establish the
first statement in (
6.45
).

If
k^{\prime}\!>\!k
, the left-hand side of the second identity in (
6.45
) vanishes
because

\textnormal{supp}\,\tau_{k^{\prime}}\subset\Psi_{k^{\prime}}\big(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}(\varepsilon_{k}/2)}\big)\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\Psi_{k^{\prime}}\big(\overline{\mathcal{N}_{k;0}(\varepsilon_{k}/2)}\big)\!\cap\!\Psi_{k}(\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ})=\emptyset.

The right-hand side of the second identity in (
6.45
) vanishes by definition
in this case.

Suppose
k^{\prime}\!\leq\!k
.
Let
S_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\subset\!\mathbb{S}_{k}
be a collection of representatives for
the right cosets of
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}\!\times\!\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
in
\mathbb{S}_{k}

preserving the order of the first
k^{\prime}\!
elements.
Since (
6.26
) is an
\mathbb{S}_{k}
-covering map, every point of
U_{k}^{\circ}

has a neighborhood
U
so that

\Psi_{k}^{-1}(U)=\bigsqcup_{\sigma\in\mathbb{S}_{k}}\!\sigma(W)\subset\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota

for some open subset
W\!\subset\!\mathcal{N}_{k;0}^{\circ}\iota
.
Since
\Psi_{k}\!=\!\Psi_{k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
and
\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
is

\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}^{c}
-invariant,
(
6.18
) implies that

\Psi_{k^{\prime}}^{-1}(U)=\bigcup_{\varpi\in\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}}\bigsqcup_{\sigma\in S_{k;k^{\prime}}}\!\!\!\!\!\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\big(\varpi\sigma(W)\!\big).

Since
\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
is
\mathbb{S}_{k;k^{\prime}}
-equivariant and
\widetilde{\tau}_{k^{\prime};k^{\prime}}
is
\mathbb{S}_{k^{\prime}}
-invariant,

\Psi_{k}^{*}\tau_{k^{\prime}}=\frac{1}{k!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k;k^{\prime}}}\sum_{\varpi\in\mathbb{S}_{k^{\prime}}}\!\{D\sigma\}^{\!*}\{\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\}^{\!*}\{D\varpi\}^{*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k^{\prime};k^{\prime}}=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k;k^{\prime}}}\!\!\!\{D\sigma\}^{\!*}\{\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}\}^{\!*}\widetilde{\tau}_{k^{\prime};k^{\prime}}\,.

Since
\epsilon_{k}\!=\!\epsilon_{k^{\prime}}\!\circ\!\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
and
\mathfrak{D}\Psi_{k;k^{\prime}}
is a product Hermitian
isomorphism, it follows that

\Psi_{k}^{*}\tau_{k^{\prime}}=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{k;k^{\prime}}}\!\!\!\{D\sigma\}^{\!*}\bigg(\underset{i\in[k^{\prime}]}{\Lambda}\pi_{k;i}^{\,*}\tau_{\rho_{k;i};\nabla^{(k;i)};2\varepsilon_{k}}\!\!\bigg).

Since
D\sigma
is also a product Hermitian isomorphism,
the last expression equals
\widetilde{\tau}_{k;k^{\prime}}
.
∎

Remark 6.13
.

If
X
and
V^{(k)}_{\iota}
are not compact,
the above proof of the first identity in (
6.45
) goes through
if
\mu
is compactly supported.
If
X
is not compact, but
V^{(k)}_{\iota}
is compact,
then this identity holds in the compactly de Rham cohomology
of
X
, as well as in the usual de Rham cohomology of
X
.

7
On the sharpness of (
1.5
) and (
1.9
)

We conclude by establishing the remaining statement of
Corollary
1.4
and showing that (
1.9
) does not need
to hold in
H^{*}(\widetilde{X};\mathbb{Z})
for arbitrary NC divisors.
The latter implies that (
1.5
) does not hold in
H^{*}(X;\mathbb{Z})

for arbitrary NC divisors either.

Continuing with the notation and setup as in Lemma
1.3
,
we denote by

\widetilde{\iota}\!:\mathbb{E}\longrightarrow\widetilde{X}\qquad\hbox{and}\qquad\widetilde{\pi}_{0}\!:\gamma\longrightarrow\mathbb{E}

the inclusion map and the tautological line bundle, respectively.
For
\mu\!\in\!H_{*}(V^{(r)};R)
, we denote by

\mathbb{E}|_{\mu}\!\in\!H_{*}(\mathbb{E};R)

the fiber product of
\mu
with
\pi|_{\mathbb{E}}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

If
R
is a commutative ring with unity,

\displaystyle\ker\!\big(\pi_{*}\!:H_{*}(\widetilde{X};R)\!\longrightarrow\!H_{*}(X;R)\!\big)=\widetilde{\iota}_{*}\!\big(\!\ker\!\big(\pi_{*}\!:H_{*}(\mathbb{E};R)\!\longrightarrow\!H_{*}(V^{(r)};R)\!\big)\!\big)\quad\hbox{and}

(7.1)

\displaystyle\ker\!\big(\pi_{*}\!:H_{*}(\mathbb{E};R)\!\longrightarrow\!H_{*}(V^{(r)};R)\!\big)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r-1}\!\big\{\!c_{1}(\gamma)^{r-1-i}\!\cap\!\big(\mathbb{E}|_{\mu_{i}}\big)\!:\mu_{i}\!\in\!H_{*-2i}(V^{(r)};R)\!\big\}.

(7.2)

Proof.

We omit the coefficient ring
R
below.
Let
U\!\subset\!X
be an open neighborhood of
Y\!\equiv\!V^{(r)}

which deformation retracts onto
Y
and
\widetilde{U}\!\subset\!\pi^{-1}(U)
.
Since
\mathbb{E}
is a deformation retract of
\widetilde{U}
, the Mayer-Vietoris sequences
for
\widetilde{X}\!=\!(\widetilde{X}\!-\!\mathbb{E})\!\cup\!\widetilde{U}
and
X\!=\!(X\!-\!Y)\!\cup\!U

induce a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{*}(\widetilde{U}\!-\!\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
id
\textstyle{H_{*}(\widetilde{X}\!-\!\mathbb{E})\!\oplus\!H_{*}(\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\textnormal{id}\oplus\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{H_{*}(\widetilde{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{*}}
\textstyle{H_{*-1}(\widetilde{U}\!-\!\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
id
\textstyle{\ldots}
\textstyle{\ldots\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{*}(U\!-\!Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{*}(X\!-\!Y)\!\oplus\!H_{*}(Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{*}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{H_{*-1}(U\!-\!Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\ldots}

of exact sequences of
R
-modules.
This gives (
7.1
).

For every
y\!\in\!Y
, the collection

\{1|_{\mathbb{E}_{y}},c_{1}(\gamma)|_{\mathbb{E}_{y}},\ldots,c_{1}(\gamma)^{r-1}|_{\mathbb{E}_{y}}\}

is a basis for
H^{*}(\mathbb{E}_{y})
.
By
[
34
, Theorem 5.7.9]
, the homomorphism

H_{*}(\mathbb{E})\longrightarrow\bigoplus_{i=0}^{r-1}\!H_{*-2i}(Y),\qquad\widetilde{\mu}\longrightarrow\big(\pi_{*}\big(\!c_{1}(\gamma)^{i}\!\cap\!\widetilde{\mu}\big)_{i=0,\ldots,r-1}\big),

is thus an isomorphism.
This implies (
7.2
).
∎

For each
k\!\in\![r\!-\!1]
, let

\widetilde{\eta}_{k}=\pi^{*}\big(\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(k)}]_{X})\!\big)\!-\!\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}\big([\overline{V}^{(k)}]_{\widetilde{X}}\big)\in H^{2k}(\widetilde{X};R).

By Lemma
7.1
, there exist

\eta_{k;i}^{c}\!\in\!H^{2(k-i)}(V^{(r)};R)

with
i\!\in\![k]
so that

\widetilde{\eta}_{k}\!\cap\!\big[\widetilde{X}\big]=\sum_{i=1}^{k}\widetilde{\iota}_{*}\!\Big(\!c_{1}(\gamma)^{i-1}\!\cap\!\big(\mathbb{E}|_{\eta_{k;i}^{c}\cap[V^{(r)}]}\big)\!\!\Big)\in H_{*}(\widetilde{X};R).

(7.3)

For example,

\big(\pi^{*}\big(\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(1)}]_{X})\!\big)\!\big)\!\cap\!\big[\widetilde{X}\big]=[\overline{V}^{(1)}]_{\widetilde{X}}\!+\!r[\mathbb{E}]_{\widetilde{X}}\qquad\hbox{if}~r\!\geq\!2;

(7.4)

the coefficient
\eta_{1;1}^{c}\!=\!r
above is obtained by intersecting both sides with

\widetilde{\iota}_{*}(c_{1}(\gamma)^{r-2}\!\cap\!\mathbb{E}_{y})
.

We set

\begin{split}\mathcal{PD}(V)&=1\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V^{(1)}]_{X}\big)\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V^{(2)}]_{X}\big)\!+\!\ldots\in\bigoplus_{i=0}^{\infty}H^{2i}(X;R),\\
\eta_{i}^{c}&=\eta_{i;i}^{c}\!+\!\ldots\!+\!\eta_{r-1;i}^{c}\in\bigoplus_{k=0}^{\infty}H^{2k}(V^{(r)};R)\quad\forall\,i\!\in\![r\!-\!1],\qquad\eta_{r}^{c}=1.\end{split}

The identity (
1.9
) is equivalent to

\begin{split}1\!-\!\frac{c(T\widetilde{X})}{\pi^{*}c(TX)(1\!+\!\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}([\mathbb{E}]_{\widetilde{X}})\!)}=&\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}\Big(\widetilde{\iota}_{*}\!\Big(\!c_{1}(\gamma)^{i-1}\!\cap\!\big(\mathbb{E}|_{\eta_{i}^{c}\cap\mathcal{PD}(V)^{-1}\cap[V^{(r)}]}\big)\!\!\Big)\!\!\Big)\\
&\hskip 36.135pt+\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}\Big(\pi^{*}\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big(\iota_{*}\big(\mathcal{PD}(V)^{-1}\!\cap\![V^{(r)}]\big)\!\big)\!\Big),\end{split}

(7.5)

where
\iota\!:V^{(r)}\!\longrightarrow\!X
is the inclusion.
Via (
7.4
), (
7.5
) in particular gives

\displaystyle c_{1}(T\widetilde{X})=\pi^{*}c_{1}(TX)\!-\!(r\!-\!1)\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}\big([\mathbb{E}]_{\widetilde{X}}\big)\hskip 180.67499pt\hbox{if}~r\!\geq\!2;

(7.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}c_{2}(T\widetilde{X})=\pi^{*}c_{2}(TX)\!-\!\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}\bigg(\widetilde{\iota}_{*}\!\Big(\!\!\big(\pi^{*}c_{1}(TX)\!+\!2c_{1}(\gamma)\!\big)\!\cap\![\mathbb{E}]\!\Big)\!+\!\pi^{*}\big(\textnormal{PD}_{X}[V^{(2)}]_{X}\big)&\\
\hskip 115.63243pt-2\mathbb{E}\big|_{\textnormal{PD}_{X}([V^{(1)}]_{X})\cap[V^{(2)}]}&\bigg)\quad\hbox{if}~r\!=\!2.\end{split}

(7.7)

With the dimension of
X
and the codimension of the blowup locus
V^{(r)}
fixed,
the left-hand side of (
7.5
) must be of the form

\begin{split}&\sum_{i=1}^{r-1}\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}\Big(\widetilde{\iota}_{*}\!\Big(\!c_{1}(\gamma)^{i-1}\!\cap\!\big(\mathbb{E}|_{P_{i}(c_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}),\ldots,c_{r}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}))\cap[V^{(r)}]}\big)\!\!\Big)\!\!\Big)\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt+\textnormal{PD}_{\widetilde{X}}\Big(\pi^{*}\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big(\iota_{*}\big(P_{r}\big(c_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}),\ldots,c_{r}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})\!\big)\!\cap\![V^{(r)}]\big)\!\big)\!\Big)\end{split}

for some universal polynomials
P_{1},\ldots,P_{r}\!\in\!\mathbb{Z}[c_{1},\ldots,c_{r}]
.
Since (
1.9
) holds with
\mathbb{Z}
-coefficients in the SC case,
the right-hand side of (
7.5
) reduces to the above form in this case.
For example,

\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V^{(k)}]_{X}\big)\!\cap\![V^{(r)}]=c_{k}\big(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}\big)\!\cap\![V^{(r)}]\in H_{*}\big(V^{(r)};\mathbb{Z})

(7.8)

in the SC case.
This also occurs if the branches of
V
at
V^{(r)}
can be distinguished globally,
i.e.
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}
splits into
r
subbundles which restrict to the subbundles

\mathcal{N}_{V_{y;I-i}}V_{y;I}
with
i\!\in\!I
for every chart

(U_{y},\{V_{y;i}\}_{i\in S_{y}})
as in Definition
4.1
and
every
I\!\subset\!S_{y}
with
|S_{y}|\!=\!r
.

Since (
1.9
) holds with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients in general,
the differences

\eta_{i}^{c}\!\cup\!\mathcal{PD}(V)^{-1}\!-\!P_{i}\big(c_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}),\ldots,c_{r}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})\!\big)\in H_{*}\big(V^{(r)};\mathbb{Z})

are torsion.
If the torsion in
H_{*}(\mathbb{E};\mathbb{Z})
lies in the kernel
\widetilde{\iota}_{*}
, then
the torsion in
H_{*}(V^{(r)};\mathbb{Z})
lies in the kernel
\iota_{*}
.
In such a case,

\begin{split}\widetilde{\iota}_{*}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{\eta_{i}^{c}\cap\mathcal{PD}(V)^{-1}\cap[V^{(r)}]}\Big)&=\widetilde{\iota}_{*}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{P_{i}(c_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}),\ldots,c_{r}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}))\cap[V^{(r)}]}\Big)\in H_{*}(\widetilde{X};\mathbb{Z}),\\
\iota_{*}\big(\mathcal{PD}(V)^{-1}\!\cap\![V^{(r)}]\big)&=\iota_{*}\big(P_{r}\big(c_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)}),\ldots,c_{r}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(r)})\!\big)\!\cap\![V^{(r)}]\big)\in H_{*}(X;\mathbb{Z}).\end{split}

This implies that (
1.9
) holds in
H^{*}(\widetilde{X};\mathbb{Z})
if
the torsion in
H_{*}(V^{(r)};\mathbb{Z})
lies in the kernel
\iota_{*}
.

We now give an example in which (
1.9
) does not hold in
H^{*}(\widetilde{X};\mathbb{Z})
.
Let
\Sigma
be a compact connected Riemann surface and
z^{*}\!\in\!\Sigma
.
Let
\widetilde{S}
be a K3 surface and
\psi\!\in\!\textnormal{Aut}(\widetilde{S})
be a fixed-point-free involution
so that
S\!\equiv\!\widetilde{S}/\psi
is an Enriques surface.
Define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\psi}\!:\widetilde{X}\!\equiv\!\widetilde{S}\!\times\!\Sigma^{2}\longrightarrow\widetilde{S},\quad\widetilde{\psi}(y,z_{1},z_{2})=\big(\psi(y),z_{2},z_{1}\big),

\displaystyle X=\widetilde{X}/\widetilde{\psi},\qquad\widetilde{V}=\widetilde{S}\!\times\!\Sigma\!\times\!\{z^{*}\}\subset\widetilde{X}.

The image
V\!\subset\!X
of
\widetilde{V}
under the quotient map
q\!:\widetilde{X}\!\longrightarrow\!X

is an NC divisor in
X
.
Its 2-fold locus
V^{(2)}\!\approx\!S
is the image of
\widetilde{S}\!\times\!\{z^{*}\}^{2}

under
q
.
Since the 3-fold locus of
V
is empty,
r\!=\!2
in this case.
We show below that

\iota_{*}\big(\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V^{(1)}]_{X}\big)\!\cap\!\big[V^{(2)}\big]\!\big)=0\in H_{2}(X;\mathbb{Z}),\quad c_{1}(\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(2)})\neq 0\in H^{2}(V^{(2)};\mathbb{Z}),

(7.9)

and the torsion of
H_{2}(V^{(2)};\mathbb{Z})
does not vanish under
\iota_{*}
.
This implies that the last term in (
7.7
) is not determined by
the Chern class of
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(2)}
.
Thus, (
7.8
), (
7.7
), (
1.9
), and (
1.5
)
do not hold with
\mathbb{Z}
coefficients in this case.

The image of
\widetilde{S}\!\times\!\{z\}^{2}
under
q

is homologous to
V^{(2)}
for any
z\!\in\!\Sigma
.
Since it is disjoint from
V
for
z\!\neq\!z^{*}
,

\iota_{*}\big(\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V^{(1)}]_{X}\big)\!\cap\!\big[V^{(2)}\big]\!\big)=\textnormal{PD}_{X}\big([V^{(1)}]_{X}\big)\!\cap\!\big[V^{(2)}\big]_{X}=0.

This confirms the first statement in (
7.9
).

The normal bundle
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(2)}
of
V^{(2)}
in
X
is the quotient of the trivial bundle

\widetilde{S}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{2}
by the involution

\widetilde{S}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{2}\longrightarrow\widetilde{S}\!\times\!\mathbb{C}^{2},\qquad(y,c_{1},c_{2})\longrightarrow\big(\psi(y),c_{2},c_{1}\big).

Thus,
\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(2)}\!\approx\!L_{+}\!\oplus\!L_{-}
, where

L_{\pm}=\big\{[y,c,\pm c]\!:y\!\in\!\widetilde{S},\,c\!\in\!\mathbb{C}\big\}\subset\mathcal{N}_{X}V^{(2)}\,.

The complex line bundle
L_{+}
over
S
is isomorphic to
S\!\times\!\mathbb{C}
.
The flat complex line bundle
L_{-}
corresponds to the non-trivial homomorphism

\pi_{1}(S)\!=\!\mathbb{Z}_{2}\longrightarrow S^{1}.

This confirms the second statement in (
7.9
).

Let
f_{+}\!:\Sigma_{+}\!\longrightarrow\!S
be a smooth map from a compact Riemann surface
representing the unique torsion element of
H_{2}(S;\mathbb{Z})
.
Let
f_{-}\!:\Sigma_{-}\!\longrightarrow\!S
be a smooth map from a compact (unorientable) surface
representing a class in
H_{2}(S;\mathbb{Z}_{2})
so that

[f_{+}]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\!\cdot_{S}\![f_{-}]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\neq 0\in\mathbb{Z}_{2},

where
\cdot_{S}
denotes the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-intersection product on
S
.
The involution
\widetilde{\psi}
pulls back to a smooth involution

\widetilde{\psi}_{-}\!:\widetilde{Z}_{-}\!\equiv\!\big\{\!(x,y)\!\in\!\Sigma_{-}\!\times\!\widetilde{S}\!:f_{-}(x)\!=\!q(y)\!\big\}\!\times\!\Sigma^{2}\longrightarrow\widetilde{Z}_{-},\quad\widetilde{\psi}_{-}(x,y,z_{1},z_{2})=\big(x,\psi(y),z_{2},z_{1}\big).

The map

F_{-}\!:Z_{-}\!\equiv\!\widetilde{Z}_{-}/\widetilde{\psi}_{-}\longrightarrow X,\qquad F_{-}\big([x,y,z_{1},z_{2}]\big)=\big[y,z_{1},z_{2}\big],

is smooth and determines an element of
H_{6}(X;\mathbb{Z})
so that

\iota_{*}\big([f_{+}]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\big)\!\cdot_{X}\![F_{-}]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}=[f_{+}]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\!\cdot_{S}\![f_{-}]_{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\neq 0\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}.

This confirms the claim just after (
7.9
).
